H_{\mathcal{R}_{0}}
. Thus, we take

(3.52)

\displaystyle K:=2c,\quad\text{and}\quad m_{0}\coloneqq\inf_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{0}}}\frac{h}{\phi},\quad\text{and}\quad M_{0}\coloneqq\sup_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{0}}}\frac{h}{\phi}

to see that (
3.51
) and (
3.52
) hold for
i=0
. We now assume that (
3.51
) and (
3.52
) hold for
i=0,1,\ldots,j
for some integer
j\geq 0
. Let us consider a function
h_{j}\coloneqq h-m_{j}\phi\geq 0
on
H_{\mathcal{R}_{j}}
to see that

\left\{\begin{aligned} v\partial_{x}h_{j}-\partial_{vv}h_{j}&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $(0,\mathcal{R}_{j}^{3})\times(-\mathcal{R}_{j},\mathcal{R}_{j})$},\\
h_{j}&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x=0\}\times(0,\mathcal{R}_{j})$}.\end{aligned}\right.

By using a rescaled version of
subsection 3.2
and
subsection 3.2
, we derive

(3.53)

\displaystyle\sup_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j}^{+}\theta/4}}\left[h/\phi-m_{j}\right]=\sup_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j}^{+}\theta/4}}h_{j}/\phi\leq c\inf_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j}^{+}\theta/4}}h_{j}/\phi\leq C\inf_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j}\theta/8}}h_{j}/\phi=C\inf_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j}\theta/8}}[h/\phi-m_{j}]

for some constants
c,C\geq 1
. Similarly, by following the same lines as in the proof of (
3.53
) with
h_{j}
replaced by
H_{j}\coloneqq M_{j}\phi-h\geq 0
, we derive

(3.54)

\displaystyle\sup_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j}^{+}\theta/4}}\left[M_{j}-h/\phi\right]=\sup_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j}^{+}\theta/4}}H_{j}/\phi\leq c\inf_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j}^{+}\theta/4}}H_{j}/\phi\leq C\inf_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j}\theta/8}}H_{j}/\phi=C\inf_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j}\theta/8}}[M_{j}-h/\phi].

We now combine (
3.53
) and (
3.54
) to deduce that

\displaystyle M_{j}-m_{j}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j}^{+}\theta/4}}\left[M_{j}-\frac{h}{\phi}\right]+\sup_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j}^{+}\theta/4}}\left[\frac{h}{\phi}-m_{j}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\inf_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j}\theta/8}}\left[M_{j}-\frac{h}{\phi}\right]+\inf_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j}\theta/8}}\left[\frac{h}{\phi}-m_{j}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=C\left(\inf_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j}\theta/8}}\frac{h}{\phi}-\sup_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j}\theta/8}}\frac{h}{\phi}-m_{j}+M_{j}\right).

By the choice of
\mathcal{R}_{j}=(1/R_{0})^{j+1}
and the inductive assumption (
3.51
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j+1}}}\frac{h}{\phi}-\inf_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j+1}}}\frac{h}{\phi}\leq\sup_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j}\theta/8}}\frac{h}{\phi}-\inf_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j}\theta/8}}\frac{h}{\phi}\leq\frac{C-1}{C}(M_{j}-m_{j})\leq\frac{C-1}{C}K\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{1})}\mathcal{R}_{j}^{\alpha}.

We now choose
\alpha\in(0,1)
sufficiently small so that

\displaystyle\sup_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j+1}}}\frac{h}{\phi}-\inf_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j+1}}}\frac{h}{\phi}\leq K\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{1})}\mathcal{R}_{j+1}^{\alpha}.

By taking

\displaystyle m_{j+1}\coloneqq\inf_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j+1}}}\frac{h}{\phi}\quad\text{and}\quad M_{j+1}\coloneqq\sup_{H_{\mathcal{R}_{j+1}}}\frac{h}{\phi},

we get (
3.51
) and (
3.52
) with
i=j+1
. Hence, by induction, (
3.51
) and (
3.52
) hold for any
j\geq 0
, which implies the desired result.
∎

3.3.
Liouville theorem involving
\phi_{0}

In this subsection, we prove a preliminary Liouville theorem in the half-space in 1D with absorbing condition. It allows solutions to grow up to order
3+\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon
.

Lemma 3.11
.

Let
h
be a weak solution to

(3.55)

\left\{\begin{aligned} v\partial_{x}h-\partial_{vv}h&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x>0\}\times\mathbb{R}$},\\
h&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x=0\}\times\{v>0\}$},\\
|h(x,v)|&\leq M\left(1+(|x|^{\frac{1}{3}}+|v|)^{\frac{1}{2}+3-\epsilon}\right)&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x>0\}\times\mathbb{R}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where
M\geq 1
and
\epsilon\in(0,3)
. Then we have
h=c\phi_{0}
for some
c\in\mathbb{R}
.

Remark 3.12
.

Note from
subsection 3.2
that

\displaystyle\frac{h(0)}{\phi_{0}(0)}=\lim_{(x,v)\to 0}\frac{h(x,v)}{\phi_{0}(x,v)}

is well defined. Thus, we obtain that the constant
c
given in
subsection 3.3
coincides with
h(0)/\phi_{0}(0)
.

Note that by using the expansion from
subsection 3.2
, it is possible to derive a Liouville theorem of order
\frac{1}{2}+\alpha
, where
\alpha>0
is as in
subsection 3.2
. We are able to upgrade the order to
\frac{1}{2}+3-\epsilon
by tracking carefully the asymptotic behavior of
h-\frac{h(0)}{\phi_{0}(0)}\phi_{0}
and the
x
-derivative of this function near
\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}
and as
|(x,v)|\to\infty
. Then, we can compare it to the asymptotic behavior of
\phi_{0}
and
\partial_{x}\phi_{0}
and conclude the proof by a contradiction argument based on the maximum principle.

As explained before, our proof is based on boundary regularity estimates of solutions to (
3.55
) away from
\gamma_{0}
. Near
\gamma_{+}
we use the regularity results in
[
35
]
, and near
\gamma_{-}
, we combine suitable interior estimates with the upper bound in
subsection 3.2
.

The first lemma is about the boundary behavior of solutions near
\gamma_{-}
. The exponential factor
e^{-\frac{1}{C}\frac{v_{0}}{R}}
shows that all derivatives vanish to infinite order.

Lemma 3.13
.

Let
h
be a weak solution to

v\partial_{x}h-\partial_{vv}h=0\quad\mbox{in $H_{2R}(0,v_{0})$}

where
v_{0}>0
and
H_{2R}(0,v_{0})\subset\{x>0\}\times\{v>0\}
. Let
k
be a nonnegative integer. Then, there are constants
c=c(k)
and
C\geq 1
such that

(3.56)

\displaystyle\|D^{k}h\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{R}(0,v_{0}))}\leq cR^{-k}e^{-\frac{1}{C}\frac{v_{0}}{R}}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2R}(0,v_{0}))}.

Proof.

By a standard covering argument it suffices to prove

(3.57)

\displaystyle\|D^{k}h\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R/2^{5}}(0,v_{0}))}\leq cR^{-k}e^{-\frac{1}{c}\frac{v_{0}}{R}}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{2R}(0,v_{0}))}

for some constant
c\geq 1
. Let us fix
(x_{1},v_{1})\in Q_{R/2^{5}}(0,v_{0})
. First, we see that

(3.58)

x_{1}<(R/2^{5})^{3}\quad\text{and}\quad v_{1}>v_{0}-R/2^{5}>R.

In addition, as in (
3.8
), we find
r_{1}
such that

(3.59)

x_{1}-(2r_{1})^{2}v_{1}=(2r_{1})^{3}\quad\text{and}\quad\left(\frac{x_{1}}{16v_{1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}<r_{1}<\left(\frac{x_{1}}{4v_{1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{R}{8}.

The value
r_{1}
is the largest radius
r
for which
\mathcal{Q}_{r}(0,x_{1},v_{1})\subset\mathbb{R}\times\{x>0\}\times\mathbb{R}
.
Note that
\widetilde{h}(t,x,v)=h(x,v)
is a weak solution to

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+v\partial_{x}-\partial_{vv})\widetilde{h}=0\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}\times\{x>0\}\times\mathbb{R}.

Thus, by the interior estimates in
subsection 2.2
, and since
\widetilde{h}
is independent of
t
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(-(r_{1}/2)^{2},(r_{1}/2)^{2})}

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B_{(r_{1}/2)^{3}}(x_{1}+tv_{1})}\|D^{k}h(x,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{r_{1}/2}(v_{1}))}=\|D^{k}\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}/2}(0,x_{1},v_{1}))}

\displaystyle\leq cr_{1}^{-k}\|\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}(0,x_{1},v_{1}))}=cr_{1}^{-k}\sup_{t\in(-r_{1}^{2},r_{1}^{2})}\sup_{x\in B_{r_{1}^{3}}(x_{1}+tv_{1})}\|h(x,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{r_{1}}(v_{1}))}.

In light of this and the fact that
2x_{1}>x_{1}+r_{1}^{2}v_{1}+r_{1}^{3}
, which follows from (
3.59
), we get

(3.60)

\displaystyle\|D^{k}h\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{r_{1}/2}(x_{1},v_{1}))}\leq cr_{1}^{-k}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}\left((0,2x_{1})\times B_{r_{1}}(v_{1})\right)}\eqqcolon J

for some constant
c=c(k)
.

Now we employ
subsection 3.2
to further estimate the term
J
. First, fix
(x_{2},v_{2})\in(0,2x_{1})\times B_{r_{1}}(v_{1})
. Then we have

\displaystyle v_{2}>v_{1}-r_{1}>R-(x_{1}/(4v_{1}))^{\frac{1}{2}}>R/2,

where we have used (
3.58
) and (
3.59
).
We now apply
subsection 3.2
with
v_{0}
and
R
replaced by
v_{2}
and
R/2
, respectively, to see that

\displaystyle|h(x,v_{2})|\leq 2e^{-\frac{1}{C_{1}}\frac{v_{2}(R/2)^{2}}{x}}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}\quad\text{for any }x\in\left[0,R^{3}/2^{13}\right]

for some constant
C_{1}\geq 1
, where

\displaystyle Q\coloneqq\left[0,R^{3}/2^{13}\right]\times\left[v_{2}-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{8}\frac{R}{2},v_{2}+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{8}\frac{R}{2}\right].

Since
Q\subset Q_{2R}(0,v_{0})
and
v_{2}<v_{1}<4v_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle|h(x,v_{2})|\leq 2e^{-\frac{1}{4C_{1}}\frac{v_{1}R^{2}}{x}}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{2R}(0,v_{0}))}\quad\text{for any }x\in\left[0,R^{3}/2^{13}\right].

Using (
3.58
), we get
x_{2}\leq 2x_{1}\leq 2\left({R}/{2^{5}}\right)^{3}\leq R^{3}/2^{13}
and

(3.61)

\displaystyle|h(x_{2},v_{2})|\leq 2e^{-\frac{1}{4C_{1}}\frac{v_{1}R^{2}}{x_{2}}}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{2R}(0,v_{0}))}\leq 2e^{-\frac{1}{8C_{1}}\frac{v_{1}R^{2}}{x_{1}}}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{2R}(0,v_{0}))}.

As (
3.61
) holds for any
(x_{1},v_{1})\in(0,2x_{1})\times B_{r_{1}}(v_{1})
, we further estimate
J
given in (
3.60
) as

\displaystyle\|D^{k}h\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{r_{1}/2}(x_{1},v_{1}))}

\displaystyle\leq cR^{-k}e^{-\frac{1}{8C_{1}}\frac{v_{1}R^{2}}{x_{1}}}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{2R}(0,v_{0}))}\leq cR^{-k}e^{-\frac{1}{32C_{1}}\frac{v_{0}R^{2}}{R^{3}}}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{2R}(0,v_{0}))},

for some constant
c=c(k)
, where we have used (
3.59
) and the fact that
x_{1}<R^{3}
, as well as
v_{0}/2<v_{1}
.
Thus, (
3.57
) follows, as the previous estimate holds for any
(x_{1},v_{1})\in H_{R/2^{5}}(0,v_{0})
, as desired.
∎

Next, we prove gradient estimates near
\gamma_{+}
. Note that the factor
(v_{0}/R)^{k/2}
can in general not be improved since it arises from the underlying kinetic scaling, when applied to a stationary equation.

Lemma 3.14
.

Let
h
be a weak solution to

v\partial_{x}h-\partial_{vv}h=0\quad\text{in }H_{2R}(0,v_{0}),

where
v_{0}<0
and
R>0
are such that
H_{2R}(0,v_{0})\subset\{x>0\}\times\{v<0\}
.
Let
k
be a nonnegative integer. Then, there is a constant
c=c(k)
such that

(3.62)

\displaystyle\|D^{k}h\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{R}(0,v_{0}))}\leq cR^{-k}\left(\frac{v_{0}}{R}\right)^{\frac{k}{2}}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2R}(0,v_{0}))},

Proof.

We fix
v_{1}\in(v_{0}-R,v_{0}+R)
to see that
|v_{1}|>R
. Note that there is
r\in(0,R)
such that

(3.63)

\displaystyle-r^{2}v_{1}+r^{3}=R^{3},

as
g(\xi)\coloneq-\xi^{2}v_{1}+\xi^{3}
is increasing when
\xi\geq 0
and

\displaystyle g\left(\frac{R^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2|v_{1}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)<R^{3}<g\left(\frac{R^{\frac{3}{2}}}{|v_{1}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right).

Thus,
r
satisfies

(3.64)

\displaystyle\frac{R^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2|v_{1}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}<r<\frac{R^{\frac{3}{2}}}{|v_{1}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\quad\text{and}\quad-(3r/2)^{2}v_{1}+(3r/2)^{3}<(2R)^{3}.

Next, we observe that by following the lines of the proof of
[
35
, Lemma 4.4]
but using only the expansion from
[
35
, Lemma 4.1]
, we deduce for any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and
l\geq 3
,

(3.65)

\displaystyle[D^{l}h]_{C^{\epsilon}({\mathcal{H}}_{r}(0,0,v_{1}))}\leq c(k,\epsilon)r^{-l+\epsilon}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathcal{H}}_{3r/2}(0,0,v_{1}))}

for some constant
c=c(l,\epsilon)
, where we write

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{r}(0,0,v_{1})\coloneqq\left\{(t,x,v)\,:\,t\in(-r^{2},r^{2}],\,v\in B_{r}(v_{1}),\,x\in B_{r^{3}}(tv_{1})\right\}\cap\{x\geq 0\}.

Here, the only difference compared to the statement of
[
35
, Lemma 4.4]
is that the constant in (
3.65
) is independent of
|v_{0}|
.
Moreover, by
[
35
, Lemma 2.5]
we get

(3.66)

\displaystyle[h]_{C^{\alpha}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{r}(0,0,v_{1}))}\leq cr^{-\alpha}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{3r/2}(0,0,v_{1}))},

for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
.
We now use a rescaled version of the interpolation inequality given in
[
27
, Proposition 2.10]
to get that

\displaystyle[r^{k}D^{k-1}h]_{C^{0,1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{r}(0,0,v_{1}))}\leq c\left([r^{k+2+\alpha}D^{k+2}h]^{\theta}_{C^{\alpha}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{r}(0,0,v_{1}))}[r^{\alpha}h]^{1-\theta}_{C^{\alpha}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{r}(0,0,v_{1}))}+r^{\alpha}[h]_{C^{\alpha}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{r}(0,0,v_{1}))}\right)

for some constant
c=c(k)
, where
\theta\in(0,1)
satisfies

\displaystyle k=\theta(k+2+\alpha)+(1-\theta)\alpha.

Using Young’s inequality, (
3.65
) with
\epsilon=\alpha
and (
3.66
), we deduce

\displaystyle\|r^{k}D^{k}h\|_{L^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{r}(0,0,v_{1}))}=[r^{k}D^{k-1}h]_{C^{0,1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{r}(0,0,v_{1}))}\leq c\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{3r/2}(0,0,v_{1}))}.

From this, we get

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in(0,-r^{2}v_{1}+r^{3})}\|D^{k}h(x,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{r}(v_{1}))}

\displaystyle=\|D^{k}h\|_{L^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{r}(0,0,v_{1}))}

\displaystyle\leq cr^{-k}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{3r/2}(0,0,v_{1}))}

\displaystyle=cr^{-k}\sup_{x\in(0,-(3r/2)^{2}v_{1}+(3r/2)^{3})}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{3r/2}(v_{1}))}

for some constant
c=c(k)
. Using this together with (
3.63
) and (
3.64
), we derive

\displaystyle\|D^{k}h\|_{L^{\infty}((0,R^{3})\times(v_{1}-r,v_{1}+r))}\leq cr^{-k}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}((0,(2R)^{3})\times(v_{1}-2r,v_{1}+2r))}\leq cR^{-k}\left(\frac{v_{1}}{R}\right)^{\frac{k}{2}}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2R}(0,v_{0}))}

for some constant
c=c(k)
. Since this holds for any
v_{1}\in(v_{0}-R,v_{0}+R)
, we derive

\displaystyle\|D^{k}h\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{R}(0,v_{0}))}\leq cR^{-k}\left(\frac{v_{0}}{R}\right)^{\frac{k}{2}}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2R}(0,v_{0}))},

where we have used the fact that
|v_{1}|\leq 2|v_{0}|
. This completes the proof.
∎

We are now ready to prove
subsection 3.3
.

Proof of
subsection 3.3
.

First, we observe that

(3.67)

\displaystyle\kappa_{0}\coloneqq\lim_{(x,v)\to 0}\frac{h(x,v)}{\phi_{0}(x,v)}\quad\text{satisfies}\quad\left|\kappa_{0}\right|\leq c\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{1})}\leq c(M),

where
\kappa_{0}
is well defined by
subsection 3.3
and we have used the growth condition in (
3.55
) for the last inequality.
Next, we define

\displaystyle H\coloneqq\partial_{x}(h-\kappa_{0}\phi_{0})\quad\text{and}\quad\Phi\coloneqq\phi_{0}+\partial_{x}\phi_{0}=\phi_{0}+\frac{1}{36}\phi_{-1},

where the last equality follows from (
C.40
).

Note that the proof is complete, once we establish that
H\equiv 0
. To deduce this, we will first prove that for any
c_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
, there is a large constant
\mathcal{M}_{0}=\mathcal{M}_{0}(c_{0},M,\epsilon)\geq 4
such that

(3.68)

\displaystyle(c_{0}H-\Phi)(x_{0},v_{0})\leq\frac{1}{2}\quad\text{if }(x_{0},v_{0})\notin(A_{1}\cup A_{2}),

where we write

\displaystyle A_{1}\cup A_{2}:=\Big(\left[(\mathcal{M}_{0}/4)^{-3},(\mathcal{M}_{0}/4)^{3}\right]\times[-\mathcal{M}_{0}\times\mathcal{M}_{0}]\Big)\cup\Big(\left[0,(\mathcal{M}_{0}/4)^{-3}\right]\times\left[-\mathcal{M}_{0},-\mathcal{M}_{0}^{-1}\right]\Big).

To this end, first, we fix
c_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
. We now divide the proof of (
3.68
) into several cases depending on the location of the point
(x_{0},v_{0})
.

•

Suppose
4x_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}\leq v_{0}
. Since
h-\kappa_{0}\phi_{0}
solves (
3.55
), by
subsection 3.3
, we derive

\displaystyle|H(x_{0},v_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq{\|\partial_{x}(h-\kappa_{0}\phi_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}({H}_{{R}}(0,v_{0}))}}\leq cR^{-3}e^{-\frac{1}{c}\frac{v_{0}}{R}}\|h-\kappa_{0}\phi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}({Q}_{2R}(0,v_{0}))},

where we set
R=x_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}
. If
x_{0},v_{0}\leq 1
, then we obtain

(3.69)

\displaystyle|H(x_{0},v_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq cR^{-3}e^{-\frac{1}{c}\frac{v_{0}}{R}}\|\phi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,8x_{0}]\times[v_{0}/2,3v_{0}/2])}\left\|\frac{h}{\phi_{0}}-\kappa_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2v_{0}})}

\displaystyle\leq cx_{0}^{-1}x_{0}v_{0}^{-\frac{5}{2}}e^{-\frac{v_{0}^{3}}{9x_{0}}}\left(v_{0}^{\alpha}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2R_{0}})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c(M)v_{0}^{-\frac{5}{2}+\alpha}e^{-\frac{v_{0}^{3}}{9x_{0}}},

where we have used (
C.36
) and (
3.18
), and the constant
R_{0}\geq 1
is determined in
subsection 3.2
. Moreover, we have from (
C.38
)

(3.70)

\displaystyle\Phi(x_{0},v_{0})\geq c^{-1}x_{0}^{-1}v_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{v_{0}^{3}}{9x_{0}}}.

Using this and (
3.69
), we obtain that if
x_{0},v_{0}\leq 1
and
v_{0}\geq 4x_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}
, then

(3.71)

\displaystyle c_{0}H(x_{0},v_{0})-\Phi(x_{0},v_{0})\leq e^{-\frac{v_{0}^{3}}{9x_{0}}}v_{0}^{-\frac{5}{2}+\alpha}\left(c_{0}C-c^{-1}\frac{v_{0}^{3-\alpha}}{x_{0}}\right)\leq e^{-\frac{v_{0}^{3}}{9x_{0}}}v_{0}^{-\frac{5}{2}+\alpha}\left(c_{0}C-c^{-1}x_{0}^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}\right).

We now consider the case when
v_{0}\geq 1
.
Then using the first line given in (
3.69
) together with (
C.36
), (
3.18
), and the third condition in (
3.55
), we get for any nonnegative integer
k
,

(3.72)

\displaystyle|H(x_{0},v_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq cx_{0}^{-1}e^{-\frac{v_{0}x_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}}{c}}x_{0}v_{0}^{-\frac{5}{2}}e^{-\frac{v_{0}^{3}}{9x_{0}}}(v_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2R_{0}v_{0}})})

\displaystyle\leq c(M,\epsilon)e^{-\frac{v_{0}x_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}}{c}}v_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}e^{-\frac{v_{0}^{3}}{9x_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leq c(k,M,\epsilon)\left(v_{0}x_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}\right)^{-k}v_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}e^{-\frac{v_{0}^{3}}{9x_{0}}},

where we have also used the fact that for any
k
,

(3.73)

\displaystyle e^{-\frac{v_{0}x_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}}{c}}\leq c(k)\left(v_{0}x_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}\right)^{-k}.

In case
x_{0}\leq 1\leq v_{0}
, we apply this fact with
k=0
and (
3.70
). This yields

\displaystyle c_{0}H(x_{0},v_{0})-\Phi(x_{0},v_{0})\leq v_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}e^{-\frac{v_{0}^{3}}{9x}}\left(c_{0}C-\frac{v_{0}^{\epsilon}}{cx_{0}}\right)\leq v_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}e^{-\frac{v_{0}^{3}}{9x_{0}}}\left(c_{0}C-\frac{x_{0}^{-1+\frac{\epsilon}{3}}}{c}\right).

By this and (
3.71
), we have

(3.74)

\displaystyle c_{0}H(x_{0},v_{0})-\Phi(x_{0},v_{0})\leq\frac{1}{2}\quad\text{in }x_{0}\in[0,(\mathcal{M}_{0}/4)^{-3}],

whenever
\mathcal{M}_{0}=\mathcal{M}_{0}(M,\epsilon)
is sufficiently large.

If
1\leq x_{0},v_{0}
, then by (
C.36
) and (
C.38
), we have

(3.75)

\displaystyle\Phi(x_{0},v_{0})\eqsim\begin{cases}x_{0}^{-1}v_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{v_{0}^{3}}{9x_{0}}}&\quad\text{if }v_{0}^{3}\geq x_{0}^{2},\\
x_{0}v_{0}^{-\frac{5}{2}}e^{-\frac{v_{0}^{3}}{9x_{0}}}&\quad\text{if }v_{0}^{3}\leq x_{0}^{2}.\end{cases}

Note from (
3.72
) and (
3.73
) with
k=3
that

\displaystyle|H(x_{0},v_{0})|\leq c(M,\epsilon)x_{0}v_{0}^{-\frac{5}{2}-\epsilon}e^{-\frac{v_{0}^{3}}{9x_{0}}}.

Using this and (
3.75
) leads to

\displaystyle(c_{0}H-\Phi)(x_{0},v_{0})\leq x_{0}v_{0}^{-\frac{5}{2}-\epsilon}e^{-\frac{v_{0}^{3}}{9x_{0}}}\left(c_{0}C-\frac{v_{0}^{\epsilon}}{c}\right)\leq\frac{1}{2},

if
v_{0}\geq\mathcal{M}_{0}
, where
\mathcal{M}_{0}=\mathcal{M}_{0}(c_{0},M,\epsilon)\geq 1
is sufficiently large. Using this and (
3.74
), we obtain by taking
\mathcal{M}_{0}
sufficiently large,

(3.76)

\displaystyle c_{0}H(x_{0},v_{0})-\Phi(x_{0},v_{0})\leq\frac{1}{2}\quad\text{in }\mathcal{A}^{-},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{-}\coloneqq\{v\geq 4x^{\frac{1}{3}}\,:\,\,x\in[0,(\mathcal{M}_{0}/4)^{-3}]\}\cup\{v\geq 4x^{\frac{1}{3}}\,:\,\,v\in[\mathcal{M}_{0},\infty)\}.

This concludes the proof of (
3.68
) in case
v_{0}\geq 4x_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}
.

•

Suppose
4x_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}\geq|v_{0}|
. By
subsection 2.2
, we deduce

(3.77)

\displaystyle|H(x_{0},v_{0})|\leq cR^{-3}\|h-\kappa_{0}\phi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{R/4}(0,x_{0},v_{0}))}\leq cR^{-3}\|h-\kappa_{0}\phi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{R}(x_{0},v_{0}))},

where
R=\frac{2x_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}}{3}
. If
x_{0}\leq 1
, then we have

\displaystyle|H(x_{0},v_{0})|\leq cx_{0}^{-1}\|\phi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,8x_{0}]\times[-5x_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}},5x_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}])}\left\|\frac{h}{\phi_{0}}-\kappa_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{5x_{0}^{1/3}})}

\displaystyle\leq cx_{0}^{-\frac{5}{6}}\left(x_{0}^{\frac{\alpha}{3}}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{5R_{0}})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c(M)x_{0}^{-\frac{5}{6}+\frac{\alpha}{3}},

where we have used (
C.36
), (
3.18
) and the third condition given in (
3.55
).
Using (
C.38
) and (
C.36
), we get that if
x_{0}\leq 1
, then

(3.78)

\displaystyle(c_{0}H-\Phi)(x_{0},v_{0})\leq x_{0}^{-\frac{5}{6}+\frac{\alpha}{3}}\left(c_{0}C-\frac{x_{0}^{-\frac{\alpha}{3}}}{c}\right).

If
1\leq x_{0}
, then using (
3.77
), (
C.36
), (
3.18
), and the third condition in (
3.55
), we get

\displaystyle|H(x_{0},v_{0})|\leq cx_{0}^{-1}\|\phi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,8x_{0}]\times[-5x_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}},5x_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}])}\left\|\frac{h}{\phi_{0}}-\kappa_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{5x_{0}^{1/3}})}\leq cx_{0}^{-\frac{5}{6}}x_{0}^{-\frac{1}{6}}x_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{6}-\frac{\epsilon}{3}}\leq cx_{0}^{\frac{1}{6}-\frac{\epsilon}{3}}.

Using (
C.38
) and (
C.36
) we get
\Phi(x_{0},v_{0})\geq\frac{1}{c}{x_{0}^{\frac{1}{6}}}
, which gives

\displaystyle(c_{0}H-\Phi)(x_{0},v_{0})\leq x_{0}^{\frac{1}{6}-\frac{\epsilon}{3}}\left(c_{0}C-\frac{x_{0}^{\frac{\epsilon}{3}}}{c}\right).

With this and (
3.78
), when
\mathcal{M}_{0}
is sufficiently large, then we have

(3.79)

\displaystyle c_{0}H(x_{0},v_{0})-\Phi(x_{0},v_{0})\leq\frac{1}{2}\quad\text{in }\mathcal{A}^{0},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{0}\coloneqq\{|v|\leq 4x^{\frac{1}{3}}\,:\,\,x\in[0,(\mathcal{M}_{0}/4)^{-3}]\}\cup\{|v|\leq 4x^{\frac{1}{3}}\,:\,\,x\in[(\mathcal{M}_{0}/4)^{3},\infty)\}.

This concludes the proof of (
3.68
) in case
|v_{0}|\leq 4x_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}
.

•

Suppose
-v_{0}\geq 4x_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}
. By
subsection 3.3
with
R=|v_{0}|/4
, we have

(3.80)

\displaystyle|H(x_{0},v_{0})|\leq\|H\|_{L^{\infty}({H}_{R}(0,v_{0}))}\leq c|v_{0}|^{-3}\|h-\kappa_{0}\phi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2R}(0,v_{0}))}.

If
|v_{0}|\leq 1
, then we further estimate

\displaystyle|H(x_{0},v_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq c|v_{0}|^{-3}\left\|\frac{h}{\phi_{0}}-\kappa_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2R}(0,v_{0}))}\|\phi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,|v_{0}|^{3}/2]\times[-|v_{0}|/2,-3|v_{0}|/2])}

\displaystyle\leq c|v_{0}|^{-3}\left\|\frac{h}{\phi_{0}}-\kappa_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2|v_{0}|})}|v_{0}|^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq c(M)|v_{0}|^{-\frac{5}{2}+\alpha},

where we have used (
C.36
) and (
3.18
). Using (
C.38
), we have

(3.81)

\displaystyle c_{0}H(x_{0},v_{0})-\Phi(x_{0},v_{0})\leq|v_{0}|^{-\frac{5}{2}+\alpha}\left(c_{0}C-\frac{|v_{0}|^{-\alpha}}{c}\right).

On the other hand, when
|v_{0}|\geq 1
, we get

\displaystyle|H(x_{0},v_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq c|v_{0}|^{-3}\|\phi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}([|v_{0}|^{3}/2]\times[3v_{0}/2,v_{0}/2])}\left\|\frac{h}{\phi_{0}}-\kappa_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{4|v_{0}|})}

\displaystyle\leq c|v_{0}|^{-\frac{5}{2}}|v_{0}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{4R_{0}|v_{0}|})}

\displaystyle\leq c|v_{0}|^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon},

where we have used (
C.36
) and (
3.18
).
Using this, (
C.36
) and (
C.38
), we derive

\displaystyle c_{0}H(x_{0},v_{0})-\Phi(x_{0},v_{0})\leq|v_{0}|^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}\left(c_{0}C-\frac{|v_{0}|^{\epsilon}}{c}\right).

This and (
3.81
) imply

(3.82)

\displaystyle c_{0}H(x_{0},v_{0})-\Phi(x_{0},v_{0})\leq\frac{1}{2}\quad\text{in }\mathcal{A}^{+},

if
\mathcal{M}_{0}
is sufficiently large, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{+}\coloneqq\{-v\geq 4x^{\frac{1}{3}}\,:\,\,v\in[0,\mathcal{M}_{0}^{-1}]\}\cup\{-v\geq 4x^{\frac{1}{3}}\,:\,\,v\in[\mathcal{M}_{0},\infty)\}.

This concludes the proof of (
3.68
) in case
-v_{0}\geq 4x_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}
.

Therefore, combining (
3.76
), (
3.79
), and (
3.82
) leads to (
3.68
).

Now using (
3.68
), we prove
H\equiv 0
. Suppose
H
is nonzero, then there is a point
H(x_{0},v_{0})\neq 0
with
(x_{0},v_{0})\in\{x>0\}\times\mathbb{R}
. Thus there is a constant
c_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
(c_{0}H-\Phi)(x_{0},v_{0})\geq 1
. By (
3.68
), there must be a strict maximum point
(x_{1},v_{1})\in A_{1}\cup A_{2}
such that
(c_{0}H-\Phi)(x_{1},v_{1})\geq 1
and
(c_{0}H-\Phi)(x_{1},v_{1})\geq(c_{0}H-\Phi)(x,v)
for any
(x,v)\in\{x>0\}\times\mathbb{R}
.

We prove that this leads to a contradiction. Indeed, consider the function

\displaystyle F\coloneqq(c_{0}H-\Phi)(x_{1},v_{1})-(c_{0}H-\Phi)(x,v)\geq 0,

which is a weak solution to

\displaystyle v\partial_{x}F-\partial_{vv}F=0\quad\text{in }\mathcal{D},

where we write

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}\coloneqq\Big(\left[(\mathcal{M}_{0}/2)^{-3},(\mathcal{M}_{0}/2)^{3}\right]\times[-2\mathcal{M}_{0}\times 2\mathcal{M}_{0}]\Big)\cup\Big(\left[0,(\mathcal{M}_{0}/2)^{-3}\right]\times\left[-2\mathcal{M}_{0},-(2\mathcal{M}_{0})^{-1}\right]\Big).

•

Let
v_{1}\geq 0
. Indeed, by (
3.5
) in
subsection 3.1
, which remains valid (with the same proof, using only interior Harnack) if we replace
x_{0}
,
v_{0}
, and
{H}_{\mathcal{R}}
by
(\mathcal{M}_{0}/4)^{-3}
,
v_{1}
, and
\left[(\mathcal{M}_{0}/2)^{-3},(\mathcal{M}_{0}/2)^{3}\right]\times[-2\mathcal{M}_{0}\times 2\mathcal{M}_{0}]
, we deduce

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\leq(c_{0}H-\Phi)(x_{1},v_{1})-(c_{0}H-\Phi)\left((\mathcal{M}_{0}/4)^{-3},v_{1}\right)=F\left((\mathcal{M}_{0}/4)^{-3},v_{1}\right)\leq cF(x_{1},v_{1})=0,

where we have also used (
3.68
). This is a contradiction.

•

Let
v_{1}<0
. Similarly, by modifying the proof of (
3.6
) of
subsection 3.1
with
x_{0}
,
x_{1}
,
v_{0}
, and
H_{\mathcal{R}}
replaced by
x_{1}
,
{(\mathcal{M}_{0}/4)^{3}}
,
v_{1}
, and
\left[0,(\mathcal{M}_{0}/2)^{-3}\right]\times\left[-2\mathcal{M}_{0},-(2\mathcal{M}_{0})^{-1}\right]
, we deduce the following contradiction

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\leq(c_{0}H-\Phi)(x_{1},v_{1})-(c_{0}H-\Phi)\left((\mathcal{M}_{0}/4)^{3},v_{1}\right)=F\left((\mathcal{M}_{0}/4)^{3},v_{1}\right)\leq cF(x_{1},v_{1})=0.

Therefore, it must be
H\equiv 0
, which gives
\partial_{vv}(h-\kappa_{0}\phi_{0})=0
by
vH-\partial_{vv}(h-\kappa_{0}\phi_{0})=0
. This yields
h=\kappa_{0}\phi_{0}+c_{1}+c_{2}v
for some constants
c_{1},c_{2}
.
Lastly, by the boundary condition given in (
3.55
), we get
c_{1}=c_{2}=0
. Hence,
h=\kappa_{0}\phi_{0}
, as desired. This completes the proof.
∎

3.4.
Liouville theorem with in-flow condition

As a last step, it remains to derive a 1D Liouville theorem for solutions to inhomogeneous equations from
subsection 3.3
, where all solutions to homogeneous equations were classified.
In this subsection we consider solutions satisfying the in-flow condition with a polynomial source term.

Theorem 3.15
.

Let
l\in\{-1,0\}
and let
p\in\mathcal{P}_{3l+1}
. Assume that
h
is a weak solution to

(3.83)

\left\{\begin{aligned} v\partial_{x}h-\partial_{vv}h&=p&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x>0\}\times\mathbb{R}$},\\
h&=\sum_{i=0}^{3(l+1)}b_{i}v^{i}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x=0\}\times\{v>0\}$},\\
|h(x,v)|&\leq c\left(1+(|x|^{\frac{1}{3}}+|v|)^{\frac{1}{2}+3(l+1)-\epsilon}\right)&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x>0\}\times\mathbb{R}$}\end{aligned}\right.

for some
c\geq 1
and
\epsilon\in(0,1/2)
. Then there exist
P\in\mathcal{P}_{3(l+1)}
,
\mu_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\kappa_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
, such that

(3.84)

h=P+\sum_{j=0}^{l}\mu_{j}\psi_{j}+\sum_{i=0}^{l}\kappa_{i}\phi_{i},

where the functions
\phi_{i}
and
\psi_{j}
are defined in (
C.11
) and (
C.50
), respectively.

In order to prove
Theorem 3.15
, we have to find weak solutions satisfying the in-flow boundary condition with a right-hand side being a homogeneous polynomial.

Lemma 3.16
.

Let
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
,
p\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2}
be a homogeneous polynomial of degree
\lambda-2
and
a\in\mathbb{R}
be such that either
p\neq 0
or
a\neq 0
. Then there is a weak solution
h
to

\displaystyle\begin{cases}v\partial_{x}h-\partial_{vv}h&=p\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ in }(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R},\\
h(x,v)&=av^{\lambda}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ on }\{0\}\times\{v>0\}.\end{cases}

Indeed, there exist
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}
and
\mu=\mu(\lambda,a,p)\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle h=P+\mu\psi_{\left[\frac{\lambda}{3}\right]}.

Moreover, if
\lambda=3l
or
3l+1
for some nonnegative integer
l
, then
\mu=0
.

The proof of this lemma will be provided in Section
C
.

With
subsection 3.4
and
subsection 3.3
, we are able to prove
Theorem 3.15
.

Proof of
Theorem 3.15
.

First, we consider the case
l=0
. We observe

\left\{\begin{aligned} v\partial_{x}(h-G)-\partial_{vv}(h-G)&=p-\partial_{vv}G\eqqcolon q&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x>0\}\times\mathbb{R}$},\\
(h-G)&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x=0\}\times\{v>0\}$},\\
|(h-G)(x,v)|&\leq M\left(1+(|x|^{\frac{1}{3}}+|v|)^{\frac{1}{2}+3-\epsilon}\right)&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x>0\}\times\mathbb{R}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where we write

(3.85)

\displaystyle G\coloneqq\sum_{i=0}^{3}b_{i}v^{i}\quad\text{and}\quad q=\sum_{i=2}^{3}c_{i}q_{i},

where
c_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
and the
q_{i}
are homogeneous polynomials of degree
i-2
.
Now by using
subsection 3.4
, there is a weak solution
h_{i}
to

\left\{\begin{aligned} v\partial_{x}h_{i}-\partial_{vv}h_{i}&=c_{i}q_{i}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x>0\}\times\mathbb{R}$},\\
h_{i}&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x=0\}\times\{v>0\}$}\end{aligned}\right.

with
h_{i}
being of the form

\displaystyle h_{i}=p_{i}+\nu_{i}\psi_{[i/3]},

where
p_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{i}
and
\nu_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
with
\nu_{i}=0
when
i=3l
or
i=3l+1
for some nonnegative integer
l
. Therefore, we get that

(3.86)

\displaystyle H\coloneqq h-G-\sum_{i=0}^{3}(p_{i}+\nu_{i}\psi_{[i/3]})

is a weak solution to

(3.87)

\left\{\begin{aligned} v\partial_{x}H-\partial_{vv}H&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x>0\}\times\mathbb{R}$},\\
H&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x=0\}\times\{v>0\}$},\\
|H(x,v)|&\leq M\left(1+(|x|^{\frac{1}{3}}+|v|)^{\frac{1}{2}+3-\epsilon}\right)&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x>0\}\times\mathbb{R}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where we have used the fact that

\displaystyle|p_{i}(x,v)|+|\nu_{i}\psi_{[i/3]}(x,v)|\leq c\left(1+(|x|^{\frac{1}{3}}+|v|)^{\frac{1}{2}+3-\epsilon}\right)

for some constant
c
, as
i\leq 3<\frac{1}{2}+3-\epsilon
. Therefore, we can apply
subsection 3.3
to (
3.87
) and see that

\displaystyle H=\kappa_{0}\phi_{0}

for some
\kappa_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
. By this, (
3.85
), and (
3.86
), we have

\displaystyle h=\sum_{i=0}^{3}b_{i}v_{i}+\sum_{i=0}^{3}(p_{i}+\nu_{i}\psi_{[i/3]})+\kappa_{0}\phi_{i}=P+\mu_{0}\psi_{0}+\kappa_{0}\phi_{0}

for some
P\in\mathcal{P}_{3}
,
\mu_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
.
This gives the desired result. In case
l=-1
, we observe that
h
also satisfies the third condition given in (
3.83
) with
l=0
so that
h
is of the form (
3.84
). By the growth condition, we get
h=c
for some constant
c
. This completes the proof.
∎

3.5.
Liouville theorem with unbounded source terms

In this subsection, we classify solutions in the half-space whose equation involves unbounded source terms, such as

\displaystyle v\partial_{x}h-\partial_{vv}h=\partial_{vv}\phi_{0}.

We will actually consider more general source terms of the form (
3.90
).
Such a classification result is needed in order to prove expansions for kinetic equations with
non-constant coefficients
via a blow up argument (see
section 5
).

Solutions to such equations are built from functions of the following form

(3.88)

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{i}=v^{i}\partial_{v}^{(i)}\phi_{0},\,{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{j+3}=v^{j}\partial_{v}^{(j)}\psi_{0},\,{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{5}=\partial_{v}\phi_{1}

for
i\in\{0,1,2\}
and
j\in\{0,1\}
. Given
\lambda\in\{0,1,2,3\}
and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
with
\lambda+\epsilon<3+\frac{1}{2}
, we define the following space, which contains all solutions to equations involving unbounded source terms of the form (
3.90
) with suitable growth at infinity:

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon}\coloneqq\mathrm{span}\left\{p,p_{i}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{i}},p_{j+3}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{j+3}},p_{5}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{5}\,\Bigg|\begin{array}[]{l}p\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}\,,p_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}]},\,p_{j+3}\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2},\,p_{5}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{5}{2}]}\\
p_{1}(0)=p_{2}(0)=Dp_{2}(0)=p_{4}(0)=0\end{array}\right\},

where
i\in\{0,1,2\}
and
j\in\{0,1\}
.

When
\lambda+\epsilon<\frac{3}{2}
, then the space
\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon}
simplifies significantly, since any
P\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon}
is of the form
P=p+c\phi_{0}
for some
p\in\mathcal{P}_{1}
and
c\in\mathbb{R}
.

Next, we define the subspace
{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon}
of the vector space

\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon}
by

(3.89)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon}\coloneqq\left\{P\in\overline{T}_{\lambda,\epsilon}\,\Bigg|\,P=0\text{ on }\{x=0\}\times\{v>0\}\right\}.

Note that
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{2}
have homogeneity
\frac{1}{2}
, while
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{3},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{4}
have homogeneity
2
, and
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{5}
has homogeneity
\frac{5}{2}
. Hence, the polynomials
p_{k}
are chosen precisely such that the products
p_{k}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{k}
do not grow faster than
|z|^{\lambda+\epsilon}
at infinity for any
k\in\{0,\dots,5\}
. Moreover, the assumptions
p_{1}(0)=p_{2}(0)=Dp_{2}(0)=p_{4}(0)=0
(together with (
3.91
)) guarantee that the space
\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon}
remains ”invariant under the blow-up proof of
section 5
“ in the sense that applying rescaled elements from
\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon}
to an equation with suitably regular coefficients leads to a limiting equation that is again solved by an element from
\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon}
.

Now we provide the Liouville theorem with possibly unbounded source terms.

Theorem 3.17
.

Let
\lambda\in\{0,1,2,3\}
,
\epsilon\in(0,1)
with
\lambda+\epsilon<3+\frac{1}{2}
, and
p\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2}
. Let
h
be a weak solution to

\left\{\begin{aligned} v\partial_{x}h-\partial_{vv}h&=p+Q&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x>0\}\times\mathbb{R}$},\\
h&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x=0\}\times\{v>0\}$},\\
|h(x,v)|&\leq c\left(1+(|x|^{\frac{1}{3}}+|v|)^{\lambda+\epsilon}\right)&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x>0\}\times\mathbb{R}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where

(3.90)

\displaystyle{Q}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{i=0}^{2}{q}_{0,i}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{i}+\sum_{k=0}^{4}{q}_{1,k}\partial_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k}+\sum_{k=0,1,3}{q}_{2,k}\partial_{vv}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k},

and for
i\in\{0,1,2\}
and
j\in\{0,1\}
,

(3.91)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle q}_{0,i}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{5}{2}]},\,{q}_{1,i}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{3}{2}]},\,{q}_{1,j+3}\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-3},\,{q}_{2,i}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}]},\,{q}_{2,3}\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2},

\displaystyle{\displaystyle q}_{1,2}(0)={{q}}_{2,0}(0)={{q}}_{2,1}(0)={{q}}_{2,3}(0)=D{q}_{2,1}(0)=0.

Then,
h\in\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\epsilon}
.

Proof.

We claim that for any
Q
as in (
3.90
), (
3.91
), we can find
H\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon}
such that

(3.92)

\left\{\begin{aligned} v\partial_{x}H-\partial_{vv}H&=Q&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x>0\}\times\mathbb{R}$},\\
H&=b_{0}v^{2}+b_{1}v^{3}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x=0\}\times\{v>0\}$},\\
|H(x,v)|&\leq c\left(1+(|x|^{\frac{1}{3}}+|v|)^{\lambda+\epsilon}\right)&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x>0\}\times\mathbb{R}$}\end{aligned}\right\}

for some constant
b_{0},b_{1},c\in\mathbb{R}
. Note that once this claim is proved, we can deduce the desired result by applying
Theorem 3.15
to
h-H
, which yields

\displaystyle h-H=P+c_{0}\phi_{0}+c_{1}\psi_{0}

for some constants
c_{0},c_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
and
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}
. Since
P,\phi_{0},\psi_{0}\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon}
and
h=H+P+c_{0}\phi_{0}+c_{1}\psi_{0}\equiv 0
on
\{x=0\}\times\{v>0\}
, we get
h\in\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\epsilon}
, and conclude the proof.

Hence, it remains to prove the claim, i.e. to find
H\in\overline{\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\epsilon}}
such that (
3.92
) holds true. To prove it, we note that by elementary computations using the fact that (
C.40
) and (
C.25
), it holds

(3.93)

\displaystyle(v\partial_{x}-\partial_{vv})(v{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0})=-2\partial_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0},\,(v\partial_{x}-\partial_{vv})(v^{2}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0}})=-2{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0}-4{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1},\,(v\partial_{x}-\partial_{vv})(v^{2}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1})={\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{2},

\displaystyle(v\partial_{x}-\partial_{vv})(v{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1}})=3\partial_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0}-5\partial_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1},\,(v\partial_{x}-\partial_{vv})(v^{2}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1})=-6{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1}-7{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{2},

\displaystyle(v\partial_{x}-\partial_{vv})(v^{2}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{2})=-10{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{2}-10v\partial_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{2},\,(v\partial_{x}-\partial_{vv}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{5}=c_{0}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0},

\displaystyle v\partial_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0}={\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1},\,v\partial_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1}={\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1}+{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{2},\,v\partial^{2}_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0}=\partial_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1}-\partial_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0},\,v^{2}\partial_{v}^{2}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0}={\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{2},\,v^{2}\partial_{v}^{2}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1}=-{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{2}+v\partial_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{2}.

Similarly, the same observations given in (
3.93
) hold with
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0}
and
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1}
replaced by
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{3}
and
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{4}
, respectively, by (
C.61
) and (
C.62
). By using these formulas (
3.93
), we can easily construct
H\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon}
such that (
3.92
) holds true, given any
Q
as in (
3.90
), (
3.91
). The proof is complete.
∎

4.
Liouville theorem in the half-space for in-flow

In this section, we prove the higher dimensional Liouville theorem in the half-space for in-flow.

4.1.
Liouville theorem with polynomials

Let us recall the space
\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}
containing all polynomials of degree less or equal than
\lambda
. Next for any
a>0
,
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, and
\lambda\in\{0,1,2,3\}
, we define vector spaces

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\epsilon,a}\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}p_{0}+p_{0,1}\phi_{0,a}+p_{0,2}\partial_{v_{n}}\phi_{1,a}+q_{0,1}\psi_{0,a}\mbox{ with}\\
p_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda},\,p_{0,1}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}]},\,q_{0,1}\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2},\,p_{0,2}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{5}{2}]}\end{array}\right\},

where the functions
\phi_{m,a}(x_{n},v_{n})\coloneqq\phi_{m}(x_{n}/a,v_{n}/a)
for
m\in\{0,1\}
and
\psi_{0,a}\coloneqq\psi_{0}(x_{n}/a,v_{n}/a)
solve the equation
(v_{n}\partial_{x_{n},x_{n}}-a^{2}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}})f=0
.

We are now ready to prove a general Liouville theorem in the half-space with in-flow boundary condition when the data are given by polynomials.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
\lambda\in\{0,1,2,3\}
,
\epsilon\in(0,1)
with
\lambda+\epsilon<3+\frac{1}{2}
,
p\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2}
, and
q\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}
. Let
f
be a weak solution to

(4.1)

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f-a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}}f&=p&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathbb{R}\times\{x_{n}>0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}$},\\
f&=q&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathbb{R}\times\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where
a^{i,j}
is a constant matrix satisfying an uniformly ellipticity condition. Suppose that there is a constant
c\geq 1
such that for any
R>0
,

(4.2)

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}\leq c(1+R)^{\lambda+\epsilon}.

Then,
f=P
for some
P\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,a}
with
a\coloneqq\sqrt{a^{n,n}}
.

Proof.

We may assume
a^{n,n}=1
. Next since
f-q
solves

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})(f-q)-a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}}(f-q)&=p+\widetilde{p}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathbb{R}\times\{x_{n}>0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}$},\\
f-q&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathbb{R}\times\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\}$}\end{aligned}\right.

for some
\widetilde{p}\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2}
and satisfies (
4.2
) with
f
replaced by
f-q
, we may assume
q=0
.

Now let us fix
h>0
and
y=hy_{i}
with
i\neq n
, where
y_{i}
denotes the
i
-th unit vector in the
x
-coordinates. Let us consider a function

\displaystyle\delta_{h,y_{i}}f(t,x,v)\coloneqq f(t,x+y,v)-f(t,x,v)

to see that
\delta_{h,y_{i}}f
is a solution to

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})\delta_{h,y_{i}}f-a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}}\delta_{h,y_{i}}f&=\delta_{h,y_{i}}p&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathbb{R}\times\{x_{n}>0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}$},\\
\delta_{h,y_{i}}f&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathbb{R}\times\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\}$}.\end{aligned}\right.

By
C^{\alpha}
-estimates for in-flow given in
[
35
, Lemma 2.25]
, we have

\displaystyle\|\delta_{h,y_{i}}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}\leq c|h|^{\alpha}\left[[f]_{C^{{\alpha}}(\mathcal{H}_{2R})}\right]

\displaystyle\leq c|h|^{\alpha}R^{-\alpha}\left(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{4R})}+\|R^{2}\delta_{h,y_{i}}p\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{4R})}\right)\leq c|h|^{\alpha}R^{\lambda+\epsilon-\alpha},

where we have chosen
R^{3}>2|h|
and used the fact that
p\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2}
. By iterating this procedure
j
-times, we deduce

\displaystyle\|\delta^{j}_{h,y_{i}}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}\leq c|h|^{j\alpha}R^{\lambda+\epsilon-j\alpha},

where we write
\delta^{j}_{h,y_{i}}f=\delta_{h,y_{i}}\circ\delta_{h,y_{i}}^{j-1}f
. Taking
j
sufficiently large and
R\to\infty
, we deduce that
f
is a polynomial with respect to the
x_{i}
variables for any
i=1,\ldots,n-1
. Similarly, applying the same argument to
\delta_{h,\tau}f(t,x,v)\coloneqq f(t+\tau,x,v)-f(t,x,v)
yields that
f
is also a polynomial with respect to the
t
-variable, and this gives

\displaystyle f=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta=(\beta_{t},\beta_{x^{\prime}},0)\in\mathcal{I}\\
|\beta|\leq\lambda\end{subarray}}f_{\beta}(x_{n},v)t^{\beta_{t}}\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}x_{j}^{\beta_{x_{j}}}\coloneqq\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal{I}}g_{\beta}(t,x,v),

where we denote by
\mathcal{I}=(\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\})^{1+2n}
with
|\beta|=2|\beta_{t}|+3|\beta_{x}|+|\beta_{v}|
being the kinetic degree of a multi-index
\beta\in\mathcal{I}
, and we used (
4.2
) to ensure
|\beta|\leq\lambda
.

Step 1.
We aim to prove that for each
\beta\in\mathcal{I}
,

(4.3)

g_{\beta}(t,x,v)\coloneqq t^{\beta_{t}}\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}x_{j}^{\beta_{x_{j}}}\sum_{\gamma_{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{\beta}}h_{\gamma_{\beta}}(x_{n},v_{n})v_{1}^{\gamma_{\beta_{1}}}\cdots v_{n-1}^{\gamma_{\beta_{n-1}}}.

Let
M_{0}
be the largest value
|\beta|
in
\mathcal{I}
so that
\delta_{h,\tau}^{\beta_{t}}\delta^{\beta_{1}}_{h,y_{1}}\cdots\delta^{\beta_{n-1}}_{h,y_{n-1}}f=c_{\beta}|h|^{\beta}f_{\beta}(x_{n},v)
for some constant
c_{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}
, and

(4.4)

\left\{\begin{aligned} v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}f_{\beta}(x_{n},v)-a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}}f_{\beta}(x_{n},v)&=\widetilde{p}_{\beta}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x_{n}>0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}$},\\
f_{\beta}(x_{n},v)&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\}$}\end{aligned}\right.

for some polynomial
\widetilde{p}_{\beta}\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2-\beta}
. This follows by applying the operator
\delta_{h,\tau}^{\beta_{t}}\delta^{\beta_{1}}_{h,y_{1}}\cdots\delta^{\beta_{n-1}}_{h,y_{n-1}}
to (
4.1
). Since we can take
\delta^{j}_{h,w_{i}}
for any
i=1,2,\ldots,n-1
in (
4.4
), we derive

\left\{\begin{aligned} v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}\delta^{j}_{h,w_{i}}f_{\beta}(x_{n},v)-a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}}\delta^{j}_{h,w_{i}}f_{\beta}(x_{n},v)&=\delta^{j}_{h,w_{i}}\widetilde{p}_{\beta}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x_{n}>0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}$},\\
\delta^{j}_{h,w_{i}}f_{\beta}(x_{n},v)&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where we write

\displaystyle\delta_{h,w_{i}}f(t,x,v)\coloneqq f(t,x,v+hw_{i})-f(t,x,v)\quad\text{and}\quad\delta_{h,w_{i}}^{j}f=\delta_{h,w_{i}}^{j-1}\circ\delta_{h,w_{i}}f.

Since
\delta^{j}_{h,w_{i}}f_{\beta}
is also a weak solution to (
4.1
) with
p=\delta^{j}_{h,w_{i}}\widetilde{p}_{\beta}
and
q=0
, by applying the
C^{\alpha}
- estimates for in-flow, we get that
f_{\beta}
is a polynomial with respect to the
v_{i}
variables. This gives (
4.3
). Inductively, we assume for any
\beta\in\mathcal{I}
such that
|\beta|\geq M_{0}-j
that
g_{\beta}
is of the form given by (
4.3
). Let us choose
\beta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}
such that
|\beta^{\prime}|=M_{0}-(j+1)
. Then we get

\displaystyle\frac{\delta_{h,\tau}^{\beta^{\prime}_{t}}\delta^{\beta^{\prime}_{1}}_{h,y_{1}}\cdots\delta^{\beta^{\prime}_{n-1}}_{h,y_{n-1}}f}{|h|^{\beta^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=c_{\beta^{\prime}}f_{\beta^{\prime}}(x_{n},v)

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\mathcal{I}\\
|\beta|>|\beta^{\prime}|\end{subarray}}c_{\beta}t^{\beta_{t}-\beta^{\prime}_{t}}x_{1}^{\beta_{x_{1}}-\beta^{\prime}_{x_{1}}}\cdots x_{n-1}^{\beta_{x_{n-1}}-\beta^{\prime}_{x_{n-1}}}\sum_{\gamma_{\beta}\in\mathcal{I}_{\beta}}c_{\gamma\beta}h_{\gamma_{\beta}}(x_{n},v_{n})v_{1}^{\gamma_{\beta_{1}}}\cdots v_{n-1}^{\gamma_{\beta_{n-1}}}}_{\eqqcolon\overline{f}(t,x,v)}

and

(4.5)

\displaystyle v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}f_{\beta^{\prime}}-a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}}f_{\beta^{\prime}}=\widetilde{p}_{\beta^{\prime}}-{\underbrace{(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}-a^{i,j}\partial_{i,j})\overline{f}}_{\eqqcolon F}}\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}\times\{x_{n}>0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{n},

where
\widetilde{p}_{\beta^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2-\beta^{\prime}}
. Note that
F
is a polynomial with respect to the
v_{i}
variables for each
i=1,\ldots,n-1
by the induction assumption. Thus, applying
\delta_{h,w_{i}}^{j}
to (
4.5
) for sufficiently large
j
, we get

\displaystyle v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}\delta_{h,w_{i}}^{j}f_{\beta^{\prime}}-a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}}\delta_{h,w_{i}}^{j}f_{\beta^{\prime}}=\delta_{h,w_{i}}^{j}\widetilde{p}_{\beta^{\prime}}\quad\text{in }\{x_{n}>0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Similarly, we deduce that
f_{\beta^{\prime}}(x_{n},v)
is polynomial with respect to the
v_{i}
variables for any
i=1,\ldots,n-1
. This implies that
g_{\beta^{\prime}}
is also of the form as in (
4.3
), and by induction, we get (
4.3
) for any
\beta\in\mathcal{I}
.

Hence, we may write

\displaystyle f(t,x,v)\coloneq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta=(\beta_{t},\beta_{x^{\prime}},\beta_{v^{\prime}})\in\mathcal{I},\\
|\beta|\leq\lambda\end{subarray}}t^{\beta_{t}}\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}x_{j}^{\beta_{x_{j}}}\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}v_{j}^{\beta_{v_{j}}}h_{\beta}(x_{n},v_{n})=\sum_{\beta}F_{\beta}(t,x,v).

Step 2.

Next, we want to prove that for each
\beta\in\mathcal{I}
,
h_{\beta}
is of the form

(4.6)

\displaystyle h_{\beta}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{p}_{\beta}+\sum_{i=0}^{2}a_{\beta,i}v_{n}^{i}\phi_{m}+b_{\beta}\partial_{v_{n}}\phi_{1}+\sum_{i=0}^{1}c_{\beta,i}v_{n}^{i}\psi_{0},

where
\widetilde{p}_{\beta}(x_{n},v_{n})\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}
,
a_{\beta,m,i},b_{\beta,m,i},c_{\beta,l,j},d_{\beta,l,j}\in\mathbb{R}
, and by the growth condition given in (
4.2
),

(4.7)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}a_{\beta,i}=0\quad&\text{if }\frac{1}{2}+i>\lambda+\epsilon,\\
b_{\beta}=0\quad&\text{if }\frac{5}{2}+j>\lambda+\epsilon,\\
c_{\beta,i}=0\quad&\text{if }2+i>\lambda.\end{cases}

Let
M_{1}
be the largest value of
|\beta|
, where
\beta\in\mathcal{I}
. Then we get

\partial_{t}^{\beta_{t}}\partial_{x_{1}}^{\beta_{x_{1}}}\cdots\partial^{\beta_{x_{n-1}}}_{x_{n-1}}\partial_{v_{1}}^{{\beta_{v_{1}}}}\cdots\partial_{v_{n-1}}^{{\beta_{v_{n-1}}}}f=c_{\beta}h_{\beta}
,

(4.8)

\left\{\begin{aligned} v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}h_{\beta}-a^{n,n}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}h_{\beta}&=\widetilde{p}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x_{n}>0\}\times\mathbb{R}$},\\
f_{\beta}&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\}$}\end{aligned}\right.

for some polynomial
\widetilde{p}=\widetilde{p}(x_{n},v_{n})\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}
. This follows by applying
\delta_{h,t}^{\beta_{t}}\delta_{h,x_{1}}^{\beta_{x_{1}}}\cdots\delta^{\beta_{x_{n-1}}}_{h,x_{n-1}}\delta_{h,v_{1}}^{{\beta_{v_{1}}}}\cdots\delta_{h,v_{n-1}}^{{\beta_{v_{n-1}}}}
to the equation and then taking
h\to 0
. By
Theorem 3.15
, we obtain

(4.9)

\displaystyle h_{\beta}=\widetilde{p}_{\beta}+a_{\beta,0}\phi_{0}+c_{\beta,0}\psi_{0}

for some constants
a_{\beta,j},c_{\beta,i}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\widetilde{p}_{\beta}(x_{n},v_{n})\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2}
. This proves (
4.6
) with
|\beta|=M_{1}
. Now we assume that (
4.6
) with
|\beta|=M_{1}-k
holds for some nonnegative integer
k
, and then we want to show (
4.6
) with
|\beta|=M_{1}-(k+1)
. To do so, we observe

\displaystyle p

\displaystyle=\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal{I}}(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}-a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}})F_{\beta}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal{I}}(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}-a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}})\left(t^{\beta_{t}}\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}x_{j}^{\beta_{x_{j}}}\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}v_{j}^{\beta_{v_{j}}}h_{\beta}(x_{n},v_{n})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal{I}}h_{\beta}(x_{n},v_{n})\left(\partial_{t}+v^{\prime}\cdot\nabla_{x^{\prime}}-\sum_{i,j\neq n}a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}}\right)\left(t^{\beta_{t}}\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}x_{j}^{\beta_{x_{j}}}\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}v_{j}^{\beta_{v_{j}}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal{I}}\left(t^{\beta_{t}}\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}x_{j}^{\beta_{x_{j}}}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=n\text{ or }j=n\\
(i,j)\neq(n,n)\end{subarray}}a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}v_{j}^{\beta_{v_{j}}}h_{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal{I}}\left(t^{\beta_{t}}\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}x_{j}^{\beta_{x_{j}}}\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}v_{j}^{\beta_{v_{j}}}\right)(v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}-a^{n,n}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}})h_{\beta}.

Let us now fix
\beta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}
with
|\beta^{\prime}|=M_{1}-(k+1)
. As before, by applying the differential operator
\partial_{t}^{\beta^{\prime}_{t}}\partial^{\beta^{\prime}_{1}}_{x_{1}}\cdots\partial^{\beta^{\prime}_{n-1}}_{x_{n-1}}\partial_{v_{1}}^{\gamma_{\beta^{\prime}_{1}}}\cdots\partial_{v_{n-1}}^{\gamma_{\beta^{\prime}_{n-1}}}
to the previous display, we deduce for some
\overline{p}\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2}
.

\displaystyle\overline{p}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\mathcal{I}\\
|\beta|\geq M_{1}-k+1\end{subarray}}c_{\beta,1}h_{\beta}\left(\partial_{t}+v^{\prime}\cdot\nabla_{x^{\prime}}-\sum_{i,j\neq n}a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}}\right)\left(t^{\beta_{t}-\beta_{t}^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}x_{j}^{\beta_{x_{j}}-\beta^{\prime}_{x_{j}}}\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}v_{j}^{\beta_{v_{j}}-\beta^{\prime}_{v_{j}}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\mathcal{I}\\
|\beta|\geq M_{1}-k\end{subarray}}c_{\beta,2}\left(t^{\beta_{t}-\beta_{t}^{\prime}}x_{1}^{\beta_{x_{1}}-\beta^{\prime}_{x_{1}}}\cdots x_{n-1}^{\beta_{x_{n-1}}-\beta^{\prime}_{x_{n-1}}}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=n\text{ or }j=n\\
(i,j)\neq(n,n)\end{subarray}}a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}}\left(v_{1}^{{\beta_{v_{1}}-\beta^{\prime}_{v_{1}}}}\cdots v_{n-1}^{{\beta_{v_{n-1}}-\beta^{\prime}_{v_{n}-1}}}h_{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\mathcal{I}\\
|\beta|\geq M_{1}-k\end{subarray}}c_{\beta,3}\left(t^{\beta_{t}-\beta_{t}^{\prime}}x_{1}^{\beta_{x_{1}}-\beta^{\prime}_{x_{1}}}\cdots x_{n-1}^{\beta_{x_{n-1}}-\beta^{\prime}_{x_{n-1}}}v_{1}^{{\beta_{v_{1}}-\beta^{\prime}_{v_{1}}}}\cdots v_{n-1}^{{\beta_{v_{n-1}}-\beta^{\prime}_{v_{n}-1}}}\right)(v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}-\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}})h_{\beta}

\displaystyle\quad+c_{4}(v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}-a^{n,n}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}})h_{\beta^{\prime}}.

Moreover, we observe that
h_{\beta^{\prime}}(0,v_{n})\equiv 0
when
v_{n}>0
. Then using this and the fact that
h_{\beta^{\prime}}
is independent of
t,x^{\prime},v^{\prime}
, we derive that for some
\widetilde{p}=\widetilde{p}(x_{n},v_{n})\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2}
,

(4.10)

\displaystyle(v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}-a^{n,n}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}})h_{\beta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{p}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\mathcal{I}\\
|\beta|=M_{1}-k+1\end{subarray}}c^{\prime}_{\beta,1}h_{\beta}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\mathcal{I}\\
|\beta|=M_{1}-k\end{subarray}}c^{\prime}_{\beta,2}\partial_{v_{n}}h_{\beta}.

Before proceeding further, as in (
3.93
), we observe

(4.11)

\displaystyle(v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}-\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}})(c_{0}v_{n}^{2}\partial_{v_{n}}\phi_{1}+c_{1}v_{n}\phi_{1})=v_{n}^{2}\phi_{0},\,(v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}-\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}})(c_{3}v_{n}\partial_{v_{n}}\phi_{1})=v_{n}\phi_{0},

\displaystyle(v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}-\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}})(c_{4}v^{3}\phi_{0}+c_{5}v\partial_{v}\phi_{1})=v_{n}^{2}\phi_{1},\,\partial^{2}_{v_{n}}\phi_{0}=v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}\phi_{1}=c_{6}v_{n}\phi_{0}.

By the induction assumption (
4.6
), as well as by the observation (
4.11
) and (
3.93
) together with the growth condition (
4.2
), we can deduce

\left\{\begin{aligned} (v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}-a^{n,n}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}})(h_{\beta^{\prime}}-g)&=\widetilde{p}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x_{n}>0\}\times\mathbb{R}$},\\
(h_{\beta^{\prime}}-g)&=\sum_{j\geq 0}b_{j}v^{j}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where
b_{j}=0
when
j>\lambda+\epsilon
and

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle=\widetilde{p}_{\beta}+\sum_{i=0}^{2}a_{i}v_{n}^{i}\phi_{m}+b_{0}\partial_{v_{n}}\phi_{1}+\sum_{i=0}^{1}c_{i}v_{n}^{i}\psi_{0}.

In particular, the constants
a_{i},b_{i},c_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
satisfy (
4.7
) with
a_{\beta,i},b_{\beta},c_{\beta,i}
replaced by
a_{i},b_{0},c_{i}
, respectively. Applying
Theorem 3.15
, we obtain (
4.6
) for
\beta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}
. The inductive argument leads to (
4.6
).

A combination with (
4.3
) and (
4.6
) produces the desired result, which completes the proof.
∎

4.2.
Liouville theorem with unbounded right-hand sides

In this subsection, we prove the Liouville theorem under the assumption that the source term is given by a confluent hypergeometric function and that the boundary data is zero. We define the following
n
-dimensional analogs of the spaces
\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon}
and
{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon}
from the previous section for
\lambda\in\{0,1,2,3\}
and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
with
\lambda+\epsilon<3+\frac{1}{2}
,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon}\coloneqq\mathrm{span}\left\{p,p_{i}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{i}},p_{j+3}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{j+3}},p_{5}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{5},\,\Bigg|\begin{array}[]{l}p\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}\,,p_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}]},\,p_{j+3}\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2},\,p_{5}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{5}{2}]}\\
p_{1}(0)=p_{2}(0)=Dp_{2}(0)=p_{4}(0)=0\end{array}\right\},

and

(4.12)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon}\coloneqq\left\{P\in\overline{T}_{\lambda,\epsilon}\,\Bigg|\,P(z)\equiv 0\text{ on }\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\}\right\},

where the functions
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k}(z)={\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k}(x_{n},v_{n})
are defined in (
3.88
) for
k\in\{0,1,2,3,4,5\}
.

In light of
Theorem 4.1
and
Theorem 3.17
, we get the following.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
\lambda\in\{0,1,2,3\}
and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
with
\lambda+\epsilon<3+\frac{1}{2}
. Let
f
be a weak solution to

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f-a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}}f&=p+Q&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathbb{R}\times\{x_{n}>0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}$},\\
f&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathbb{R}\times\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where
a^{i,j}
is a constant uniformly elliptic matrix with
a^{n,n}=1
and where

(4.13)

\displaystyle{Q}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{i=0}^{2}{q}_{0,i}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{i}+\sum_{k=0}^{4}q_{1,k}\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k}+\sum_{k=0,1,3}{q}_{2,k}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k},

and for
i\in\{0,1,2\}
and
j\in\{0,1\}
,

(4.14)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle q}_{0,i}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{5}{2}]},\,{q}_{1,i}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{3}{2}]},\,{q}_{1,j+3}\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-3},\,{q}_{2,i}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}]},\,{q}_{2,3}\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2},

\displaystyle{\displaystyle q}_{1,2}(0)={q}_{2,0}(0)={q}_{2,1}(0)={q}_{2,3}(0)=D{q}_{2,1}(0)=0.

Suppose that is
c\geq 1
such that for any
R>0
,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}\leq c(1+R)^{\lambda+\epsilon}.

Then,
f\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon}
.

Proof.

First, we claim that for any
Q
as in (
4.13
), (
4.14
), there is a function
H\in\overline{T}_{\lambda,\epsilon}
such that
(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})H-a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}}H=Q
and
H=q
on
\mathbb{R}\times\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\}
for some
q\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}
. Once the claim is proved, we deduce the desired result by applying
Theorem 4.1
to
f-H
.

By
Theorem 3.17
, the claim is already proved whenever
Q=Q(x_{n},v_{n})
. Due to (
4.14
) and the linearity of the equation, it remains to find
H\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon}
in case

\displaystyle Q\in\{v_{i}\partial_{v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0},v_{i}\partial_{v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1},{v_{i}\partial_{v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{2}},{v_{i}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0}},v_{i}v_{j}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0},t\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0},v_{i}v_{j}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1},t\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1},v_{i}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{3}\},

where
i,j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
and
i\not=n
. Before proceeding further, as in (
3.93
), we observe

(4.15)

\displaystyle(v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}-\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}})(v_{n}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{2})=-12\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0}+12\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1}-8\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{2},

\displaystyle(v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}-\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1}=-4\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0},\,(v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}-\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{2}=-24\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0}-6\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1}.

In case
Q\in\{v_{i}\partial_{v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{k}\}
for some
k\in\{0,1,2\}
, we can deduce the claim from (
3.93
) and (
4.15
) , since

(4.16)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}-a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}})\left(v_{i}\sum_{l=0}^{2}c_{k,l}v_{n}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{l}\right)-v_{i}\partial_{v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{k}=\left(\sum_{l=0}^{2}a^{i,n}c_{k,l}(\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{l}+v_{n}\partial_{v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{l})\right).

Moreover, we get from (
4.15
) that

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}-a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}})(c_{0}v_{i}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1})-v_{i}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0}=c_{1}\partial_{v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{1},

which proves the claim for the case
Q=v_{i}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0}
.
To prove the claim for
Q=q_{2,1}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1}
, where
q_{2,1}\in\{t,v_{i}v_{j}\}
, we observe from (
3.93
) that

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}-a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}})\left(q_{2,1}\sum_{k=1}^{2}c_{7,k}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{k}}\right)-q_{2,1}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}q_{2,1}\sum_{k=1}^{2}c_{7,k}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{k}}-a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}}q_{2,1}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{2}c_{7,k}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{k}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-a^{i,n}\partial_{v_{i}}q_{2,1}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{k}}\eqqcolon\sum_{l=0}^{2}Q_{l}.

Since
\mathrm{deg}(q_{2,1})\leq 2
, we note
Q_{l}=Q_{l}(x_{n},v_{n})
for
l\in\{0,1\}
. To treat
Q_{2}
, we use (
4.16
) to see

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}-a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}})\left(\partial_{v_{m}}q_{2,1}\sum_{l=0}^{2}c_{5,k}v_{n}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{l}}\right)-\partial_{v_{m}}q_{2,1}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k}

\displaystyle=-a^{i,n}\partial_{v_{i},v_{m}}q_{2,1}\sum_{l=0}^{2}c_{5,k}({\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{k}}+v_{n}\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{k}})\eqqcolon Q_{3}(x_{n},v_{n}).

Since
Q_{0}+Q_{1}+Q_{3}
is of the form given in (
3.90
), by
Theorem 3.17
, there is a function
H\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon}
such that
(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}-a^{i,j}\partial_{v_{i},v_{j}})H=q_{2,1}\partial_{v_{n}v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1}
.
The proof of the claim in the remaining cases
Q\in\{v_{i}v_{j}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0},t\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0},v_{i}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{3}\}
goes in an analogous way. The proof is complete.
∎

5.
Expansion at grazing boundary points

In this section, we prove expansion estimates at the grazing set. To this end, for any
z_{0}
with
(x_{0})_{n}=(v_{0})_{n}=0
and
a>0
, we introduce two vector spaces

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,z_{0},a}\coloneqq\mathrm{span}\left\{p,p_{i}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{i,a}},p_{j+3}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{j+3,a}},p_{5}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{5,a}\,\Bigg|\begin{array}[]{l}p\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}\,,p_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}]},\,p_{j+3}\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2},\,p_{5}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{5}{2}]}\\
p_{1}(z_{0})=p_{2}(z_{0})=Dp_{2}(z_{0})=p_{4}(z_{0})=0\end{array}\right\},

and

(5.1)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,z_{0},a}\coloneqq\left\{P\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,z_{0},a}\,\Bigg|\,P(z)\equiv 0\text{ on }\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\}\right\},

where
i\in\{0,1,2\}
,
j\in\{0,1\}
and
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{i,a}}(x_{n},v_{n})\coloneqq{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{i}(x_{n}/a,v_{n}/a)
are determined in (
3.88
). In case
a=1
, we denote
{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,z_{0},a}={\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,z_{0}}
and
\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,z_{0},a}=\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,z_{0}}
. The reason to consider these rescaled functions is that they satisfy the equation
(v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}-a^{2}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}})\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,a}=0
.

Now we are ready to provide the main result of this section.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
\lambda\in\{0,1,2,3\}
and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
with
\lambda+\epsilon<3+\frac{1}{2}
and
\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}\not\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
. Let
f
be a weak solution to

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}f)&=B\cdot\nabla_{v}f+F&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0})$},\\
f&=g&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0})$},\end{aligned}\right.

where
(x_{0})_{n}=(v_{0})_{n}=0
,
R\leq 1
,
A\in C^{\lambda-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))
,
B\in C^{\lambda-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))
,
F\in C^{\lambda-2+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))
. Then there exists
P_{z_{0},a}\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,z_{0},a}
such that for any
r\in(0,R/2]
and
z\in\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})
,

(5.2)

\displaystyle|f(z)-P_{z_{0},a}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{\lambda+\epsilon}\bigg(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{\lambda+\epsilon}[F]_{C^{\lambda-2+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{\lambda+\epsilon}[g]_{C^{\lambda+\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))}\bigg),

where
a\coloneqq\sqrt{(A(z_{0}))_{n,n}}
and
c=c(n,\Lambda,\lambda,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{\lambda-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},\|{B}\|_{C^{\lambda-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},{|v_{0}|{\mbox{\Large$\chi$}}_{\lambda\geq 2}})
. In particular, when
\lambda\leq 1
, then

(5.3)

\displaystyle|f(z)-P_{z_{0},a}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq c\left(1+\|B\|_{C^{\lambda-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}\right)^{\lambda+\epsilon}\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{\lambda+\epsilon}\Bigg(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{2}\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle+R^{\lambda+\epsilon}[g]_{C^{\lambda+\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))}\Bigg)

for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))})
.

We provide the proof only for the case
(A(z_{0}))_{n,n}=1
since the result in the general case can easily be deduced using scaling arguments. Moreover, we will explain below how to deduce (
5.3
) from (
5.2
).

To see that it is sufficient to consider
(A(z_{0}))_{n,n}=1
, take
a\coloneqq\sqrt{(A(z_{0}))_{n,n}}\in[\sqrt{\Lambda^{-1}},\sqrt{\Lambda}]
and define

\displaystyle f_{a}(z)\coloneqq f(t,x^{\prime},ax_{n},v^{\prime},av_{n}),\,F_{a}(z)\coloneqq F(t,x^{\prime},ax_{n},v^{\prime},av_{n}),\,g_{a}(z)\coloneqq g(t,x^{\prime},ax_{n},v^{\prime},av_{n})

to see that

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f_{a}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(\widetilde{A}\nabla_{v}f_{a})&=\widetilde{B}\cdot\nabla_{v}f+F_{a}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\widetilde{{H}}_{R/a}(z_{0})$},\\
f_{a}&=g_{a}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\widetilde{{Q}}_{R/a}(z_{0})$},\end{aligned}\right.

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{R/a}(z_{0})\coloneqq\{(t,x^{\prime},x_{n},v^{\prime},v_{n})\,:\,t\in I_{R}(t_{0}),\,|(v^{\prime},av_{n})|<R,\,|(x^{\prime}-x_{0}^{\prime}-v_{0}^{\prime}(t-t_{0}),ax_{n})|<R^{3}\},

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{R/a}(z_{0})\coloneqq\widetilde{Q}_{R/a}(z_{0})\cap(\mathbb{R}\times\{x_{n}>0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{i,j}(z)\coloneqq\begin{cases}A_{i,j}(t,x^{\prime},ax_{n},v^{\prime},av_{n})&\quad\text{if }i,j\neq n,\\
A_{i,n}(t,x^{\prime},ax_{n},v^{\prime},av_{n})/a&\quad\text{if }i\neq n,\\
A_{n,n}(t,x^{\prime},ax_{n},v^{\prime},av_{n})/a^{2},\end{cases}\quad\widetilde{B}_{i}(z)\coloneqq\begin{cases}B(t,x^{\prime},ax_{n},v^{\prime},av_{n})\quad\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \text{if }i\neq n,\\
B(t,x^{\prime},ax_{n},v^{\prime},av_{n})/a\quad\text{if }i=n.\end{cases}

Now, we consider two cases depending on the range of
a
.

•

a\geq 1
. Then by the estimates given in
section 5
with
(A(z_{0}))_{n,n}=1
together with the fact that
\mathcal{Q}_{R/a}(z_{0})\subset\widetilde{{Q}}_{R/a}(z_{0})
and
\mathcal{H}_{R/a}(z_{0})\subset\widetilde{{H}}_{R/a}(z_{0})
, there is a function
P\in\mathcal{\overline{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,z_{0}}
such that for any
ra\leq R/(2a)
,

\displaystyle\|f_{a}-P\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{ra}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{2ra^{2}}{R}\right)^{\lambda+\epsilon}\Bigg(\|f_{a}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R/a}(z_{0}))}+R^{\lambda+\epsilon}[F_{a}]_{C^{\lambda-2+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R/a}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+R^{\lambda+\epsilon}[g_{a}]_{C^{\lambda+\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R/a}(z_{0}))}\Bigg),

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|\widetilde{A}\|_{C^{\lambda-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},\|\widetilde{B}\|_{C^{\lambda-1+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},{|v_{0}|{\mbox{\Large$\chi$}}_{\lambda\geq 2}})
. Therefore, by the change of variables, we have for any
r\leq R/(2a^{2})

\displaystyle\|f-P_{a}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{\lambda+\epsilon}\bigg(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{\lambda+\epsilon}[F]_{C^{\lambda-2+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+R^{\lambda+\epsilon}[g]_{C^{\lambda+\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))}\bigg)\eqqcolon L_{1}(r),

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|{A}\|_{C^{\lambda-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},\|{B}\|_{C^{\lambda-1+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},{|v_{0}|{\mbox{\Large$\chi$}}_{\lambda\geq 2}})
, as
a\in[\sqrt{\Lambda^{-1}},\sqrt{\Lambda}]
, and

\displaystyle P_{a}(t,x^{\prime},x_{n},v^{\prime},v_{n})\coloneqq P(t,x^{\prime},x_{n}/a,v^{\prime},v_{n}/a)\in\mathcal{\overline{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,z_{0},a}.

To prove the expansion for
r\in(R/(2a^{2}),R/2)
, we observe that by taking
r=R/(2a^{2})
, we get

\displaystyle\|P_{a}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R/(2a)^{2}}(z_{0}))}\leq cL_{1}(R)

for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|{A}\|_{C^{\lambda-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},\|{B}\|_{C^{\lambda-1+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},{|v_{0}|{\mbox{\Large$\chi$}}_{\lambda\geq 2}})
.
Since the vector space
\mathcal{\overline{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,z_{0},a}
is finite dimensional and
a\in[\Lambda^{-1},\Lambda]
, we have for any
r\in(R/(2a^{2}),R/2)
,

\displaystyle\|P_{a}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq c\|P_{a}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R/(2a^{2})}(z_{0}))},

where
c=c(\Lambda)
. These two estimates imply the desired result when
r>R/(2a)^{2}
.

•

a<1
. Then we have
\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0})\subset\widetilde{{Q}}_{R/a}(z_{0})
and
\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0})\subset\widetilde{{H}}_{R/a}(z_{0})
, and there is a function
P\in\mathcal{\overline{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,z_{0}}
such that for any
r/a\leq R/2
,

\displaystyle\|f_{a}-P\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r/a}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{2r}{R/a}\right)^{\lambda+\epsilon}\Bigg(\|f_{a}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{\lambda+\epsilon}[F_{a}]_{C^{\lambda-2+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+R^{\lambda+\epsilon}[g_{a}]_{C^{\lambda+\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))}\Bigg),

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|\widetilde{A}\|_{C^{\lambda-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},\|\widetilde{B}\|_{C^{\lambda-1+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},{|v_{0}|{\mbox{\Large$\chi$}}_{\lambda\geq 2}})
. Similarly, by the change of variables when
r\leq aR/2
and by taking the constant
c
sufficiently large when
r>aR/2
, we get the desired estimate.

Next, we derive the estimate (
5.3
) from (
5.2
) when
\lambda\leq 1
. To this end, for any
\mathcal{B}>0
, we define

(5.4)

\displaystyle f_{\mathcal{B}}(z)\coloneqq f(S_{\mathcal{B}}z),\,F_{\mathcal{B}}(z)\coloneqq\mathcal{B}^{2}F(S_{\mathcal{B}}z),\,g_{\mathcal{B}}(z)\coloneqq g(S_{\mathcal{B}}z),

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}\coloneqq A(S_{\mathcal{B}}z),\,\widetilde{B}(z)\coloneqq\mathcal{B}{B(S_{\mathcal{B}}z)},\,z_{\mathcal{B}}\coloneqq S_{\mathcal{B}^{-1}}z_{0}

to see that

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f_{\mathcal{B}}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(\widetilde{A}\nabla_{v}f_{\mathcal{B}})&=\widetilde{B}\cdot\nabla_{v}f_{\mathcal{B}}+F_{\mathcal{B}}&&\qquad\mbox{in ${\mathcal{H}}_{R/{\mathcal{B}}}(z_{\mathcal{B}})$},\\
f_{\mathcal{B}}&=g_{\mathcal{B}}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}_{R/\mathcal{B}}(z_{\mathcal{B}})$}\end{aligned}\right.

with
z_{\mathcal{B}}\in\gamma_{0}
. Since
\Lambda^{-1}I\leq\widetilde{A}\leq\Lambda I
, by (
5.2
), there is a function
\widetilde{P}_{0,a}\in\mathcal{\overline{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,0,a}
with
a=\sqrt{(\widetilde{A}(z_{\mathcal{B}}))_{n,n}}
such that for any
\rho\leq\min\left\{\frac{R}{\mathcal{B}},1\right\}\eqqcolon R_{0}\leq 1
and
z\in\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{\mathcal{B}})
,

\displaystyle|f_{\mathcal{B}}(z)-\widetilde{P}_{0,a}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{\rho}{R_{0}}\right)^{\lambda+\epsilon}\Bigg(\|f_{\mathcal{B}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(z_{\mathcal{B}}))}+R_{0}^{\lambda+\epsilon}[F_{\mathcal{B}}]_{C^{\lambda-2+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(z_{\mathcal{B}}))}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+R_{0}^{\lambda+\epsilon}[g_{\mathcal{B}}]_{C^{\lambda+\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{\mathcal{B}}))}\Bigg),

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\lambda,\epsilon,\|\widetilde{A}\|_{C^{\lambda-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(z_{\mathcal{B}}))},\|\widetilde{B}\|_{C^{\lambda-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(z_{\mathcal{B}}))})
. By choosing

\mathcal{B}\coloneqq\left(1+\|B\|_{C^{\lambda-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}}\right)^{-1}\leq 1,

we have

(5.5)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{A}\|_{C^{\lambda-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(z_{\mathcal{B}}))}\leq\|{A}\|_{C^{\lambda-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}\mathcal{B}}(z_{0}))}\leq\|A\|_{C^{\lambda-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{B}\|_{C^{\lambda-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(z_{\mathcal{B}}))}\leq\mathcal{B}\|{B}\|_{C^{\lambda-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}\mathcal{B}}(z_{0}))}\leq\mathcal{B}\|B\|_{C^{\lambda-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}\leq c_{B}.

Next, by scaling back together with the fact that
(\widetilde{A}(0))_{n,n}=(A(z_{0}))_{n,n}
and by the homogeneity of the functions
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{k}
, there is
{P}_{0,a}\in\mathcal{\overline{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,0,a}
such that for any
\rho\leq R_{0}
and
z\in\mathcal{H}_{\rho\mathcal{B}}(z_{0})
, we have

\displaystyle|f(z)-{P}_{0,a}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{\rho}{R_{0}}\right)^{\lambda+\epsilon}\Bigg(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+{(R_{0}\mathcal{B})}^{\lambda+\epsilon}[F]_{C^{\lambda-2+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle+{(R_{0}\mathcal{B})}^{\lambda+\epsilon}[g]_{C^{\lambda+\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))}\Bigg),

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\lambda,\epsilon,\|{A}\|_{C^{\lambda-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},c_{B})
by (
5.5
). Thus, for any
z\in\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{0})
, we have

(5.6)

\displaystyle|f(z)-{P}_{0,a}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{\rho}{R}\right)^{\lambda+\epsilon}\Bigg(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+{R}^{\lambda+\epsilon}[F]_{C^{\lambda-2+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+{R}^{\lambda+\epsilon}[g]_{C^{\lambda+\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))}\Bigg),

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\lambda,\epsilon,\|{A}\|_{C^{\lambda-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},c_{B})
, whenever
R\leq\mathcal{B}
.

On the other hand, if
\frac{R}{\mathcal{B}}\geq 1
, then we have for any
\rho\leq\mathcal{B}
and
z\in\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{0})
,

(5.7)

\displaystyle|f(z)-{P}_{0,a}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{\rho}{\mathcal{B}}\right)^{\lambda+\epsilon}\Bigg(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+{\mathcal{B}}^{\lambda+\epsilon}[F]_{C^{\lambda-2+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle+{\mathcal{B}}^{\lambda+\epsilon}[g]_{C^{\lambda+\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))}\Bigg)\eqqcolon L_{2}(\rho).

Since
P_{0,a}(z)=\sum\limits_{\beta\in\mathcal{I}}p_{0,a,\beta}
, where the
p_{0,a,\beta}
are homogeneous functions of degree
\beta
with
\beta\leq 3
, we have

\displaystyle\|p_{0,a,\beta}(z)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq c\left(\frac{r}{\mathcal{B}}\right)^{\beta}L_{2}(\mathcal{B}),

whenever
r\geq\mathcal{B}
.
Therefore, using this and the fact that the set
\mathcal{I}
is finite, and that
\mathcal{B}\leq r
,
R\leq 1
, we derive for
\mathcal{B}\leq\ r\leq\frac{R}{2}
,

\displaystyle|f(z)-{P}_{0,a}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal{I},|\beta|\leq 3}c\left(\frac{r}{\mathcal{B}}\right)^{\beta}L_{2}(\mathcal{B})

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{\rho}{R}\right)^{\lambda+\epsilon}\frac{1}{\mathcal{B}^{\lambda+\epsilon}}\Bigg(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+{R}^{\lambda+\epsilon}[F]_{C^{\lambda-2+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+{R}^{\lambda+\epsilon}[g]_{C^{\lambda+\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))}\Bigg).

Therefore, using this, (
5.7
), and (
5.6
), we get (
5.3
). Thus, it remains to prove only (
5.2
) with
(A(z_{0}))_{n,n}=1
.

5.1.
Basic lemmas

Before proving
section 5
, we prove several lemmas which provide global uniform bounds of solutions when the source term is given by a multiple of a smooth function and another function from the following set

(5.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\coloneqq\{1,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{k},\partial_{v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{k},\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{k}\,:\,k\in\{0,1,2,3,4,5\}\}.

Recall that the functions
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{k}
are defined in (
3.88
) and source terms of this form occur when applying for instance
\phi_{0}
to an equation with coefficients. Hence, the following results will be crucial in the proof of
section 5
which is based on a blow-up argument.

The following lemma yields a Hölder regularity estimate. We will prove it by Campanato-type perturbation arguments. We will use crucially that for any
\boldsymbol{\Phi}\in\mathcal{G}
, it holds
\boldsymbol{\Phi}=\boldsymbol{\Phi}(x_{n},v_{n})\in L^{2+\epsilon_{0}}(H_{R})
and
\partial_{v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0}\in L^{4+\epsilon_{0}}(H_{R})
for any
R>0
with
\epsilon_{0}=\frac{1}{3}
(see
subsection C.1
).

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
f
be a weak solution to

(5.9)

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}f)&=B\cdot\nabla_{v}f+F\boldsymbol{\Phi}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(G\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0})&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{1}$},\\
f&=g&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{1}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where
B\in L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1};\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
F\in L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1})
,
G\in L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1};\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
g\in C^{\alpha}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{1})
, and
\boldsymbol{\Phi}\in\mathcal{G}
. Then there is a small constant
\beta=\beta(n,\Lambda,\alpha)
such that

\displaystyle[f]_{C^{\beta}(\mathcal{H}_{1/2})}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}+[g]_{C^{\alpha}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{1})}+\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}+\|G\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}\right)

for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda,\alpha,\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1})})
.

Proof.

Let
z_{0}\in\gamma
and
r\leq 1/16
. We want to prove that

(5.10)

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})}|f-(f)_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})}|\leq cr^{\beta}(M_{0}+[g]_{C^{\alpha}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0}))})

for some
\beta=\beta(n,\Lambda)
, where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1})})
and
M_{0}\coloneqq\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}+\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}+\|G\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}
.
Let us assume
z_{0}\in\gamma_{0}
.
First, by
[
39
, Corollary 2.9]
, there is a unique weak solution
h
to

(5.11)

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})h-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}h)&=B\nabla_{v}h&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})$},\\
h&=f&&\qquad\mbox{in $\partial_{\mathrm{kin}}\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})$}.\end{aligned}\right.

Let us write
\widetilde{F}\coloneqq-F\boldsymbol{\Phi}
and
\widetilde{G}\coloneqq G{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0}
, and denote
x_{0}=(x_{0}^{\prime},0)
and
v_{0}=(v_{0}^{\prime},0)
to see that

(5.12)

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})}|r^{2}\widetilde{F}|^{2}

\displaystyle+|r\widetilde{G}|^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq r^{4}\|F\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}\left(\sup_{t\in I_{r}(t_{0})}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{B_{r^{3}}}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{{B}_{r}}|\boldsymbol{\Phi}|^{2+\epsilon_{0}}\,dx_{n}\,dv_{n}\right)^{\frac{2}{2+\epsilon_{0}}}

\displaystyle+r^{2}\|G\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}\left(\sup_{t\in I_{r}(t_{0})}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{B_{r^{3}}}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{{B}_{r}}|{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0}|^{4+\epsilon_{0}}\,dx_{n}\,dv_{n}\right)^{\frac{2}{4+\epsilon_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leq cr^{\frac{2\epsilon_{0}}{4+\epsilon_{0}}}(M_{0})^{2},

where we have used Hölder’s inequality,
\|\boldsymbol{\Phi}\|_{L^{2+\epsilon_{0}}(H_{1})},\|\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0}\|_{L^{4+\epsilon_{0}}(H_{1})}\leq c
with
\epsilon_{0}=\frac{1}{3}
.
Since
f-h
solves the following equation

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})(f-h)-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}(f-h))&=B\nabla_{v}(f-h)+\widetilde{F}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(\widetilde{G})&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})$},\\
f-h&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\partial_{\mathrm{kin}}\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})$}\end{aligned}\right.

we can test with
f-h
, and deduce

(5.13)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Lambda}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})}|\nabla_{v}(f-h)|^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0}))}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})}|\nabla_{v}(f-h)|f-h|

\displaystyle+{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})}|\widetilde{F}||f-h|^{2}+{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})}|\widetilde{G}||\nabla_{v}(f-h)|.

Next note from the Poincaré inequality with respect to the
v
variable and using also (
5.12
), (
5.13
), and Young’s inequality, we get
