\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})}|f-h|^{2}\,dz\leq c{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})}r^{2}|\nabla_{v}(f-h)|^{2}\,dz\leq c\left(\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}^{2}r^{2}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})}|f-h|^{2}\,dz+cr^{\frac{2\epsilon_{0}}{8+\epsilon_{0}}}M_{0}^{2}\right)

for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda)
. Hence, there is a small constant
r_{0}=r_{0}(n,\Lambda,\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1})})
such that when
r\leq r_{0}
, then it holds

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})}|f-h|^{2}\,dz\leq cr^{\frac{2\epsilon_{0}}{8+\epsilon_{0}}}M_{0}^{2}.

Note that when
r>r_{0}
, then (
5.10
) follows directly. Thus, we assume
r\leq r_{0}
. Next, by
[
35
, Lemma 2.25]
together with
subsection 7.1
, we derive

\displaystyle[h]_{C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{r/16}(z_{0}))}\leq c\left(r^{-(2n+1)}\|r^{-\alpha}h\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{H}_{r/8}(z_{0}))}+[g]_{C^{\alpha}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r/8}(z_{0}))}\right),

as
f=g
on
\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r/8}(z_{0})
. This together with the fact that
h-(h)_{\mathcal{H}_{r/8}(z_{0})}
solves (
5.11
) with
f
replaced by
f-(h)_{\mathcal{H}_{r/8}(z_{0})}
implies that for any
\rho\in(0,1/16]
,

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho r}(z_{0})}|h-(h)_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho r}(z_{0})}|^{2}\leq c\rho^{2\alpha}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})}|h-(h)_{\mathcal{H}_{r/8}(z_{0})}|^{2}\,dz+cr^{2\alpha}[g]_{C^{\alpha}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r/8}(z_{0}))}^{2}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho r}(z_{0})}|f-(f)_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho r}(z_{0})}|^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq c{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho r}(z_{0})}|h-(h)_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho r}(z_{0})}|^{2}\,dz+c{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho r}(z_{0})}|f-h|^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq c\rho^{2\alpha}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r/8}(z_{0})}|h-(h)_{\mathcal{H}_{r/8}(z_{0})}|^{2}+cr^{2\alpha}[g]^{2}_{C^{\alpha}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r/8}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\quad+c\rho^{-(4n+2)}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r/8}(z_{0})}|f-h|^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq c\rho^{2\alpha}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r/8}(z_{0})}|f-(f)_{\mathcal{H}_{r/8}(z_{0})}|^{2}+cr^{2\alpha}[g]^{2}_{C^{\alpha}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r/8}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\quad+c\rho^{-(4n+2)}r^{\frac{2\epsilon_{0}}{8+\epsilon_{0}}}M_{0}^{2}

for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda)
.
Now, taking
\rho^{2(\alpha-\beta)}\leq\frac{1}{c}
with
\beta=\frac{1}{2}\min\left\{\alpha,\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{8+\epsilon_{0}}\right\}
and following the standard iteration as in
[
30
, Lemma 5.6]
, we get (
5.10
) when
z_{0}\in\gamma_{0}
.

We now assume
z_{0}\in\gamma\setminus\gamma_{0}
. If
\mathcal{H}_{2r}(z_{0})\cap\gamma_{0}\neq\emptyset
, then there is a point
z_{1}\in\gamma_{0}
such that
\mathcal{H}_{2r}(z_{0})\subset\mathcal{H}_{8r}(z_{1})
. Indeed, we can choose
z_{1}=(t_{0},x_{1}^{\prime},0,v_{1}^{\prime},0)
, where
(t_{1},x_{1}^{\prime},0,v_{1}^{\prime},0)\in\mathcal{H}_{2r}(z_{0})\cap\gamma_{0}
. In addition, in this case,
|v_{0,n}|\leq 2r
, and we have
|\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})|\eqsim r^{4n+2}
. Therefore, we get

(5.14)

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})}|f-(f)_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})}|^{2}\,dz\leq c{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{8r}(z_{1})}|f-(f)_{\mathcal{H}_{8r}(z_{1})}|^{2}\,dz\leq cr^{\beta},

where we have also used (
5.10
) together with the fact that
z_{1}\in\gamma_{0}
. We assume
\mathcal{H}_{2r}(z_{0})\cap\gamma_{0}=\emptyset
so that
2r<|v_{0,n}|
. Then by
[
35
, Lemma 2.25]
, we have for
r_{0}\coloneqq|v_{0,n}|/2
,

\displaystyle[f]_{C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq c\left(|\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0})|^{-\frac{1}{2}}r_{0}^{-\alpha}\|f-(f)_{\mathcal{H}_{3r_{0}/2}(z_{0})}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{H}_{3r_{0}/2}(z_{0}))}+r_{0}v_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}M_{0}+[g]_{C^{\alpha}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{3r_{0}/2}(z_{0}))}\right),

where we have also used the fact that
|\boldsymbol{\Phi}(z)|,|\partial_{v_{n}}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0}(z)|\leq cv_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
when
z\in\mathcal{H}_{3r_{0}/2}(z_{0})
, as
|v_{0,n}|-3r_{0}/2\geq|v_{0,n}|/4
.
Thus, we deduce

(5.15)

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})}|f-(f)_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})}|^{2}\leq c\left(\frac{r}{r_{0}}\right)^{\beta}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{3r_{0}/2}(z_{0})}|f-(f)_{\mathcal{H}_{3r_{0}/2}(z_{0})}|^{2}+cr^{\beta}(M_{0}+[g]_{C^{\alpha}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{1})}).

Since
\mathcal{H}_{3r_{0}(z_{0})}\cap\gamma_{0}\neq\emptyset
, plugging (
5.14
) into (
5.15
) leads to (
5.10
) when
z_{0}\in\gamma\setminus\gamma_{0}
.
Now, combining (
5.10
) and the interior regularity results from
[
12
]
leads to the desired estimate.
∎

Based on
[
35
, Lemma 2.27]
, we now use a compactness argument to replace the
L^{\infty}
-norm by the Hölder seminorm. As we will use this result only for equations without source terms in divergence form, we will prove the result only in this setting.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
f
be a weak solution to

(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}f)=B\cdot\nabla_{v}f+F\boldsymbol{\Phi}\quad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{1}$},

where
A\in C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{1})
and
B\in C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{1};\mathbb{R}^{n})
for some
\alpha>0
,
F\in C^{\lambda,\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1})
for some nonnegative integer
\lambda\geq 0
and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, and
\boldsymbol{\Phi}\in\mathcal{G}
. Then,

\displaystyle\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}+[F]_{C^{\lambda,\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}\right),

where
c
depends only on
n,\Lambda,\lambda,\epsilon,\alpha,\|A\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{1})},\|B\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}
.

Proof.

Let us fix any polynomial
p\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}
and write
F=(F-p)+p
.
We want to show that

(5.16)

\displaystyle\|p\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}

\displaystyle\leq c\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}+\|F-p\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}.

Now by following the argument given in the proof of
[
35
, Lemma 2.7]
, we derive that if (
5.16
) is not true, then there are sequences
(A_{l})_{l}
,
(B_{l})_{l}
,
(F_{l})_{l}
,
(f_{l})_{l}
,
(p_{l})_{l}
such that

(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f_{l}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A_{l}\nabla_{v}f_{l})=B_{l}\nabla_{v}f_{l}+F_{l}\boldsymbol{\Phi}\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{1}$},

where

\displaystyle\|A_{l}\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}+\|B_{l}\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}\leq\Lambda\quad\text{and}\quad p_{l}\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda},

as well as

(5.17)

\displaystyle\|p_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}=1,

and

(5.18)

\displaystyle\|f_{l}\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}+\|F_{l}-p_{l}\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}\to 0.

By (
5.8
) and the explicit formulae of
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}\in\mathcal{G}
given in the appendix, we observe
[\boldsymbol{\Phi}]_{C^{\alpha}(Q_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq c(\alpha,r,z_{0})
for any
Q_{r}(z_{0})\Subset\{x_{n}>0\}\times\mathbb{R}
for each
i=1,2
. Thus, using
[
30
, Theorem 1.15]
, we get

\displaystyle\|f_{l}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq c(\alpha,r,z_{0})

for any
\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0})\Subset\mathcal{H}_{1}
, where the constant
c
is independent of
l
. This implies that there is a function
f_{\infty}
such that
f_{l}\to f_{\infty}
in
C^{1,\alpha}(\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0}))
, whenever
\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0})\Subset\mathcal{H}_{1}
. Using this and (
5.18
), we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0})}-f_{\infty}(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})\psi

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0})}A_{\infty}\nabla_{v}f_{\infty}\nabla_{v}\psi+\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0})}B_{\infty}\cdot\nabla_{v}\psi\,dz+\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0})}p_{\infty}\boldsymbol{\Phi}\psi

for any
\psi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0}))
.
At the same time, by (
5.18
) it holds
f_{\infty}=0
, but this is a contradiction with (
5.17
).

Hence, we have shown (
5.16
). From the characterization of kinetic Hölder spaces via polynomials, we deduce the desired result.
∎

By combining
subsection 5.1
and
subsection 5.1
with a scaling argument, we derive the following lemma, which will be used to obtain compactness of the blow-up sequence in the proof of
section 5
. Recall that the functions
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k}
are determined in (
3.88
).

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
f
be a weak solution to

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}f)&=B\cdot\nabla_{v}f+F_{0}+\sum_{k=0}^{5}\sum_{j=0}^{2}F_{k,j}\partial_{v_{n}}^{(j)}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{k}}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{R}$},\\
f&=g&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where
A\in C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{R})
,
F_{0}\in C^{\lambda,\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{R})
,
F_{i,j}\in C^{\lambda_{i,j},\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{R})
,
g\in C^{\alpha}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R})
for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
\lambda,\lambda_{k,j}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
. Then there is a small constant
\beta=\beta(n,\Lambda,\alpha)
such that

(5.19)

\displaystyle[f]_{C^{\beta}(\mathcal{H}_{R/2})}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}+[F_{0}]_{C^{\lambda,\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}+\sum_{k=0}^{5}\sum_{j=0}^{2}[F_{k,j}]_{C^{\lambda_{k,j},\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}+[g]_{C^{\alpha}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R})}\right)

for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda,\lambda,\lambda_{k,j},\alpha,\|A\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{R})},\|B\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{R})},R)
.

Note that it is possible to compute the precise dependence of the constant
c
on
R
in (
5.19
). However, for our purpose it will be sufficient to use the fact that
c
depends only on
n,\Lambda,\|A\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{R})},\|B\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{R})},R
.

5.2.
Proof of expansion estimates

Now we are going to prove
section 5
when
(A(z_{0}))_{n,n}=1
.

Proof of
section 5
with
(A(z_{0}))_{n,n}=1
.

We may assume
R=1
and

(5.20)

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}(z_{0}))}+\rho^{\epsilon}[g]_{C^{\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}(z_{0}))}\leq\rho^{\lambda+\epsilon}[g]_{C^{\lambda,\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}(z_{0}))}\quad\text{for any }\rho\leq 1

for some constant
c=c(n,\lambda,\epsilon
), by considering
(f-P[g]_{z_{0},\lambda})
as a solution instead of
f
itself, where
P[g]_{z_{0},\lambda}
is the Taylor expansion of the function
g
of order
\lambda
at
z_{0}
. This creates an explicit
|v_{0}|
dependence of the constant in the estimate when
\lambda\geq 2
, since the source term now depends on
v_{0}
.

By contradiction, we suppose that there are sequences
(f_{l})_{l}
,
(z_{0,l})_{l}
,
(A_{l})_{l}
,
(B_{l})
,
(F_{l})_{l}
, and
(g_{l})_{l}
with

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f_{l}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A_{l}\nabla_{v}f_{l})&=B_{l}\cdot\nabla_{v}f_{l}+F_{l}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0,l})$},\\
f_{l}&=g_{l}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{1}(z_{0,l})$}\end{aligned}\right.

with
(A_{l}(z_{0,l}))_{n,n}=1
, as well as
(x_{0,l})_{n}=(v_{0,l})_{n}=0
such that

(5.21)

\displaystyle\|f_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0,l}))},\quad[F_{l}]_{C^{\lambda-2,\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0,l}))}\leq 1,\quad\|A\|_{C^{\lambda-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0,l}))},\|B\|_{C^{\lambda-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0,l}))}\leq\Lambda,

\displaystyle\|g_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{0,l}))}+\rho^{\epsilon}[g_{l}]_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{0,l}))}\leq\rho^{\lambda+\epsilon}\quad\text{for any }\rho\leq 1,

but

\displaystyle\sup_{l\in\mathbb{N}}\inf_{P\in\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\epsilon,l}}\sup_{\rho\in(0,1/2]}\rho^{-(\lambda+\epsilon)}\|f_{l}-P\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{\rho}(z_{0,l}))}=\infty,

where we denote from now on
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\epsilon,l}\coloneq\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\epsilon,z_{0,l}}
.

Since
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\epsilon,l}
is a finite dimensional vector space, for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
\rho\in(0,1/2]
, there is a unique
P_{l,\rho}\in\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\epsilon,l}
such that

\displaystyle\|f_{l}-P_{l,\rho}\|_{L^{2}(H_{\rho}(z_{0,l}))}\leq\|f_{l}-P\|_{L^{2}(H_{\rho}(z_{0,l}))}\quad\text{for any }P\in\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\epsilon,l},

and

(5.22)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{0,l})}(f_{l}-P_{l,\rho})P\,dz=0\quad\text{for any }P\in\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\epsilon,l}.

Let us define

\displaystyle\theta(r)\coloneqq\sup_{l\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{\rho\in[r,1/2]}\rho^{-(\lambda+\epsilon)}\|f_{l}-P_{l,\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{0,l}))}.

Then we have
\theta(r)\to\infty
as
r\to 0
by
[
35
, Lemma 2.14]
, using the same argument as in the proof of
[
35
, Lemma 4.1]
.
Since
\theta(r)
is non-increasing, there are sequences
(l_{m})_{m}
and
(r_{l_{m}})_{m}
such that
r_{l_{m}}\to 0
, as
m\to\infty
, and

(5.23)

\displaystyle\theta(r_{l_{m}})\geq r_{l_{m}}^{-(\lambda+\epsilon)}\|f_{l_{m}}-P_{l_{m},r_{l_{m}}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{l_{m}}}(z_{0,l_{m}}))}\geq\theta(r_{l_{m}})/2.

We may write from now on
l_{m}=m
. We deduce from
[
35
, Lemma 2.15]
that

(5.24)

\displaystyle\|f_{m}-P_{m,r_{m}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{Rr_{m}}(z_{0,m}))}\leq c(Rr_{m})^{\lambda+\epsilon}\theta(r_{m})

and

(5.25)

\displaystyle\frac{C_{m}}{\theta(r_{m})}\coloneqq\frac{(\|p_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{1}(z_{0,m}))}+\|p_{m,k}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{1}(z_{0,m}))})}{\theta(r_{m})}\to 0

for each
k\in\{0,\ldots,5\}
. Now define

(5.26)

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{m}(z)\coloneqq\frac{f_{m}(z_{0,m}\circ S_{r_{m}}\,z)-P_{m,r_{m}}(z_{0,m}\circ S_{r_{m}}\,z)}{r_{m}^{\lambda+\epsilon}\theta(r_{m})}

and

(5.27)

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{m}(z)\coloneqq A_{m}(z_{0,m}\circ S_{r_{m}}z),\quad\widetilde{B}_{m}(z)\coloneqq r_{m}B_{m}(z_{0,m}\circ S_{r_{m}}z).

Then, we recall from (
5.1
) that
P_{m,r_{m}}
is of the form

\displaystyle P_{m,r_{m}}(z)\coloneqq p_{m}+\sum_{k=0}^{5}p_{m,k}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k}

to see that
\widetilde{f}_{m}
solves

(5.28)

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})\widetilde{f}_{m}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(\widetilde{A}_{m}\nabla_{v}\widetilde{f}_{m})&=\widetilde{B}_{m}\cdot\nabla_{v}\widetilde{f}_{m}+\widetilde{F}_{m}+\widetilde{Q}_{m}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{1/r_{m}}$},\\
\widetilde{f}_{m}&=\widetilde{g}_{m}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{1/r_{m}}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{m}(z)\coloneqq\frac{F_{m}(z_{0,m}\circ S_{r_{m}}\,z)-\widetilde{p}_{m}(z)}{r_{m}^{\lambda-2+\epsilon}\theta(r_{m})},\penalty\ \penalty\ \widetilde{g}_{m}(z)\coloneqq\frac{g_{m}(z_{0,m}\circ S_{r_{m}}\,z)}{r_{m}^{\lambda+\epsilon}\theta(r_{m})},

\displaystyle\widetilde{p}_{m}(z)\coloneqq(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})(\overline{p}_{m}(z))+\mathrm{div}_{v}(\widetilde{A}_{m}\nabla_{v}(\overline{p}_{m}(z)))-\widetilde{B}_{m}\cdot\nabla_{v}(\overline{p}_{m}(z)),

\displaystyle P_{m}(z)\coloneqq\sum_{k=0}^{5}\frac{(p_{m,k}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{k})(z_{0,m}\circ S_{r_{m}}z)}{r_{m}^{\lambda+\epsilon}\theta(r_{m})},

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{m}(z)\coloneqq(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})(P_{m})(z)-\mathrm{div}_{v}(\widetilde{A}_{m}\nabla_{v}P_{m}(z))-\widetilde{B}_{m}\cdot\nabla_{v}P_{m}(z)

and
\overline{p}_{m}(z)\coloneqq{p}_{m}(z_{0,m}\circ S_{r_{m}}\,z)
.
In order to conclude the proof, our goal is to establish compactness of the sequence
(\widetilde{f}_{m})
so that we can pass the equation for
\widetilde{f}_{m}
to the limit
m\to\infty
. Then, we will apply the Liouville theorem from
subsection 4.2
to obtain a contradiction with (
5.23
). To do so, the main work is to carefully track the structural properties of the terms
\widetilde{Q}_{m}
. In fact, we can already observe that
p_{m}\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}
,
p_{m,i}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}]}
,
p_{m,j+3}\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2}
,
p_{m,5}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{5}{2}]}
for
i\in\{0,1,2\}
and
j\in\{0,1\}
with

(5.29)

p_{m,1}(z_{0})=p_{m,2}(z_{0})=Dp_{m,2}(z_{0})=p_{m,4}(z_{0})=0.

Hence, after a few computations, using (
5.29
), we derive

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{m}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{2}\left({\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{i}(r_{m}X_{n})\left[\frac{c_{m,i}}{r_{m}^{\lambda+\epsilon-2}\theta(r_{m})}+\frac{\widetilde{B}_{m}\cdot\widetilde{P}_{m,i}}{r_{m}^{\lambda+\epsilon-1}\theta(r_{m})}\right]-\frac{\mathrm{div}_{v}(\widetilde{A}_{m}\widetilde{P}_{m,i}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{i}(r_{m}X_{n}))}{r_{m}^{\lambda+\epsilon-1}\theta(r_{m})}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\left({\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{j+3}(r_{m}X_{n})\frac{\widetilde{B}_{m}\cdot\widetilde{P}_{m,j+3}}{r_{m}^{\lambda+\epsilon-1}\theta(r_{m})}-\frac{\mathrm{div}_{v}(\widetilde{A}_{m}\widetilde{P}_{m,j+3}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{j+3}(r_{m}X_{n}))}{r_{m}^{\lambda+\epsilon-1}\theta(r_{m})}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=0}^{2}{r_{m}v_{n}(\partial_{x_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{i})(r_{m}X_{n})}\frac{\overline{p}_{m,i}}{r_{m}^{\lambda+\epsilon-2}\theta(r_{m})}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}{r_{m}v_{n}(\partial_{x_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{j+3})(r_{m}X_{n})}\frac{\overline{p}_{m,i}}{r_{m}^{\lambda+\epsilon-2}\theta(r_{m})}

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{i=0}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{div}_{v}(\widetilde{A}_{m}\overline{p}_{m,i}(\nabla_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{i})(r_{m}X_{n}))}{r_{m}^{\lambda+\epsilon-1}\theta(r_{m})}-\sum_{j=0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{div}_{v}(\widetilde{A}_{m}\overline{p}_{m,j}(\nabla_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{j+3})(r_{m}X_{n}))}{r_{m}^{\lambda+\epsilon-1}\theta(r_{m})}

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{i=0}^{2}{(\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{i})(r_{m}X_{n})}\frac{(\widetilde{B}_{m})_{n}\overline{p}_{m,i}}{r_{m}^{\lambda+\epsilon-1}\theta(r_{m})}-\sum_{j=0}^{1}{(\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{j+3})(r_{m}X_{n})}\frac{(\widetilde{B}_{m})_{n}\overline{p}_{m,j}}{r_{m}^{\lambda+\epsilon-1}\theta(r_{m})}

\displaystyle\quad+{\overline{p}_{m,5}}\left(\frac{r_{m}v_{n}(\partial_{x_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{5})(r_{m}X_{n})}{r_{m}^{\lambda+\epsilon-2}\theta(r_{m})}-\frac{\mathrm{div}_{v}(\widetilde{A}_{m}(\nabla_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{5})(r_{m}X_{n}))}{r_{m}^{\lambda+\epsilon-1}\theta(r_{m})}\right)\eqqcolon\sum_{N=1}^{6}\widetilde{Q}_{m,N}(z),

where we write
X_{n}=(x_{n},v_{n})
and
r_{m}X_{n}=(r_{m}^{3}x_{n},r_{m}v_{n})
, and

\displaystyle c_{m,i}\coloneqq\partial_{t}p_{m,i},\quad\widetilde{P}_{m,i}(z)\coloneqq(\nabla_{v}p_{m,i})(z_{0,m}\circ S_{r_{m}}z),\quad\overline{p}_{m,i}(z)\coloneqq p_{m,i}(z_{0,m}\circ S_{r_{m}}z).

Before estimating the terms
\widetilde{Q}_{m,N}
, by the homogeneity of the functions
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{k}
, we note

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k}(r_{m}X_{n})=r_{m}^{\alpha_{k}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k}(X_{n}),\,(\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k})(r_{m}X_{n})=r_{m}^{-1+\alpha_{k}}(\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k})(X_{n}),

\displaystyle r_{m}v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k}(r_{m}X_{n})=r_{m}^{-2+\alpha_{k}}v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k}(X_{n})

with
\alpha_{i}=\frac{1}{2}
,
\alpha_{j+3}=2
for
i\in\{0,1,2\},j\in\{0,1\}
and
\alpha_{5}=\frac{5}{2}
.

•

The case
N=1,2
. Using (
5.21
), (
5.27
), and (
5.25
), we get

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{m,1}+\widetilde{Q}_{m,2}=\sum_{i=0}^{4}\left({\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{i}F_{m,0,i}-\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{i}F_{m,1,i}\right),

where for any
R>0
,

\displaystyle[F_{m,0,i}]_{C^{\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{5}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}+[F_{m,1,i}]_{C^{\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{3}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}+\|F_{m,0,j+3}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}+[F_{m,1,j+3}]_{C^{\lambda+\epsilon-3}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}\leq\frac{c(R)C_{m}}{\theta(r_{m})},

which goes to 0, for
i\in\{0,1,2\}
and
j\in\{0,1\}
.
In particular, by (
5.29
), we get

(5.30)

\displaystyle F_{m,1,2}(0)=0.

•

The case
N=3,4,5
.
Using that the functions
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{k}
solve a translation-invariant equation (see (
4.15
)) and the following observation

(5.31)

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}_{v}(\widetilde{A}_{m}\overline{p}_{m,i}(\nabla_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{i})(r_{m}X_{n}))=\mathrm{div}_{v}((\widetilde{A}_{m}-I)\overline{p}_{m,i}(\nabla_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{i})(r_{m}X_{n}))+\mathrm{div}_{v}(\overline{p}_{m,i}(\nabla_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{i})(r_{m}X_{n})),

we derive when
\lambda+\epsilon<\frac{3}{2}
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{m,3}+\widetilde{Q}_{m,4}+\widetilde{Q}_{m,5}=-\mathrm{div}_{v}(G_{m,0,0}\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0})+F_{m,1,0}\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0}

with

\displaystyle\|G_{m,0,0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}+\|F_{m,1,0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}\leq\frac{c(R)C_{m}}{\theta(r_{m})}\to 0,

where we used that
\overline{p}_{m,0}
is constant and
\overline{p}_{m,k}=0
for any
k\geq 1
in that case.

Moreover, when
\lambda+\epsilon>\frac{3}{2}
, after several elementary computations we can write

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{m,3}+\widetilde{Q}_{m,4}+\widetilde{Q}_{m,5}=\sum_{k=0}^{4}F_{m,2,k}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k}+\sum_{k=0}^{4}F_{m,1,k}\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k},

where

(5.32)

\displaystyle[F_{m,l,i}]_{C^{\lambda+\epsilon-(2-l)-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}+[F_{m,l,j+3}]_{C^{\lambda+\epsilon-(2-l)-2}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}\leq\frac{c(R)C_{m}}{C_{v_{0,m}}\theta(r_{m})}\to 0

for
l\in\{1,2\}
,
i\in\{0,1,2\}
, and
j\in\{0,1\}
. Moreover, in light of (
5.29
) together with the fact that
D(fg)(0)=0
whenever
f(0)=g(0)=0
, we observe that for any
k\in\{0,1,2,3,4\}
,

(5.33)

\displaystyle F_{m,1,2}(0)=F_{m,2,k}(0)=DF_{m,2,1}(0)=DF_{m,2,2}(0)=DF_{m,2,4}(0)=D^{2}F_{m,2,2}(0)=0.

•

The case
N=6
. Similarly, by (
5.31
) and the fact that
\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}\phi_{1}=v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}\phi_{1}=c_{0}v_{n}\phi_{0}
for some constant
c_{0}
by (
C.40
), we get

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{m,6}=F_{m,0,5}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0}}(X_{n})-F_{m,1,5}\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1},

where

(5.34)

\displaystyle[F_{m,0,5}]_{C^{\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{5}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}+\|F_{m,1,5}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}\leq\frac{c(R)c_{m}}{\theta(r_{m})}\to 0.

Therefore, combining all the estimates for the terms
\widetilde{Q}_{m,N}
together with the fact that

(5.35)

\displaystyle[\widetilde{F}_{m}]_{C^{\lambda-2+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}+\|\widetilde{g}_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}\leq\frac{c(R)}{\theta(r_{m})}\to 0,

which can be seen from the same arguments as in
[
35
, Lemma 4.1]
by using (
5.21
), we can apply
subsection 5.1
and
subsection 5.1
when
\lambda+\epsilon<\frac{3}{2}
and
\lambda+\epsilon>\frac{3}{2}
, respectively, to get that

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{f}_{m}\|_{C_{\ell}^{\beta}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}\leq c(R).

In addition, provided that
z_{0}
is such that
\mathcal{Q}_{1}(z_{0})\Subset\mathcal{H}_{1/r_{m}}
, by the interior regularity given in
subsection 2.2
together with a rescaled version of
subsection 5.1
, we deduce

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{f}_{m}\|_{C_{\ell}^{1,\epsilon}(\mathcal{Q}_{1}(z_{0}))}\leq c(\lambda,\epsilon,z_{0}),

where the constant
c
is independent of
m
. Therefore, we get

(5.36)

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{m}\to\widetilde{f}_{\infty}\quad\text{in }C_{\ell}^{1,\epsilon}(\mathcal{Q}_{1}(z_{0}))

for any
\mathcal{Q}_{1}(z_{0})\Subset\{x_{n}>0\}
.
Moreover, by
[
36
, Lemma 5.9]
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{H}_{R/2}}|\nabla_{v}(\widetilde{f}_{m}-\max_{\mathcal{H}_{R/2}}\widetilde{g}_{m})_{+}|^{2}+\int_{\mathcal{H}_{R/2}}|\nabla_{v}(\widetilde{f}_{m}-\min_{\mathcal{H}_{R/2}}\widetilde{g}_{m})_{-}|^{2}\leq c(R,\lambda,\epsilon).

Since
\nabla_{v}\widetilde{f}_{m}\to\nabla_{v}\widetilde{f}_{\infty}
pointwise (see (
5.36
)) and
\min_{\mathcal{H}_{R/2}}\widetilde{g}_{m}\to 0
,
\max_{\mathcal{H}_{R/2}}\widetilde{g}_{m}\to 0
(see (
5.35
)), by Fatou’s lemma, we have

(5.37)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{H}_{R}}|\nabla_{v}\widetilde{f}_{\infty}|^{2}=\int_{\mathcal{H}_{R}}|\nabla_{v}(\widetilde{f}_{\infty})_{+}|^{2}+|\nabla_{v}(\widetilde{f}_{\infty})_{-}|^{2}\leq c(R,\lambda,\epsilon).

We are now ready to apply
Appendix D
in order to derive that
\widetilde{f}_{\infty}
is a weak solution to

(5.38)

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})\widetilde{f}_{\infty}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(\widetilde{A}_{\infty}\nabla_{v}\widetilde{f}_{\infty})&=\widetilde{p}_{\infty}+\widetilde{Q}_{\infty}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathbb{R}\times\{x_{n}>0\}\times\mathbb{R}$},\\
\widetilde{f}_{\infty}&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\}$},\\
|\widetilde{f}_{\infty}(z)|&\leq c(1+|z|)^{\lambda+\epsilon}&&,\end{aligned}\right.

where
\widetilde{p}_{\infty}\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2}
and by (
5.30
) and (
5.33
), we also know

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{i=0}^{2}{p}_{\infty,0,i}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{i}+\sum_{k=0}^{4}p_{\infty,1,k}\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k}+\sum_{k=0,1,3}{p}_{\infty,2,k}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k},

with

(5.39)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle p}_{\infty,0,i}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{5}{2}]},\,p_{\infty,1,i}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{3}{2}]},\,p_{1,j+3}\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-3},\,p_{\infty,2,i}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}]},\,p_{2,3}\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2},

\displaystyle p_{\infty,1,2}(0)={p}_{\infty,2,0}(0)={p}_{\infty,2,1}(0)={p}_{\infty,2,3}(0)=Dp_{\infty,2,1}(0)=0

for
i\in\{0,1,2\}
and
j\in\{0,1\}
. Here, we have also used
subsection 5.1
to prove that for all functions
F_{m,l,k}
it holds
F_{m,l,k}\to p_{\infty,l,k}
locally uniformly. Moreover, we used that
F_{m,2,2},F_{m,2,4}\to 0
by (
5.32
) and (
5.33
), and that
F_{m,1,5}\to 0
by (
5.34
).

By
subsection 4.2
, we get
\widetilde{f}_{\infty}=P_{\infty}
for some
P_{\infty}\in\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\epsilon}
. However we observe from (
5.22
) after a change of variables that

\displaystyle\lim_{m\to\infty}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}|\widetilde{f}_{m}|^{2}=\lim_{m\to\infty}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}\widetilde{f}_{m}P_{\infty}=0.

This contradicts the fact that
\|\widetilde{f}_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1})}\geq 1/2
, which is an immediate consequence of (
5.23
) and the definition of
\widetilde{f}_{m}
. This completes the proof.
∎

6.
Optimal boundary regularity with in-flow condition

In this section, using
section 5
, we derive several fine boundary regularity results for kinetic equations with in-flow condition.

First, we provide the boundary regularity result in
\gamma_{-}
and
\gamma_{+}
, away from
\gamma_{0}
, which was essentially already proved in
[
35
]
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,
z_{0}\in\gamma_{+}\cup\gamma_{-}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{2R}(z_{0})\cap\gamma_{0}=\emptyset
with
R\leq 1
. Let
f
be a weak solution to

(6.1)

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}f)&=B\cdot\nabla_{v}f+F&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0})$},\\
f&=g&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0})$}.\end{aligned}\right.

Let
A\in C^{\lambda-1,\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))
,
B\in C^{\lambda-2,\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0});\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
F\in C^{\lambda-2,\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))
. Then there is a polynomial
p_{z_{0}}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+\epsilon]}
such that for any
r\leq R/2

\displaystyle\|f-p_{z_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{\lambda+\epsilon}\Bigg(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{\lambda+\epsilon}([F]_{C^{\lambda-2,\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+[g]_{C^{\lambda,\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))})\Bigg),

where
c=c(n,\lambda,\epsilon,\Lambda,\|A\|_{C^{\lambda-1,\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},\|B\|_{C^{\lambda-2,\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))})
.

Proof.

First note that the regularity at
\gamma_{+}
and
\gamma_{-}
of kinetic equations in non-divergence form was established in
[
35
, Lemma 4.1 and Lemma 4.3]
by using the blow-up argument as in the proof of
section 5
. Therefore, just a few simple modifications of the proof are required in order to deal with divergence operators (see
section 5
).
∎

6.1.
Optimal regularity up to the grazing set

Now we prove the optimal
C^{1/2}
global regularity for solutions with in-flow boundary condition.

Theorem 6.2
.

Let
f
be a weak solution to

(6.2)

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}f)&=B\cdot\nabla_{v}f+F&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0})$},\\
f&=g&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0})$}.\end{aligned}\right.

If
A\in C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))
,
F\in L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))
, and
g\in C^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))
for some
\epsilon>0
, then we have

\displaystyle R^{\frac{1}{2}}[f]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{R/2}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\mathbf{B}(z_{0},R)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathcal{H}}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{2}\|F\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathcal{H}}_{R}(z_{0}))}+{R^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}[g]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}_{R}(z_{0}))}}\Bigg),

where
c=c(n,\epsilon,\Lambda,\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0})})
and we write
\mathbf{B}(z_{0},R)\coloneqq 1+\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}
.

Proof.

By the same scaling argument as in (
5.4
), we may assume
\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}\leq 1
. Now we assume

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathcal{H}}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{2}\|F\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathcal{H}}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}[g]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}_{R}(z_{0}))}\leq 1.

Suppose
\mathcal{Q}_{3R/4}(z_{0})\cap\gamma_{0}\neq\emptyset
, otherwise the results follow from a combination of interior estimates and
section 6
.
First, we want to prove that for any
z_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{R}{4}}(z_{0})
and
r\leq\frac{R}{32}
, there is a constant
c_{z_{1}}
such that

(6.3)

\displaystyle J\coloneqq\|f-c_{z_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1}))}\leq cr^{\frac{1}{2}}

for some constant
c=c(n,\epsilon,\Lambda,\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},c_{B})
.

To do so, we divide the proof into three cases

•

Let
\left(\frac{|v_{1,n}|}{4}\right)^{3}\leq x_{1,n}
. When
x_{1,n}=0
, then using
section 5
, we observe that there are constants
c_{0},c_{1}
such that

\displaystyle\|f-c_{0}-c_{1}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}({z}_{1}))}\leq cr^{\frac{1}{2}}

for any
r\leq\frac{1}{2}
, where
a\coloneqq\sqrt{(A(z_{1}))_{n,n}}
and
c=c(n,\epsilon,\Lambda,\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))})
. Thus, by taking
c_{z_{1}}\coloneqq c_{0}
, we derive

(6.4)

\displaystyle\|f-c_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1}))}

\displaystyle\leq\|f-c_{0}-c_{1}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1}))}+c_{1}\|{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a}-{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a}(z_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1}))}\leq cr^{\frac{1}{2}}

for some constant
c=c(n,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0}))},c_{B})
, where we have used the fact that
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a}(z_{1})=0
,
\|{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a}\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1}))}=\|{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a}\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}({H}_{r})}\leq cr^{\frac{1}{2}}
.

Now we assume
x_{1,n}>0
. Then there are constants
r_{1}\coloneqq\frac{|x_{1,n}|^{\frac{1}{3}}}{8}
,
r_{2}=8r_{1}
and
\widetilde{z}_{1}=(t_{1},x_{1}^{\prime},0,v_{1}^{\prime},0)\in\gamma_{0}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{3R}{4}}(z_{0})
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{2r_{1}}({z}_{1})\cap\gamma=\emptyset,\quad\mathcal{Q}_{2r_{1}}(z_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{r_{2}}(\widetilde{z_{1}})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{H}_{2r_{2}}(\widetilde{z_{1}})\subset\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}).

We further assume
r\leq r_{1}
to see that for any
c_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\|f-c_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1}))}\leq c\left(\frac{r}{r_{1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f-c_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{2r_{1}}(z_{1}))}+cr^{\frac{1}{2}},

where we have used interior regularity results given in
[
30
, Lemma 5.7]
with
G=0
. We now choose
c_{0}\coloneqq c_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}
determined in (
6.4
) to derive that

(6.5)

\displaystyle\|f-c_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1}))}\leq c\left(\frac{r}{r_{1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}r_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq cr^{\frac{1}{2}}.

On the other hand, if
r\geq r_{1}
, then we have
\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{8r}(\widetilde{z}_{1})
and

(6.6)

\displaystyle\|f-c_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1}))}\leq\|f-c_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{8r}(\widetilde{z}_{1}))}\leq cr^{\frac{1}{2}},

where
c_{0}\coloneqq c_{\widetilde{z}_{1},4r}
is determined in (
6.4
). Therefore, we have proved (
6.3
) when
\left(\frac{|v_{1,n}|}{4}\right)^{3}\leq x_{1,n}
.

•

Let
\left(\frac{v_{1,n}}{4}\right)^{3}\geq x_{1,n}
. When
v_{1,n}=0
, (
6.3
) follows from the previous case, so that we may assume
v_{1,n}>0
. If
r\geq\frac{v_{1,n}}{4}
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{8r}(\widetilde{z}_{1}),

where
\widetilde{z}_{1}\coloneqq(t_{1},x_{1}^{\prime},0,v_{1}^{\prime},0)
. Thus, we get the desired estimate as in (
6.6
). Now suppose
r\leq\frac{v_{1,n}}{4}
. When
r\leq\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{x_{1,n}}{v_{1,n}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\eqqcolon r_{1}
, then we observe

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{r_{1}}(z_{1})\cap\gamma=\emptyset,\quad\mathcal{H}_{2r_{1}}(z_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{8r_{1}}(\widehat{z}_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\frac{v_{1,n}}{4}}(\widehat{z}_{1}),\quad\mathcal{H}_{\frac{v_{1,n}}{4}}(\widehat{z}_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{v_{1,n}}(\widetilde{z}_{1}),

where we write

\displaystyle\widehat{z}_{1}\coloneqq\left(t_{1}-\frac{x_{1,n}}{v_{1,n}},x_{1}^{\prime}-\frac{x_{1,n}}{v_{1,n}}v_{1}^{\prime},0,v_{1}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\widetilde{z}_{1}\coloneqq\left(t_{1}-\frac{x_{1,n}}{v_{1,n}},x_{1}^{\prime}-\frac{x_{1,n}}{v_{1,n}}v_{1}^{\prime},0,v_{1}^{\prime},0\right).

Note that
\widehat{z}_{1}=z_{1}\circ(-\frac{x_{1,n}}{v_{1,n}},0,0)
is a natural projection of
z_{1}
to
\gamma_{-}
in the kinetic geometry, and similarly
\widetilde{z}_{1}=\widehat{z}_{1}\circ(0,0,-v_{1,n})
is the projection of
\widehat{z}_{1}
to
\gamma_{0}
.

By
section 6
, there is a constant
c_{1}
such that

(6.7)

\displaystyle\|f-c_{0}-c_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{8r_{1}}(\widehat{z}_{1}))}\leq c\left(\left(\frac{r_{1}}{v_{1,n}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f-c_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\frac{v_{1,n}}{4}}(\widehat{z}_{1}))}+r_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right),

where the constant
c_{0}=c_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}
is determined as in (
6.5
). Therefore, we have

(6.8)

\displaystyle\|f-c_{0}-c_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{8r_{1}}(\widehat{z}_{1}))}\leq cr_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

On the other hand, by
[
30
]
, there is a constant
c_{2}
such that

(6.9)

\displaystyle\|f-c_{0}-c_{1}-c_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1}))}\leq c\left(\left(\frac{r}{r_{1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f-c_{0}-c_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{1}}({z}_{1}))}+r^{\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Thus, combining two estimates (
6.7
) and (
6.9
) leads to the desired estimate if
r\leq r_{1}
. On the other hand, when
r>r_{1}
, then
\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{4r}(\widehat{z}_{1})
. Therefore, by (
6.7
) and (
6.8
) with
r_{1}
replaced by
r
, we get

\displaystyle\|f-c_{0}-c_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}({z}_{1}))}\leq\|f-c_{0}-c_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{4r}(\widehat{z}_{1}))}\leq cr^{\frac{1}{2}},

which completes the proof of (
6.3
) when
\left(\frac{v_{1,n}}{4}\right)^{3}\geq x_{1,n}
.

•

Let
\left(\frac{-v_{1,n}}{4}\right)^{3}\geq x_{1,n}
. Similarly, we may assume
v_{1,n}<0
and
r\leq v_{1,n}/4
. Now using
section 6
and interior regularity results by
[
30
]
, we derive (
6.3
) when
\left(\frac{-v_{1,n}}{4}\right)^{3}\geq x_{1,n}
, as in (
6.7
)-(
6.9
).

Therefore, we have proved (
6.3
), which completes the proof.

∎

6.2.
Higher order asymptotics in
\mathcal{R}^{0}\cup\mathcal{R}^{+}

Next we prove the Lipschitz estimate of the function
f/{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}
, where
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}\eqsim\phi_{0}
.
To this end, for any
z_{0}\in\gamma_{0}
with
(x_{0})_{n}=(v_{0})_{n}=0
and
M\geq 1
, let us define

(6.10)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{0}_{\rho,M}(z_{0})\coloneqq\left\{z=(t,x^{\prime},x_{n},v^{\prime},v_{n})\in\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{0})\,:\,\frac{v_{n}^{3}}{x_{n}}\leq M^{3}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{+}_{\rho}(z_{0})\coloneqq\left\{z=(t,x^{\prime},x_{n},v^{\prime},v_{n})\in\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{0})\,:\,v_{n}<0\right\}.

Theorem 6.3
.

Let
M\geq 1
and let
f
be a weak solution to

(6.11)

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}f)&=B\cdot\nabla_{v}f+F&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0})$},\\
f&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cup\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0})$},\end{aligned}\right.

where
(x_{0})_{n}=(v_{0})_{n}=0
and
R\leq 1
.
If
A\in C^{1,\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))
,
F\in L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))
,
B\in C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))
for some
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, then for any
z,{z^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{H}^{0}_{\frac{R}{2},M}(z_{0})\cap\mathcal{H}_{\frac{R}{2}}^{+}(z_{0})
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{f}{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a}}(z)-\frac{f}{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a^{\prime}}}(z^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\leq c\frac{|z-z^{\prime}|}{R}\Bigg(\|R^{-\frac{1}{2}}f\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathcal{H}}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{\frac{3}{2}}\|F\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathcal{H}}_{R}(z_{0}))}\Bigg)

for some constant
c=c(n,\epsilon,\Lambda,\|A\|_{C^{1,\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},\|B\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},M)
, where
a\coloneqq\sqrt{(A(z))_{n,n}}
and
a^{\prime}\coloneqq\sqrt{(A(z^{\prime}))_{n,n}}
.

Proof.

We may assume
R=1
and

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathcal{H}}_{1}(z_{0}))}+\|F\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathcal{H}}_{1}(z_{0}))}\leq 1.

In addition, to simplify the notation, we omit the dependence of the constant when it depends only on
n,\epsilon,\Lambda,\|A\|_{C^{1,\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0}))},\|B\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0}))},M
. Furthermore, we write

(6.12)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})\coloneqq\mathcal{H}^{0}_{\frac{1}{2},M}(z_{0})\cap\mathcal{H}_{\frac{1}{2}}^{+}(z_{0}).

First, we show that for each
z_{1}\in\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})
and
\rho\leq\frac{1}{16}
, there is a constant
c_{z_{1}}
such that

(6.13)

\displaystyle J\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})}\left|\frac{f}{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a_{1}}}(z)-c_{z_{1}}\right|\,dz\leq c\rho,

where
a_{1}\coloneqq\sqrt{(A(z_{1})_{n,n})}
.
Note that for any
z_{1}\in\gamma_{0}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{5}{8}}(z_{0})
, by
section 5
, there are constants
c_{0},c_{1},c_{2}
such that for any
\rho<\frac{1}{32}
,

(6.14)

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})}|f-(c_{0}+\mathbf{c}_{1}\cdot(v-v_{1})){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a_{1}}-\mathbf{c}_{2}\cdot(v-v_{1}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1,a_{1}}|\leq c\rho^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}

for some
i,j\in\{1,2,\ldots,n\}
, where
\mathbf{c}_{1},\mathbf{c}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and we have also used that
\mathcal{H}_{\frac{1}{32}}(z_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0})
.

To this end, we split the proof into three cases.

•

Let
z_{1}\in\gamma_{0}
, i.e.,
z_{1}=(t_{1},x_{1}^{\prime},0,v_{1}^{\prime},0)
. Take
z\in\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})
and
c_{z_{1}}\coloneqq c_{0}
, where the constant
c_{0}
is given in (
6.14
). Then by the fact that
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,a_{1}}}(z)\geq\frac{1}{c}|x_{n}|^{\frac{1}{6}}
in
\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})
, by (
C.36
) and (
6.10
), we have

(6.15)

\displaystyle J

\displaystyle={\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})}|{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a_{1}}(z)|^{-1}\left|f(z)-c_{0}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a_{1}}(z)\right|\,dz

\displaystyle\leq c{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})}|x_{n}|^{-\frac{1}{6}}\,dz\left(\sup_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})}\left|f(z)-c_{0}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a_{1}}(z)\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq c\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})}\left|f(z)-c_{0}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a_{1}}(z)\right|,

where we have also used the fact that

(6.16)

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})}|x_{n}|^{-\frac{1}{6}}\,dz\leq{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{H_{\rho}}|x_{n}|^{-\frac{1}{6}}\,dx_{n}\,dv_{n}\leq c\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

With the help of (
6.14
) and the fact that
|{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{i,a}|\leq c|z|^{\frac{1}{2}}
, we further estimate the term
J
as

(6.17)

\displaystyle J

\displaystyle\leq c\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})}\Bigg(|f-(c_{0}+\mathbf{c}_{1}\cdot(v-v_{1})){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a_{1}}-\mathbf{c}_{2}\cdot(v_{j}-v_{1}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1,a_{1}}|

\displaystyle+|\mathbf{c}_{1}\cdot(v-v_{1}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a_{1}}-\mathbf{c}_{2}\cdot(v_{j}-v_{1}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1,a_{1}}|\Bigg)\leq c\rho.

Thus, we have proved (
6.13
) when
z_{1}\in\gamma_{0}
.

•

Let
z_{1}\in(\mathcal{H}^{0}_{\frac{1}{2},M}\setminus\gamma_{0})
and
z_{1}=(t_{1},x_{1}^{\prime},x_{1,n},v_{1}^{\prime},v_{1,n})
with
x_{1,n}\neq 0
. By (
6.10
),
z_{1}\notin\gamma_{0}\cup\gamma_{-}
. Next, we have for
\widetilde{z_{1}}\coloneqq(t_{1},x_{1}^{\prime},0,v_{1}^{\prime},0)
and
r_{1}\coloneqq\frac{(x_{1,n})^{\frac{1}{3}}}{8\sqrt{M}}
and
r_{2}=4(x_{1,n})^{\frac{1}{3}}+|v_{1,n}|
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{2r_{1}}(z_{1})\cap\gamma=\emptyset,\quad\mathcal{Q}_{2r_{1}}(z_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{r_{2}}(\widetilde{z_{1}})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{H}_{2r_{2}}(\widetilde{z_{1}})\subset\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0}).

By (
6.14
), there is a constant
c_{i}
such that

(6.18)

\displaystyle\left\|f-(c_{0}+\mathbf{c}_{1}\cdot(v-\widetilde{v}_{1}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,\widetilde{a}}-\mathbf{c}_{2}\cdot(v-\widetilde{v}_{1}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1,\widetilde{a}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(\widetilde{z_{1}}))}\leq cr^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}

for any
r\leq\frac{1}{32}
, where
\widetilde{v_{1}}=(\widetilde{v}_{1,1}\ldots,\widetilde{v}_{1,n})=(v_{1}^{\prime},0)
and
\widetilde{a}\coloneqq\sqrt{(A(\widetilde{z_{1}}))_{n,n}}
. On the other hand, we employ
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a}(x_{n},v_{n})=\phi_{0}(x_{n}/a,v_{n}/a)
and the fundamental theorem of the calculus, (
C.36
), and
A\in C^{1+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0}))
to see that for any
z\in\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(\widetilde{z}_{1})
,

(6.19)

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,a_{1}}(z)-\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,\widetilde{a}}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int_{0}^{1}x_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}\phi_{0}(x_{n}(\xi/a_{1}+(1-\xi)/\widetilde{a}),v_{n}/a_{1})\,d\xi(a_{1}-\widetilde{a})\right|

\displaystyle+c\left|\int_{0}^{1}v_{n}\partial_{v}\phi_{0}(x_{n}/\widetilde{a},v_{n}(\xi/a_{1}+(1-\xi)/\widetilde{a}))\,d\xi(a_{1}-\widetilde{a})\right|

\displaystyle\leq c\|\phi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}({H}_{\rho})}|z_{1}-\widetilde{z}_{1}|.

To proceed further, first we consider the case
\rho\geq r_{1}
. In this case, we have
\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\rho_{0}}(\widetilde{z}_{1})
with
\rho_{0}\coloneqq 16M^{\frac{3}{2}}\rho
and

\displaystyle J\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})}\left|\frac{f}{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a_{1}}}(z)-c_{z_{1}}\right|\,dz\leq c\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho}({z}_{1})}|f(z)-c_{z_{1}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,a_{1}}}(z)|,

where we have used (
6.16
). By taking
c_{z_{1}}=c_{0}
and following the same lines as in (
6.17
) and (
6.19
), we have

(6.20)

\displaystyle J\leq c\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho_{0}}(\widetilde{z}_{1})}|f(z)-c_{0}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,\widetilde{a}}}(z)|+\sup_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho_{0}}(\widetilde{z}_{1})}|{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,a_{1}}}(z)-{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,\widetilde{a}}}(z)|\right)\leq c\rho.

Thus, we now assume
\rho\leq r_{1}
. We fix
c_{z_{1}}\coloneqq\frac{f(z_{1})}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,a_{1}}(z_{1})}
to observe

(6.21)

\displaystyle J

\displaystyle\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})}\left|\frac{f}{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a_{1}}}(z)-c_{z_{1}}\right|\,dz

\displaystyle={\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})}\left|\frac{f(z)-c_{0}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a_{1}}(z)}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,a_{1}}(z)}-\frac{f(z_{1})-c_{0}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,a_{1}}(z_{1})}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,a_{1}}(z_{1})}\right|\,dz

\displaystyle\leq c\rho\left[\frac{f-c_{0}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a_{1}}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,a_{1}}}\right]_{C^{0,1}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\rho[f-c_{0}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,a_{1}}]_{C^{0,1}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0}))}\|\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,a_{1}}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle+c\rho[\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,a_{1}}^{-1}]_{C^{0,1}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0}))}\|f-c_{0}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,a_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0}))}\eqqcolon J_{1}+J_{2},

where we have used the product rule of the Hölder space for the last inequality.
Next, we observe that
\mathcal{Q}_{2r_{1}}(z_{1})\cap\gamma_{-}=\emptyset
and
\widetilde{f}(z)\coloneqq f(z)-c_{0}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,{a}_{1}}(z)
solves

(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})\widetilde{f}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}\widetilde{f})=B\cdot\nabla_{v}\widetilde{f}+\widetilde{F}\quad\text{in }\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}(z_{1}),

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}

\displaystyle=F+c_{0}\left(v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,{a}_{1}}-\partial_{v_{i}}A_{i,n}\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,{a}_{1}}-B_{n}\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,{a}_{1}}+A_{n,n}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,{a}_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle=F-c_{0}\left(\partial_{v_{i}}A_{i,n}\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,{a}_{1}}+B_{n}\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,{a}_{1}}+(A_{n,n}({z}_{1})-A_{n,n}(z))\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,{a}_{1}}\right),

which follows from the fact that
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,{a}_{1}}
solves the equation

\displaystyle(v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}}-{a}_{1}^{2}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}})f=0.

Therefore, by
subsection 2.2
, we have

(6.22)

\displaystyle{}_{C^{0,1}(\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}(z_{1}))}

\displaystyle\leq cr_{1}^{-1}(\|\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}(z_{1}))}+r_{1}^{2}\|\widetilde{F}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}(z_{1}))})

\displaystyle\leq cr_{1}^{-1}(\|\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}(z_{1}))}+r_{1}^{\frac{3}{2}}),

where we have used the fact that for any
z\in\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}(z_{1})
,

(6.23)

\displaystyle\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,{a}_{1}}(z)\leq c|X_{n}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\leq cr_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}},\quad|A_{n,n}(z_{1})-A_{n,n}(z)||\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,{a}_{1}}|\leq c|z_{1}-z||X_{n}|^{-\frac{3}{2}}\leq cr_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}

with
X_{n}=(x_{n},v_{n})
, which follows from (
C.36
).

Thus, using (
6.19
) with
\rho
replaced by
2r_{2}
and the fact that
\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}(z_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{2_{r}2}(\widetilde{z}_{1})
and
|v_{1,n}|^{3}\leq Mx_{1,n}
, we get

(6.24)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}(z_{1}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\|f-c_{0}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,\widetilde{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}(z_{1}))}+\|\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,a_{1}}-\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,\widetilde{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}(z_{1}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\|f-c_{0}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,\widetilde{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}(z_{1}))}+r_{1}^{\frac{3}{2}}\right).

Now by (
6.18
) together with the fact that
\mathcal{Q}_{2r_{1}}(z_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{r_{2}}(\widetilde{z_{1}})
and
|v_{1,n}|^{3}\leq Mx_{1,n}
, we get

(6.25)

\displaystyle\|f-c_{0}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,\widetilde{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{1}}(z_{1}))}

\displaystyle\leq\|f-(c_{0}+\mathbf{c}_{2}\cdot(v-\widetilde{v}_{1})){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,\widetilde{a}}-\mathbf{c}_{3}\cdot(v-\widetilde{v}_{1}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1,\widetilde{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{2}}(\widetilde{z_{1}}))}

\displaystyle+\|\mathbf{c}_{2}\cdot(v-\widetilde{v}_{1})){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,\widetilde{a}}-\mathbf{c}_{3}\cdot(v-\widetilde{v}_{1}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1,\widetilde{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{2}}(\widetilde{z_{1}}))}\leq cr_{1}^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Combining (
6.22
), (
6.24
), (
6.25
) and

(6.26)

\|\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,a_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0}))}\geq\|\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,a_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{1}}(z_{1})\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0}))}\geq c^{-1}{r_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

leads to

\displaystyle J_{1}\leq c\rho.

On the other hand, for the estimate of
J_{2}
, in light of (
C.36
), (
6.19
) and (
6.25
), we deduce

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c\rho}{r_{1}}\sup_{z,z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})}\frac{|\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,{a}_{1}}(z)-\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,{a}_{1}}(z^{\prime})|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}\left(\|f-c_{0}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,\widetilde{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1}))}+\|\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,{a}_{1}}-\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,\widetilde{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c\rho r_{1}^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left(\|f-c_{0}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,\widetilde{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1}))}+r_{1}^{\frac{3}{2}}\right)\leq c\rho.

In particular, we have used the fact that

(6.27)

\displaystyle\sup_{z,z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})}\frac{|\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,{a}_{1}}(z)-\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,{a}_{1}}(z^{\prime})|}{|z-z^{\prime}|}\leq c\sup_{|X_{n}|,|X_{n}^{\prime}|\geq\frac{r_{1}}{16c_{M}}}\frac{|\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0}(X_{n})-\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0}(X_{n}^{\prime})|}{|X_{n}-X_{n}^{\prime}|}\leq cr_{n}^{-\frac{1}{2}}

Since we have proved
J_{1}+J_{2}\leq c\rho
, this implies (
6.13
).

•

Let
z_{1}\in\mathcal{H}^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})
and
z_{1}=(t_{1},x_{1}^{\prime},x_{1,n},v_{1}^{\prime},v_{1,n})
with
v_{1,n}<0
and
x_{1,n}\leq\left(\frac{|v_{1,n}|}{4}\right)^{3}
. Then we have for
\widetilde{z_{1}}\coloneqq(t_{1},x_{1}^{\prime},0,v_{1}^{\prime},0)
,
r_{1}=|v_{1,n}|/2
and
r_{2}\coloneqq 2|v_{1,n}|
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{r_{1}}(z_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{2r_{2}}(\widetilde{z_{1}})\subset\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0}).

By (
6.14
), there is a constant
c_{i}
such that

(6.28)

\displaystyle\left\|f-(c_{0}+\mathbf{c}_{1}\cdot(v-\widetilde{v}_{1}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,\widetilde{a}}-\mathbf{c}_{2}\cdot(v-\widetilde{v}_{1}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{1,\widetilde{a}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(\widetilde{z_{1}}))}\leq cr^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}

for any
r\leq\frac{1}{32}
, where
\widetilde{v_{1}}=(\widetilde{v}_{1,1}\ldots,\widetilde{v}_{1,n})
and
\widetilde{a}\coloneqq\sqrt{(A(\widetilde{z_{1}}))_{n,n}}
. As in (
6.20
), we get the desired estimate (
6.13
) when
\rho\geq r_{1}
, so that we assume
\rho\leq r_{1}
. Combining interior regularity results given in
subsection 2.2
and
section 6
together with a standard covering argument, we have

[\widetilde{f}]_{C^{0,1}(\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}(z_{1}))}\leq cr_{1}^{-1}(\|\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r_{1}}(z_{1}))}+r_{1}^{\frac{3}{2}}),

where we have also used (
6.23
) and
\widetilde{f}\coloneqq f-c_{0}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a_{1}}
. Similarly, we get (
6.26
) and (
6.27
), as for any
z\in\mathcal{H}_{r_{1}}(z_{1})
,
|v_{n}|\geq\frac{|v_{1,n}|}{2}
, and
|\phi_{0}(z)|\eqsim|X_{n}|^{\frac{1}{2}}
on
\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})
. Therefore, we can follow the same arguments as in the estimates of
J_{1}
and
J_{2}
, to derive that the desired estimate (
6.13
) when
z_{1}\in\mathcal{H}^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})
.

Before completing the proof, we note that for any
z_{1},z_{2}\in\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})
,

(6.29)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{f}{\Phi_{a_{1}}}(z_{2})-\frac{f}{\Phi_{a_{2}}}(z_{2})\right|\leq c|f(z_{2})||X_{2}|^{-1}|\Phi_{a_{1}}(z_{2})-\Phi_{a_{2}}(z_{2})|\leq c|f(z_{2})||X_{2}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}|z_{1}-z_{2}|,

where we have used (
6.26
) with
r_{1}=|X_{2}|
and (
6.19
) with
\rho=|X_{2}|
. On the other hand, by
Theorem 6.2
, we derive

(6.30)

\displaystyle|f(z_{2})|=|f(z_{2})-f(\widetilde{z}_{2})|\leq c|z_{2}-\widetilde{z}_{2}|\leq c|X_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}},

where
\widetilde{z}_{2}=(t_{2},x_{2}^{\prime},0,v_{2}^{\prime},0)
and we have also used the fact that
z_{2}\in\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})
for the last inequality. Thus combining (
6.29
) and (
6.30
) with
z_{2}
replaced by
z
yields

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1})\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})}\left|\frac{f}{\Phi_{a}}(z)-\frac{f}{\Phi_{a_{1}}}(z)\right|\,dz\leq c{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1})\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})}|z-z_{1}|\,dz\leq cr,

where
a=\sqrt{A_{n,n}(z)}
.
Therefore, using this and (
6.13
) gives

(6.31)

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1})\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})}\left|\frac{f}{\Phi_{a}}(z)-c_{z_{1}}\right|\,dz\leq cr.

Now by the classical theory of Campanato’s space, we deduce the desired estimate from (
6.31
). This completes the proof.
∎

In order to discuss the sharpness of our result, we briefly explain how to derive
C^{0,1}
estimates of
f/{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0,a}}
when the right-hand is given by divergence type data. To this end, let us consider a weak solution to

(6.32)

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}f)&=B\cdot\nabla_{v}f+F-\mathrm{div}_{v}(G)&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0})$},\\
f&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{1}(z_{0})$},\end{aligned}\right.

where
(x_{0})_{n}=(v_{0})_{n}=0
,
A\in C^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0}))
,
B\in C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0}))
,
F\in L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0}))
,
G\in C^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0}))
. We may assume

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0})}+\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0})}+[G]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0})}\leq 1.

Then by following the same lines as in the proof of
section 5
together with the fact that
-\mathrm{div}_{v}(G)=-\mathrm{div}_{v}(G-G(z_{0}))
, which allows us to use
\|G-G(z_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})}\leq cr^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}[G]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0})}
, we deduce
(
6.17
) with
z_{1}
replaced by
z_{0}
, as the expansion order is less than 2 so that we only use
subsection 5.1
. Furthermore, we know interior
C^{1,\epsilon}
-estimates of the solution to (
6.32
) (see
subsection 2.2
). Therefore, by repeating the argument used in
Theorem 6.3
, we derive that the weak solution
f
to (
6.32
) satisfies for any

z,z^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{f}{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a}}(z)-\frac{f}{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{0,a^{\prime}}}(z^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\leq c{|z-z^{\prime}|}.

Example 6.4
.

Here, we explain the sharpness of
Theorem 1.7
. Let us consider
n=1
and
f\coloneqq\phi_{0}+v^{2}\partial_{v}\phi_{0}
. Then by (
3.93
), we have that
f
is a solution to

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\partial_{x})f-\partial_{vv}f&=-\partial_{v}(-3\phi_{0}+5v\partial_{v}\phi_{0})&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{1}$},\\
f&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{1}$},\end{aligned}\right.

i.e. it solves (
6.32
) with
G=-3\phi_{0}+5v\partial_{v}\phi_{0}
and
B=F=0
.
Moreover, by (
C.19
) and (
C.23
) we have that
\varphi\coloneqq\frac{f}{\phi_{0}}=1+\frac{v^{2}\partial_{v}\phi_{0}}{\phi_{0}}=1+\frac{v^{4}}{3x}\frac{U(-\frac{5}{6},\frac{5}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x})}{U(\frac{1}{6},\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x})}
. Using the homogeneity as in the proof of
[
35
, Proposition 3.4]
, we deduce that
\varphi\in C^{0,1}\setminus C^{1}
. Therefore, in order to get the
C^{0,1}
regularity, we have to assume
G\in C^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}
for any
\epsilon\geq 0
in (
6.32
). In this sense, our result is sharp.

Remark 6.5
.

We now argue that when
A\equiv 1
and
B\equiv 0
in (
6.11
), then the regularity of
f/\phi_{0}
is better than
C^{0,1}
. Let
f
be a solution to

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f-\Delta_{v}f&=F&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0})$},\\
f&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{1}(z_{0})$},\end{aligned}\right.

where
x_{0,n}=v_{0,n}=0
and
F\in C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0}))
. To show this, first, we note from
Theorem 1.10
that we only need two elements
\phi_{0}
and
\psi_{0}
to prove the expansion estimates of order
2+\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon
given in
section 5
. This implies that by repeating the same arguments as in the proof of
section 5
, we deduce

\displaystyle\|f-(c_{0}+\mathbf{c}_{1}\cdot(v-v_{0})+(v-v_{0})^{T}\mathbf{C}_{2}(v-v_{j}))\phi_{0}-c_{3}\psi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{0}))}\leq c\rho^{2+\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}

for some constants
\mathbf{c}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\mathbf{C}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n},c\in\mathbb{R}
,
where we have assumed
\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0}))}+[F]_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0}))}\leq 1
. Therefore, as in the proof of
section 5
with a few modifications and due to the fact that
\psi_{0}
has homogeneity 2, one could show
f/\phi_{0}\in C^{\frac{3}{2}}(\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0}))
(see (
6.12
) for the definition of the set
\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0})
). Clearly, this result is sharp, since
f=\psi_{0}
is a solution.

On the other hand, when
F=0
, there is no
\psi_{0}
in the expansion, so that we can deduce

\displaystyle\|f-(c_{0}+\mathbf{c}_{1}\cdot(v-v_{0})+(v-v_{0})^{T}\mathbf{C}_{2}(v-v_{j}))\phi_{0}-c_{3}\partial_{v_{n}}\phi_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{0}))}\leq c\rho^{2+\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}.

Since
\partial_{v_{n}}\phi_{1}
has homogeneity
\frac{5}{2}
, one could prove
f/\phi_{0}\in C^{1,1}(\overline{\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{1}{2}}(z_{0}))
. Clearly, also this result is sharp, since
f=cv_{1}^{2}\phi_{0}-\partial_{v_{n}}\phi_{1}
is a solution for some
c\in\mathbb{R}
.

6.3.
Higher regularity in
\mathcal{R}^{-}

Now we prove the optimal regularity near
\gamma_{0}\cup\gamma_{-}
.
More precisely, for any
\epsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{3})
and
z_{0}
with
(x_{0})_{n}=(v_{0})_{n}=0
, we define the set
\mathcal{H}^{-}_{\rho,\epsilon}(z_{0})
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{-}_{\rho,\epsilon}(z_{0})\coloneqq\left\{(t,x^{\prime},x_{n},v^{\prime},v_{n})\in\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{0})\mathcal{\,}:\,v_{n}^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\geq x_{n}\right\},

and we show that the following fine regularity result in this set. The main point here is that the expansion from
section 5
is improved in the sense that the complicated function in
\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,z_{0},a}
can be replaced by a polynomial since all the elements in the expansion are sufficiently smooth themselves, in
\mathcal{H}^{-}_{\rho,\epsilon}(z_{0})
.

Theorem 6.6
.

Let
f
be a weak solution to

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}f)&=B\cdot\nabla_{v}f+F&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0})$},\\
f&=g&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0})$}\end{aligned}\right.

with
(x_{0})_{n}=(v_{0})_{n}=0
.
Let
A\in C^{2,\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))
,
F\in C^{1,\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))
,
B\in C^{1,\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))
, and
g\in C^{2,1}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))
. For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and
z_{1}\in\mathcal{H}^{-}_{\frac{R}{2},\epsilon}(z_{0})
, whenever
\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}^{-}_{\frac{R}{2},\epsilon}(z_{0})
, then there is a polynomial
p_{z_{1}}
such that

\displaystyle\|f-p_{z_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1}))}\leq c\left(\frac{\rho}{R}\right)^{3(1-\epsilon)}\left(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{3}[F]_{C^{2,\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{3}[g]_{C^{2,1}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))}\right),

for some constant
c=c(n,\epsilon,\Lambda,\|A\|_{C^{2,\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},\|B\|_{C^{1,\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},|v_{0}|)
.

Proof.

We may assume
R=1
and

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0}))}+[F]_{C^{2,\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0}))}+[g]_{C^{3}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{1}(z_{0}))}\leq 1.

For simplicity of notation, we omit the dependence of the constant when it depends only on the data such as
n,\epsilon,\Lambda,\|A\|_{C^{2,\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0}))},\|B\|_{C^{1,\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0}))}
.

We fix
z_{1}=(t_{1},x_{1}^{\prime},x_{1,n},v_{1}^{\prime},v_{1,n})\in\mathcal{H}^{-}_{\frac{1}{2},\epsilon}(z_{0})
, which implies
x_{1,n}\leq v_{1,n}^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}}
, and assume
\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}^{-}_{\frac{1}{2},\epsilon}(z_{0})
.

First, write
r_{1}\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{x_{1,n}}{v_{1,n}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
to observe that

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{r_{1}}(z_{1})\cap\gamma=\emptyset,\quad\mathcal{H}_{2r_{1}}(z_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{8r_{1}}(\widehat{z}_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\frac{v_{1,n}}{4}}(\widehat{z}_{1}),\quad\mathcal{H}_{\frac{v_{1,n}}{4}}(\widehat{z}_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{v_{1,n}}(\widetilde{z}_{1}),

where we write

\displaystyle\widehat{z}_{1}\coloneqq\left(t_{1}-\frac{x_{1,n}}{v_{1,n}},x_{1}^{\prime}-\frac{x_{1,n}}{v_{1,n}}v_{1}^{\prime},0,v_{1}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\widetilde{z}_{1}\coloneqq\left(t_{1}-\frac{x_{1,n}}{v_{1,n}},x_{1}^{\prime}-\frac{x_{1,n}}{v_{1,n}}v_{1}^{\prime},0,v_{1}^{\prime},0\right).

By
section 5
, there is
P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}\in\mathcal{\overline{T}}_{2,(1-\frac{3\epsilon}{2}),\widetilde{z}_{1},\widetilde{a}_{1}}
with
\widetilde{a}_{1}\coloneqq\sqrt{(A(\widetilde{z}_{1}))_{n,n}}
such that for any
r\leq\frac{1}{32}

(6.33)

\displaystyle\|f-P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(\widetilde{z}_{1}))}\leq cr^{3(1-\frac{\epsilon}{2})}.

Indeed,
P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}
is of the form

\displaystyle P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}\coloneqq p_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}+\sum_{i=0}^{2}p_{i}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\widetilde{a}_{1},i}+b_{3}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\widetilde{a}_{1},3}+b_{5}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\widetilde{a}_{1},5},

where
p_{\widetilde{z}_{1}},p_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}
and
p_{1}(\widetilde{z}_{1})=p_{2}(\widetilde{z}_{1})=Dp_{2}(\widetilde{z}_{1})=0
.

Next, let us choose a constant
\delta\in(\frac{\epsilon}{3},1)
, which will be determined later (see (
6.42
)). Then we observe for
\widehat{r}_{1}\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}({v_{1,n}})^{\frac{1}{2}(\frac{3}{\delta}-1)}
, that there is a constant small
c_{\delta}=c_{\delta}(\delta)
such that when
v_{1,n}\leq c_{\delta}
, then

(6.34)

\displaystyle v_{1,n}-4\widehat{r}_{1}>\frac{v_{1,n}}{2}.

We now assume (
6.34
) so that we have

(6.35)

\displaystyle(4\widehat{r}_{1})^{2}v_{1,n}+(4\widehat{r}_{1})^{3}\leq\left(16(v_{1,n}-\widehat{r}_{1})\right)^{\frac{3}{\delta}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{H}_{{4\widehat{r}_{1}}}(\widehat{z}_{1})\subset\{z\in\mathcal{R}^{-}\,:\,x_{n}\leq(16v_{n})^{\frac{3}{\delta}}\}.

Then, by (
C.39
) together with (
6.35
), we get

(6.36)

\displaystyle\|D^{2}(\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\widetilde{a}_{1},i})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{4\widehat{r}_{1}}(\widehat{z}_{1}))}\leq c

for any
i\in\{0,1,2,5\}
. It remains to estimate
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\widetilde{a}_{1},i}
. To do so, we use that since
\psi_{0}
is a solution to

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})\psi_{0}-\Delta_{v}\psi_{0}&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathbb{R}\times\{x_{n}>0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}$},\\
\psi_{0}&=c_{0}v_{n}^{2}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathbb{R}\times\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\}$},\end{aligned}\right.

by
section 6
, we get for
\delta^{\prime}\coloneqq 1-3\epsilon
,

\displaystyle[\psi_{0}]_{C^{2,\delta^{\prime}}(\mathcal{H}_{8\widehat{r}_{1}}(\widehat{z}_{1}))}\leq[\psi_{0}]_{C^{2,\delta^{\prime}}(\mathcal{H}_{\frac{v_{1,n}}{4}}(\widehat{z}_{1}))}\leq c({v_{1,n}})^{-(2+\delta^{\prime})}\|\psi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\frac{v_{1,n}}{2}}(\widehat{z}_{1}))}.

Since
\mathcal{H}_{\frac{v_{1,n}}{2}}(\widehat{z}_{1})\subset\{x_{n}\leq(2v_{n})^{3}\}
, using (
C.84
), we have
\|\psi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\frac{v_{1,n}}{2}}(\widehat{z}_{1}))}\leq cv_{1,n}^{2}
. Thus we deduce

\displaystyle[\psi_{0}]_{C^{2,\delta^{\prime}}(\mathcal{H}_{4\widehat{r}_{1}}(\widetilde{z}_{1}))}\leq c(v_{1,n})^{-\delta^{\prime}},

which implies

(6.37)

\displaystyle[{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\widetilde{a}_{1},3}]_{C^{2,\delta^{\prime}}(\mathcal{H}_{4\widehat{r}_{1}}(\widetilde{z}_{1}))}\leq c(v_{1,n})^{-\delta^{\prime}},

Furthermore, we note

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\widetilde{a}_{1},3}}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\widetilde{a}_{1},3}})-B\nabla_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\widetilde{a}_{1},3}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{v_{n}}(A_{n,n}(z)-A_{n,n}(\widetilde{z}_{1}))\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\widetilde{a}_{1},3}}+(A_{n,n}(z)-A_{n,n}(\widetilde{z}_{1}))\partial^{2}_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\widetilde{a}_{1},3}}-B\partial_{v_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\widetilde{a}_{1},3}},

and

\displaystyle\|A_{n,n}(z)-A_{n,n}(\widetilde{z}_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{r}_{1}}(4\widehat{z}_{1}))}\leq\|A_{n,n}(z)-A_{n,n}(\widetilde{z}_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{v_{1,n}}(\widetilde{z}_{1}))}\leq cv_{1,n},

where we have used the Lipschitz regularity of
A
and
\mathcal{H}_{v_{1,n}}(\widetilde{z}_{1})\supset\mathcal{H}_{8r_{1}}(\widehat{z}_{1})
.
Therefore, using these together with (
6.37
), we have

\displaystyle[(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\widetilde{a}_{1},3}}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\widetilde{a}_{1},3}})-B\nabla_{v}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\widetilde{a}_{1},3}}]_{C^{1-3\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{4\widehat{r}_{1}}(\widehat{z}_{1}))}\leq c(\epsilon),

Thus, with this, (
6.36
) and
\|p_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\frac{1}{32}}(\widetilde{z}_{1}))}\leq c
by (
6.33
), we have

(6.38)

\displaystyle[(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}})-B\nabla_{v}P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}]_{C^{1-3\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{4\widehat{r}_{1}}(\widehat{z}_{1}))}\leq c(\epsilon).

Since
\widetilde{f}\coloneqq f-P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}
satisfies

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})\widetilde{f}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}\widetilde{f})=B\cdot\nabla_{v}\widetilde{f}+F+\widetilde{F}\quad\text{in }\mathcal{H}_{4\widehat{r}_{1}}(\widehat{z}_{1})

with
\widetilde{F}\coloneqq(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}})-B\nabla_{v}P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}
and
\widetilde{f}=g-\widetilde{p}
on
\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{4\widehat{r}_{1}}(\widehat{z}_{1})
for some
\widetilde{p}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}
, by applying
section 6
, there is a polynomial
p_{\widehat{z}_{1}}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}
such that for any
r\leq 4\widehat{r}_{1}
,

(6.39)

\displaystyle\|f-P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}-p_{\widehat{z}_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(\widehat{z}_{1}))}\leq c\left(\left(\frac{r}{\widehat{r}_{1}}\right)^{3-3\epsilon}\|f-P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{4\widehat{r}_{1}}(\widetilde{z}_{1}))}+r^{3-3\epsilon}\right),

where we have also used (
6.38
). This gives

\displaystyle\|p_{\widehat{z}_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{4\widehat{r}_{1}}(\widehat{z}_{1}))}\leq c(\|f-P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{4\widehat{r}_{1}}(\widetilde{z}_{1}))}+r_{1}^{3-3\epsilon}),

which implies

\displaystyle[p_{\widehat{z}_{1}}]_{C^{k,\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{4\widehat{r}_{1}}(\widetilde{z}_{1}))}\leq\frac{c}{(\widehat{r}_{1})^{k+\epsilon}}(\|f-P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{4\widehat{r}_{1}}(\widetilde{z}_{1}))}+\widehat{r_{1}}^{3-3\epsilon}).

On the other hand, we observe that
\widehat{f}=f-P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}-p_{\widehat{z}_{1}}
satisfies

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})\widehat{f}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}\widehat{f})=B\cdot\nabla_{v}\widehat{f}+F+\widetilde{F}+\widehat{G},

where
\widehat{G}=-\partial_{t}p_{\widehat{z}_{1}}+\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}p_{\widehat{z}_{1}})+B\cdot\nabla_{v}p_{\widehat{z}_{1}}
. Thus, by
section 6
, we have for any
\rho\leq r_{1}
,

(6.40)

\displaystyle\|f-P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}-p_{\widehat{z}_{1}}-p_{z_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{\rho}{{r}_{1}}\right)^{3-3\epsilon}\|f-P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}-p_{\widehat{z}_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{1}}(z_{1}))}

\displaystyle+\rho^{3-3\epsilon}\left[1+\frac{c}{(\widehat{r}_{1})^{3-3\epsilon}}\|f-P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{4\widehat{r}_{1}}(z_{1}))}\right],

where we have also used (
6.38
) and
[\widehat{G}]_{C^{1-3\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{1}}(z_{1}))}\leq[\widehat{G}]_{C^{1-3\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{4\widehat{r}_{1}}(\widehat{z}_{1}))}\leq\|p_{\widehat{z}_{1}}\|_{C^{2,1-3\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{4\widehat{r}_{1}}(\widehat{z}_{1}))}
. Thus, combining (
6.39
) with
r=4r_{1}
, (
6.40
) yields

(6.41)

\displaystyle\|f-P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}-p_{\widehat{z}_{1}}-p_{z_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1}))}\leq c\left(\left(\frac{\rho}{\widehat{r}_{1}}\right)^{3-3\epsilon}\|f-P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{4\widehat{r}_{1}}(z_{1}))}+\rho^{3-3\epsilon}\right),

where we have also used the fact that
\mathcal{H}_{r_{1}}(z_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{4{r}_{1}}(\widehat{z}_{1})
. Lastly, combining this, (
6.33
) with
r=v_{1,n}
and the fact that
\mathcal{H}_{4\widehat{r}_{1}}(\widehat{z}_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{4v_{1,n}}(\widetilde{z}_{1})
leads to

\displaystyle\|f-P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}-p_{\widehat{z}_{1}}-p_{z_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\varrho}(z_{1}))}\leq c\left(\left(\frac{\rho}{\widehat{r}_{1}}\right)^{3(1-\epsilon)}v_{1,n}^{3(1-\frac{\epsilon}{2})}+\rho^{3(1-\epsilon)}\right),

where
c=c(\delta)
. Now by taking
\delta=\delta(\epsilon)
very close to 1 so that

(6.42)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{3}{\delta}-1)(3-3\epsilon)+(3-\frac{3}{2}\epsilon)\geq 0,

we have
\widehat{r}_{1}^{-3(1-\epsilon)}v_{1,n}^{3-\frac{3}{2}\epsilon}\leq c
,
as
\widehat{r}_{1}=\frac{1}{2}({v_{1,n}})^{\frac{1}{2}(\frac{3}{\delta}-1)}
. This implies that

\displaystyle\|f-P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}-p_{\widehat{z}_{1}}-p_{z_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\varrho}(z_{1}))}\leq c\rho^{3(1-\epsilon)}.

Now using (
C.39
) and (
C.88
) together with the fact that
\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}^{-}_{1,\epsilon}(z_{0})
, there is the second order Taylor polynomial of
P_{\widetilde{z_{1}}}
,
\widetilde{p}_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}
such that

\displaystyle\|P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}-\widetilde{p}_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1}))}\leq c\rho^{3-3\epsilon}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\|f-\widetilde{p}_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}-p_{\widehat{z}_{1}}-p_{z_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1}))}\leq c\rho^{3(1-\epsilon)},

whenever
v_{1,n}\leq c_{\delta}
by (
6.34
). However, if
v_{1,n}>c_{\delta}
, then by
section 6
, we get the desired estimate by a combination of interior regularity estimates and the boundary estimates at
\gamma_{-}
, as
z_{1}
is away from
\gamma_{0}
and
c_{\delta}>0
only depends on the constant
\epsilon
. More precisely, as in (
6.39
) and (
6.40
) with
P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}=0
and
\widehat{r}_{1}=\frac{v_{1,n}}{8}
, we get (
6.41
) with
P_{\widetilde{z}_{1}}=0
and
\widehat{r}_{1}=\frac{v_{1,n}}{8}
. Then the fact that
v_{1,n}\geq c_{\delta}
yields the desired estimate. This completes the proof.
∎

We end this section with an explicit example verifying the optimality of
Theorem 6.6
(and therefore of
Theorem 1.6
).

Example 6.7
.

Let
n=1
and
f\coloneqq\psi_{0}
. Then we have

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\partial_{x})f-\partial_{vv}f&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{1}$},\\
f&=c_{0}v^{2}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{1}$}.\end{aligned}\right.

By (
C.89
), we have to assume
D\subset\{x\leq v^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\}
to prove
f\in C^{3-3\epsilon}(D)
. This implies that our result given in
Theorem 6.6
is optimal.

7.
Optimal boundary regularity with diffuse reflection condition

In this section, we prove global regularity with diffuse reflection condition in the halfspace
\Omega=\{x_{n}>0\}
. Throughout this section, we denote

(7.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}f(t,x,v)\coloneqq\mathcal{M}(z)g(t,x^{\prime})\coloneqq\mathcal{M}(z)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\cap\{w_{n}<0\}}f(t,x^{\prime},0,w^{\prime},w_{n})(w_{n})_{-}\,dw

and assume that
\mathcal{M}(z)
has fast decay as
|v|\to\infty
and is sufficiently smooth. Later, by flattening the boundary and a covering argument, we can derive global estimates for a general domain. Moreover,
subsection 7.2
shows that the
C^{\frac{1}{2}}
regularity is optimal.

The following is the main result of this section and it is our most general result on diffuse reflection and yields
Theorem 1.1
. We define

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{R,\infty}=(-R^{2},R^{2})\times\big(B_{R^{3}}^{\prime}\times(0,R^{3})\big)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
f
be a weak solution to

(7.2)

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}f)&=B\cdot\nabla_{v}f+F&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty}$},\\
f&=\mathcal{N}f&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\overline{\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty}}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where
\mathcal{N}f
is defined as in (
7.1
). Let us fix
p\geq\frac{1}{2}
and write for any
v_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,
r_{0}\coloneqq\frac{\langle v_{0}\rangle^{-p}}{64}
. Let
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}
, and
M>0
. Then we have the following.

•

Let us fix
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and assume that for some
c_{B},M>0
,

(7.3)

\displaystyle\sup_{z_{0}\in\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty}}\left(\langle v_{0}\rangle^{M}\|\mathcal{M}\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}+\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}+\langle v_{0}\rangle^{-2}\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}\right)\leq c_{B}.

Then there is
M_{0}=M_{0}(n,p)>0
such that if
M>M_{0}
, then for any
z_{0}\in\mathcal{H}_{1,\infty}
, we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\langle v_{0}\rangle(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2r_{0}}(z_{0}))}+r_{0}^{2}\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2r_{0}}(z_{0}))})

\displaystyle\quad+c\langle v_{0}\rangle\|\mathcal{M}\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}\left(\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{q}f(t,x,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty})}+\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{q}F(t,x,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty})}\right),

for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda,c_{B},p)
and
q=q(n,p)
.

•

Let us fix
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}
,
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and assume that for some
c_{B},M>0
,

(7.4)

\displaystyle\sup_{z_{0}\in\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty}}\left(\langle v_{0}\rangle^{M}\|\mathcal{M}\|_{C^{6\lambda+\frac{7}{6}}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}+\|A\|_{C^{6\lambda+1}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}+\langle v_{0}\rangle^{-2}\|B\|_{C^{6\lambda}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(v_{0}))}\right)\leq c_{B}.

Then, there is
M_{1}=M_{1}(n,\lambda,\epsilon,p)
, such that if
M>M_{1}
, then for any
z_{0}\in\mathcal{H}_{1,\infty}

and
\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0})\cap\gamma_{0}=\emptyset
, we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{C^{\lambda,\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq c\left(\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{q}f(t,x,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty})}+\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{q}F(t,x,\cdot)\right\|_{C^{6\lambda}(\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty})}\right),

for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda,\lambda,\epsilon,c_{B},p)
and
q=q(n,\lambda,\epsilon,p)
, where we write

(7.5)

\displaystyle\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{q}F(t,x,\cdot)\right\|_{C^{6\lambda}(\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty})}\coloneqq\sup_{z_{0}\in\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty}}\langle v_{0}\rangle^{q}\|F\|_{C^{6\lambda}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{0})\cap\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty})}.

Note that we assume
p\geq\frac{1}{2}
to ensure that
\mathcal{H}_{4r_{0}}(z_{0})\subset\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty}
for any
z_{0}\in\mathcal{H}_{1,\infty}
