. In addition, we will select the constant
p\geq\frac{1}{2}
in the proof of
Theorem 1.1
given in Section
8
, to satisfy assumptions such as (
7.3
) and (
7.4
) after flattening arguments (see the proof of
Theorem 1.1
for more details).

The key difficulty of the proof is to obtain regularity of the boundary datum
\mathcal{N}f
, which will then allow us to apply known regularity results about in-flow (see
[
35
]
). The following is the main result in this direction.

Proposition 7.2
.

Let us fix
p\geq\frac{1}{2}
and write
r_{0}\coloneqq\frac{\langle v_{0}\rangle^{-p}}{64}
for any
v_{0}\in\mathcal{H}_{1,\infty}
. Let
f
be a weak solution to (
7.2
). Then we have the following:

•

There is
M_{0}=M_{0}(n,p)>0
such that if (
7.3
) holds for some
\epsilon\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
and
M>M_{0}
, then for any
z_{0}\in\mathcal{H}_{1,\infty}
,

(7.6)

\displaystyle[\mathcal{N}f]_{C^{\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq c\left(\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{q}f(t,x,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty})}+\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{q}F(t,x,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty})}\right),

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,c_{B},p,\mathcal{M})
, and
q
depend only on
n
and
\epsilon
.

•

There is
M_{1}=M_{1}(n,\lambda,\epsilon,p)>0
such that that if (
7.4
) holds for some
\lambda\geq 1
,
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, and
M\geq M_{1}
, then for any
z_{0}\in\mathcal{H}_{1,\infty}
,

(7.7)

\displaystyle[\mathcal{N}f]_{C^{\lambda,\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq c\left(\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{q}f(t,x,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty})}+\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{q}F(t,x,\cdot)\right\|_{C^{6\lambda}(\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty})}\right),

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\lambda,\epsilon,c_{B},p,\mathcal{M})
, and
q
depend only on
n,\lambda
, and
\epsilon
.

The regularity assumptions on the data
A,B,F
and
\mathcal{M}
in (
7.7
) are not sharp. In this paper, we focus on proving the
C^{\infty}
-regularity away from
\gamma_{0}
when the given data is smooth. Therefore, we do not proceed to find a sharp relation between the regularity of the solution and the regularity of data.

Since the proof involves a lot of steps, we here briefly explain the strategy of the proof. We will use a difference quotient technique and a bootstrap argument to derive Hölder estimates of

\displaystyle\widehat{g}_{k}\coloneqq\frac{\delta_{h,k}\mathcal{N}f}{|h|^{k}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\widehat{f}_{k}\coloneqq\frac{\delta_{h,k}f}{|h|^{k}}.

See (
7.20
) for the definition of
\delta_{h,k}
. First, we can observe that
L^{\infty}
estimates of
\widehat{f}_{k}
and
C^{1,\epsilon}
estimates of
\widehat{f}_{k}
near
\gamma_{+}
are enough to get uniform bounds of
\widehat{g}_{k}
in Hölder spaces
C^{\frac{4}{3}-\epsilon}
for any
\delta>0
(see (
7.25
) below). Next, we prove
C^{1,\epsilon}
estimates of
\widehat{f}_{k}
away from
\gamma_{+}
in terms of the supremum norm of
\widehat{f}_{k}
(see (
7.30
)). Based on these observations, we will inductively prove
C^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}
estimates of
\widehat{f}_{k}
(see (
7.31
) below).

There are three steps to derive uniform
C^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}
estimates of
\widehat{f}_{k+1}
from the estimates of
\widehat{f}_{k}
.

1.

Using (
A.2
), we get that
\frac{\delta_{h,k+1}f}{|h|^{k+\alpha}}
is bounded for any
\alpha<\frac{1}{6}
. Thus, using this and
C^{2-\epsilon}
estimates, we can derive
\frac{\delta_{h,k+1}\mathcal{N}f}{|h|^{k+\alpha}}\in C^{\frac{7}{6}}
.

2.

Note that
\frac{\delta_{h,k+1}f}{|h|^{k+\alpha}}
satisfies an equation with an unbounded right-hand side, such as for example
-\mathrm{div}_{v}\left(\frac{\delta_{h}A}{|h|^{\alpha}}\nabla_{v}\widehat{f}_{k}\right)
, which prevents us from applying global
C^{\frac{1}{2}}
regularity estimates. Instead, we prove that
\nabla_{v}\widehat{f}_{k}\in L^{q}
for a sufficiently large
q
, by obtaining gradient estimates of
\widehat{f}_{k}
away from
\gamma_{0}
, and combining this with
C^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}
estimates of
\widehat{f}_{k}
at
\gamma_{0}
.

3.

Now we use a perturbation argument together with a higher integrability result of
\nabla_{v}\widehat{f}_{k}
to derive
\frac{\delta_{h,k+1}f}{|h|^{k+\alpha}}\in C^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}
, which implies that
\frac{\delta_{h,k+1}f}{|h|^{k+\alpha}}
is bounded for any
\alpha<\frac{2}{6}
. Now we iterate this procedures 7-times so that
\alpha
becomes 1 and we get uniform
C^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}
estimates of
\widehat{f}_{k+1}
.

Therefore, by (
7.25
), we prove
\widehat{g}_{k}\in C^{\frac{4}{3}-\epsilon}
, which gives
\partial^{k}g\in C^{\frac{4}{3}-\epsilon}
. Now using (
A.1
), we can find a number
k
such that
g\in C^{\lambda,\delta}
. As we mentioned before, we just find a large number
k
, which is not sharp, but is enough to prove
g\in C^{\lambda,\delta}
.

7.1.
Preliminary results: boundary estimates with in-flow

Before we can prove
section 7
, we need to provide several results on regularity estimates away from
\gamma_{0}
. They were basically shown in
[
35
]
(see
[
30
]
for the interior case), but for the application to diffuse reflection, we need to track the explicit dependence of the constants on the drift and include source terms in divergence form of the form
\mathrm{div}_{v}(G)
into our study. Moreover, in
subsection 7.1
we prove
C^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}
estimates up to
\gamma_{0}
for unbounded source terms, thereby refining
Theorem 6.2
.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
f
be a weak solution to

(7.8)

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}f)&=B\cdot\nabla_{v}f-\mathrm{div}_{v}(G)+F&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0})$},\\
f&=g&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0})$}.\end{aligned}\right.

Suppose
\mathcal{H}_{2R}(z_{0})\cap\gamma_{0}=\emptyset
. For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, we have

(7.9)

\displaystyle R\|\nabla_{v}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R/2}(z_{0}))}+R^{1+\epsilon}[\nabla_{v}f]_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R/2}(z_{0}))}+R^{1+\epsilon}[f]_{C^{1,\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R/2}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\mathbf{B}(z_{0},R)^{1+\epsilon}\Bigg(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{1+\epsilon}[G]_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle+R^{2}\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{1+\epsilon}[g]_{C^{1,\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))}\Bigg),

where
\mathbf{B}(z_{0},R)\coloneqq(1+\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))})
and

c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))})
.

Proof.

The explicit dependence of the constant on
B
can be obtained by the same scaling argument as in the proof of (
5.3
). Hence, it suffices to prove (
7.9
), when
\|{B}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}\leq 1
.

In case
G=0
, interior estimates and boundary expansions have been obtained in
[
30
]
and
section 6
in case
G=0
. To treat divergence form source terms, first by
subsection 2.2
, we have

(7.10)

\displaystyle R^{1+\epsilon}[f]_{C^{1,\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R/2}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{\epsilon}[G]_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))})+cR^{2}\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}\right),

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))})
, whenever
\mathcal{H}_{2R}(z_{0})\cap\gamma=\emptyset
. In addition, for any
z_{1}\in\mathcal{H}_{2R}(z_{0})\cap\gamma
, by following the same lines as in the proof of
subsection 5.1
together with the following observation
-\mathrm{div}_{v}(G)=\mathrm{div}_{v}(G-G(z_{0}))
, which allows us to estimate
\|G-G(z_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1}))}\lesssim r^{\epsilon}[G]_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1}))}
, we get that there is a small constant
\alpha=\alpha(n,\Lambda)
such that

(7.11)

\displaystyle r^{\alpha}[f]_{C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{r/2}(z_{1}))}\leq c(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1}))}+r^{\epsilon}[G]_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1}))}+r^{2}\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1}))}+r^{\alpha}[g]_{C^{\alpha}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1}))}),

for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1}))})
, whenever
\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0})
.
In order to prove expansions at boundary points, when
\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0})\cap\gamma_{0}\neq\emptyset
, we use the same arguments as in the proof of
[
35
, Lemma 4.1 and Lemma 4.4]
(see also
section 5
) together with interior
C^{1,\epsilon}
-estimates (
7.10
) and boundary
C^{\alpha}
-estimates (
7.11
), which will be used in order to guarantee convergence of the blow-up sequence as in (
5.36
). It is easy to see that the assumption on
G
has the right scaling and hence guarantees convergence of the blow-up sequence to a suitable limit. Altogether, we get

\displaystyle\|f-p_{z_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{1+\epsilon}\Bigg(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{1+\epsilon}[G]_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle+R^{2}\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{1+\epsilon}[g]_{C^{1,\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))}\Bigg)

for some polynomials
p_{z_{0}}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}
,
which implies the following estimate upon combination with the interior estimates from (
7.10
)

\displaystyle R^{1+\epsilon}[f]_{C^{1+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R/2}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\Bigg(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{\epsilon}[G]_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+R^{2}\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{1+\epsilon}[g]_{C^{1,\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))}\Bigg),

for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))})
. This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 7.4
.

Note that that by repeating the same arguments as in the proof
subsection 7.1
, we derive that whenever
\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0})\cap\gamma_{0}=\emptyset
,

\displaystyle R^{\frac{1}{2}}[f]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{R/2}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\mathbf{B}(z_{0},R)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R\|G\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+R^{2}\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{\frac{1}{2}}[g]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))}\Bigg),

for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda,\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))})
.

Next, we prove Hölder estimates of order
\frac{1}{2}-\delta
near the boundary and up to
\gamma_{0}
.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
R\leq 1
,
z_{0}\in\gamma_{0}
with
x_{0,n}=v_{0,n}=0
and let
f
be a weak solution to

(7.12)

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}f)&=B\cdot\nabla_{v}f-\mathrm{div}_{v}(G)+F&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0})$},\\
f&=g&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0})$},\end{aligned}\right.

where
A\in C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))
for some
\epsilon>0
.
Let us fix
p\geq\frac{1}{2}
,
C_{1}\geq 1
and write
r_{1}\coloneqq\frac{\langle v_{1}\rangle^{-p}}{64}
and
\widehat{r}_{1}\coloneqq\frac{\langle v_{1}\rangle^{-p}}{C_{1}}
for any
z_{1}
. We now fix
\delta<\frac{1}{2}
. Suppose

(7.13)

\displaystyle\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}\leq c_{B}\langle v_{0}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\sup_{z_{1}\in\gamma_{0}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0})}\sup_{(t,x^{\prime},v^{\prime})\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\widehat{r}_{1}}(z_{1}^{\prime})}\|G(t,x^{\prime},\cdot,v^{\prime},\cdot)\|_{L^{8-\delta_{0}}(H_{\widehat{r}_{1}})}+\|F(t,x^{\prime},\cdot,v^{\prime},\cdot)\|_{L^{8-\delta_{0}}(H_{\widehat{r}_{1}})}\leq C_{0},

\displaystyle\sup_{z_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0})}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}(z_{2})\cap\gamma_{0}=\emptyset,\\
\rho\leq r_{2}\end{subarray}}\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}-\delta}(\|G\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{2}))}+\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{2}))})\leq C_{0}

for some constants
c_{B}
and
C_{0}
, where
\delta_{0}\coloneqq\frac{16\delta}{1+2\delta}
and we write
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{r}(z_{1}^{\prime})
as the kinetic cylinder defined in
\mathbb{R}^{2(n-1)+1}
and
{H}_{r}
as the stationary cylinder determined in (
3.1
). If
2r_{0}={R}
, we have

(7.14)

\displaystyle[f]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq c\langle v_{0}\rangle^{c_{p}}(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2r_{0}}(z_{0}))}+[g]_{C^{\frac{3}{4}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{2r_{0}}(z_{0}))}+C_{0}),

where
c_{p}=c(p)
and
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))},c_{B},\delta,C_{1},p)
.

To prove this, first we provide the following lemma explaining that the
L^{\infty}
-norm of the boundary datum can be estimated by its Hölder semi-norm.

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
f
be a weak solution to

(7.15)

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}f)&=B\cdot\nabla_{v}f-\mathrm{div}_{v}(G)+F&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0})$},\\
f&=g&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0})$}\end{aligned}\right.

with
z_{0}\in\gamma_{-}\cup\gamma_{0}
.
If
g\in C^{\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))
for some
\epsilon>0
, then we have

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c(R^{-(4n+2)}\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+\|RG\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+\|R^{2}F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))})

\displaystyle\quad+cR^{\epsilon}[g]_{C^{\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))},

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))})
.

Proof.

First, by
[
36
]
,
f
is continuous up to the boundary, which implies
f(z_{0})=g(z_{0})
.
Thus, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle|f(z_{0})|\leq c(R^{-(4n+2)}\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+\|RG\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+\|R^{2}F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))}+R^{\epsilon}[g]_{C^{\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))}),

where
c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R}(z_{0}))})
, as

\displaystyle\|g-f(z_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))}=\|g-g(z_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))}\leq cR^{\epsilon}[g]_{C^{\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R}(z_{0}))}.

Suppose not, then there are sequences
(f_{k})_{k}
,
(A_{k})_{k}
,
(B_{k})_{k}
,
(G_{k})
,
(F_{k})
,
(R_{k})_{k}
,
(z_{k})_{k}
, and
(g_{k})_{k}
with

(7.16)

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f_{k}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A_{k}\nabla_{v}f_{k})&=B_{k}\cdot\nabla_{v}f_{k}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(G_{k})+F_{k}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{R_{k}}(z_{k})$},\\
f_{k}&=g_{k}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R_{k}}(z_{k})$},\end{aligned}\right.

and
\Lambda^{-1}I\leq A_{k}\leq\Lambda I
and
\|B_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{k}}(z_{k}))}\leq\Lambda
,
but
|f_{k}(z_{k})|=1
and

\displaystyle R_{k}^{-(4n+2)}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{k}}(z_{k}))}+\|R_{k}G_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{k}}(z_{k}))}+\|R_{k}^{2}F_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{k}}(z_{k}))}+[R_{k}^{\epsilon}g_{k}]_{C^{\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R_{k}}(z_{k}))}\to 0.

After simple modifications of the proof given in
[
35
, Lemma 2.25]
(see also
subsection 5.1
) to treat the right-hand side
-\mathrm{div}_{v}(G_{k})
, we deduce that

\displaystyle R_{k}^{\epsilon}[f_{k}]_{C^{\epsilon}(\overline{\mathcal{H}_{R_{k}/2}(z_{k})})}\leq c.

We now consider the function
\widehat{f}_{k}\coloneqq f_{k}(z_{k}\circ S_{R_{k}}z)
to see that

\displaystyle[\widehat{f}_{k}]_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1,k})}\leq c\quad\text{and}\quad\|\widehat{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{H}_{1,k})}\to 0,

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{1,k}\coloneqq\left\{z\,:\,z_{k}\circ S_{R_{k}}z\in\mathcal{H}_{R_{k}}(z_{k})\right\}\supset[0,1)\times B_{1}^{\prime}\times[0,1]\times B_{1}.

Indeed, we have used the fact that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{H}_{R_{k}}(z_{k})|\eqsim R_{k}^{-(4n+2)}\quad\text{and}\quad|\mathcal{H}_{1,k}|\eqsim 1.

By Arzelà-Ascoli’s theorem,
\widehat{f}_{k}
converges uniformly to
\widehat{f}_{\infty}
in
[0,1)\times(B_{1}^{\prime}\times[0,1])\times B_{1}
with

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}([0,1)\times(B_{1}^{\prime}\times[0,1])\times B_{1})}=0.

This gives the contradiction
0=|\widehat{f}_{\infty}(0)|=\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}|\widehat{f}_{k}(0)|=\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}|{f}_{k}(z_{k})|=1
, completing the proof.
∎

Now we are ready to prove
subsection 7.1
.

Proof of
subsection 7.1
.

The proof is similar to
subsection 5.1
except that here we want to get the optimal Hölder exponent. We may assume
z_{0}=0
. Let us fix
\delta<\frac{1}{2}
.

First, we want to show that for any
\widetilde{z}_{0}\in\gamma_{0}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r_{0}}(z_{0})
and
r\leq r_{0}
,

(7.17)

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(\widetilde{z}_{0})}|f-(f)_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(\widetilde{z}_{0})}|\,dz\leq c\langle v_{0}\rangle^{c_{p}}r^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}\big(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2r_{0}}(z_{0}))}+[g]_{C^{\frac{3}{4}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{2r_{0}}(z_{0}))}+C_{0}\big),

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{2r_{0}}(z_{0}))},c_{B},\delta,C_{1},p)
. Let us fix
M_{0}=M_{0}(n,\Lambda,c_{B})
, which will be determined later (see (
7.18
)). Note that when
r>\frac{\langle v_{0}\rangle^{-(2+p)}}{M_{0}}
, then we observe

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(\widetilde{z}_{0})}|f-(f)_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(\widetilde{z}_{0})}|\,dz\leq cr^{-(4n+2)-\frac{1}{2}+\delta}r^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2_{r_{0}}}(z_{0}))}\leq c\langle v_{0}\rangle^{-(4n+3)(2+p)}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2r_{0}}(z_{0}))},

which gives (
7.17
) with
c_{p}>(4n+3)(2+p)
and
c=c(n,M_{0})
. Thus, it suffices to prove (
7.17
) with
r\leq\frac{\langle v_{0}\rangle^{-(2+p)}}{M_{0}}
.

Now let
h
be a unique weak solution to

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})h-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}h)&=B\cdot\nabla_{v}f&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{r}(\widetilde{z}_{0})$},\\
h&=f&&\qquad\mbox{in $\partial_{\mathrm{kin}}\mathcal{Q}_{R}(\widetilde{z}_{0})$.}\end{aligned}\right.

By following the same lines as in the proof of
subsection 5.1
together with the second condition given in (
7.13
), we deduce

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(\widetilde{z}_{0})}|f-h|^{2}\,dz\leq c\Big(\|B\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(\widetilde{z}_{0})}r^{2}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(\widetilde{z}_{0})}|f-h|^{2}\,dz+C_{0}^{2}r^{2-2\delta}\Big).

By taking
M_{0}=M_{0}(n,\Lambda,c_{B})
large enough, we have

(7.18)

\displaystyle r(1+\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(\widetilde{z}_{0}))})\leq\frac{\langle v_{0}\rangle^{-2}}{M_{0}}(c_{B}\langle v_{0}\rangle^{2}+1)\leq\frac{1}{2c},

which implies

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(\widetilde{z}_{0})}|f-h|^{2}\,dz\leq cC_{0}^{2}r^{2-2\delta}.

By a combination of
Theorem 6.2
, the second estimates of (2.33) in
[
35
, Lemma 2.25]
and
subsection 7.1
together with the scaling argument as in (
5.4
), we obtain

\displaystyle r^{\frac{1}{2}}[h]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\delta}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{\frac{r}{2}}(\widetilde{z}_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\big(r^{-(2n+1)}\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(\widetilde{z}_{0}))}+[r^{\frac{3}{4}}g]_{C^{\frac{3}{4}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\widetilde{z}_{0}))}\big),

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))})
.
In particular, for the scaling argument, we have used (
5.6
) by (
7.18
).
Thus, as in the proof of
subsection 5.1
, we deduce

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho r}(\widetilde{z}_{0})}|f-(f)_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho r}(\widetilde{z}_{0})}|^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq c\rho^{1-{\delta}}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(\widetilde{z}_{0})}|f-(f)_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(\widetilde{z}_{0})}|^{2}\,dz+cr^{\frac{3}{4}}[g]_{C^{\frac{3}{4}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r}(\widetilde{z}_{0}))}+cr^{1-2\delta}C_{0},

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))},\delta)
. Now taking
\rho
sufficiently small so that
\rho^{{\delta}}\leq\frac{1}{16c}
and following the standard iterative argument as in
[
30
, Lemma 5.6]
, we have (
7.17
).

Now we prove (
7.17
) with
\widetilde{z}_{0}
replaced by
z_{1}\in\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0})
. Similarly, we assume
r\leq\frac{\langle v_{0}\rangle^{-2-p}}{M_{0}}
. First, suppose
\mathcal{H}_{2r}(z_{1})\cap\gamma_{0}\neq\emptyset
. Then there is a point
\widetilde{z}_{1}\in\mathcal{H}_{2r}(z_{1})\cap\gamma_{0}
such that
\mathcal{H}_{2r}(z_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{8r}(\widetilde{z}_{1})
. Thus, using this, we also get (
7.17
). Now we assume
\mathcal{H}_{2r}(z_{1})\cap\gamma_{0}=\emptyset
. Then we have for
\rho_{1}\coloneqq\frac{1}{64}\max\{{(x_{1,n})^{\frac{1}{3}}},|v_{1,n}|\}
,
r\leq 64\rho_{1}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\rho_{1}}(z_{1})\cap\gamma_{0}=\emptyset
. By
subsection 7.1
together with the scaling arguments as in (
5.6
) by (
7.18
), which allows us to remove the dependence of
\|B\|_{L^{\infty}}
, we have

\displaystyle J\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1})}|f-(f)_{\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1})}|^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{r}{\rho_{1}}\right)^{1-2\delta}\Bigg({\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho_{1}}(z_{1})}|f-(f)_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho_{1}}(z_{1})}|^{2}\,dz+\rho_{1}^{2}\|G\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho_{1}}(z_{1})}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\rho_{1}^{4}\|F\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho_{1}}(z_{1})}+\rho_{1}[g]^{2}_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{H}_{\rho_{1}}(z_{1}))}\Bigg),

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))})
, whenever
r\leq\rho_{1}/2
. Now using the third condition given in (
7.13
) together with
\mathcal{H}_{\rho_{1}}(z_{1})\cap\gamma_{0}=\emptyset
, we further estimate
J
as

\displaystyle J\leq c\left(\left(\frac{r}{\rho_{1}}\right)^{1-2\delta}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho_{1}}(z_{1})}|f-(f)_{\mathcal{H}_{\rho_{1}}(z_{1})}|^{2}\,dz+r^{1-2\delta}(C_{0}+[g]^{2}_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))})\right).

When
r\in[\rho_{1}/2,64\rho_{1}]
, we have
J\leq c{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{64\rho_{1}}(z_{1})}|f-(f)_{\mathcal{H}_{64\rho_{1}}(z_{1})}|^{2}\,dz
, which gives

\displaystyle J\leq c\left(\left(\frac{r}{\rho_{1}}\right)^{1-2\delta}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{64\rho_{1}}(z_{1})}|f-(f)_{\mathcal{H}_{64\rho_{1}}(z_{1})}|^{2}\,dz+r^{1-2\delta}((C_{0})^{2}+[g]^{2}_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))})\right).

Since
\mathcal{H}_{64\rho_{1}}(z_{1})\cap\gamma_{0}\neq\emptyset
, there is a point
\widetilde{z}_{1}\in\gamma_{0}
such that
\mathcal{H}_{64\rho_{1}}(z_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{2^{9}\rho_{1}}(\widetilde{z}_{1})
. Now we plug (
7.17
) with
\widetilde{z}_{0}
and
r
replaced by
\widetilde{z}_{1}
and
64\rho_{1}
, respectively, into the estimate of
J
so that we derive

\displaystyle J\leq c\langle v_{0}\rangle^{2c_{p}}r^{1-2\delta}(\|f\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2r_{0}}(z_{0}))}+[g]^{2}_{C^{\frac{3}{4}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{2r_{0}}(z_{0}))}+(C_{0})^{2}).

Therefore, we have proved (
7.17
) for any
z\in\gamma_{0}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r_{0}}(z_{0})
, which implies the desired
C^{\frac{1}{2}}
-estimate.
∎

7.2.
Regularity of the diffuse reflection boundary datum

In this subsection, we prove
section 7
.
First, we introduce some notation, which will be used frequently.

(7.19)

\displaystyle\langle v_{0}\rangle\coloneqq 1+|v_{0}|,\quad r_{0}\coloneqq\frac{\langle v_{0}\rangle^{-p}}{64},\quad\rho_{0}\coloneqq\max\left\{\frac{|v_{0,n}|}{2},\left(\frac{|x_{0,n}|}{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right\},

where the constant
p\geq\frac{1}{2}
is fixed in
section 7
.

Proof of
section 7
.

First, we prove regularity of the time derivative of
\mathcal{N}f
. Recall the definition of
g=g(t,x^{\prime})
in (
7.1
).
For any
h\in\mathbb{R}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
, we write

(7.20)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\delta_{h,1}f(z)&\coloneqq\delta_{h}f(z)\coloneqq f_{h}(z)-f(z),\quad f_{h}(z)\coloneqq f((h,0,0)\circ z)=f(t+h,x,v),\\
\delta_{h,k}f&\coloneqq\delta_{h,k-1}\circ\delta_{h}f,\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\widehat{g}_{k}\coloneqq\frac{\delta_{h,k}g}{|h|^{k}}.\end{split}

It is straightforward to check that
f_{h}
is also a weak solution. We assume

(7.21)

\displaystyle\sup_{z_{0}\in\mathcal{H}_{1,\infty}}\langle v_{0}\rangle^{M}\sum_{i=0}^{k}\left\|\partial_{t}^{i}\mathcal{M}\right\|_{C^{\frac{7}{6}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{32r_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq 1

for some large constant
M
. Let us now fix
k\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
. We will prove that for any
z_{0}\in\mathcal{H}_{1,\infty}
,
r\in(0,r_{0}]
,
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, and
|h|\leq 2^{-100k}\eqqcolon h_{0}
, if
M
is sufficiently large, then it holds

(7.22)

\displaystyle\|\widehat{g}_{k}-a_{r}\|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq cr^{\frac{7}{6}}\langle v_{0}\rangle^{c_{k}}D_{k},

where
c_{k}=c_{k}(n,k,p)
and
c=c(n,\Lambda,k,C_{k})
,

(7.23)

\displaystyle a_{r}\coloneqq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\int_{w_{n}<-16r-r_{w^{\prime}}}\frac{\delta_{h,k}f}{|h|^{k}}(t_{0},x_{0}^{\prime},0,w^{\prime},w_{n})(w_{n})_{-}\,dw_{n}\,dw^{\prime},

\displaystyle C_{k}\coloneqq\sup_{z_{0}\in\mathcal{H}_{\frac{3}{2},\infty}}\left\|{A}\right\|_{C^{3k+1}(\mathcal{H}_{32r_{0}}(z_{0}))}+\frac{1}{\langle v_{0}\rangle^{2}}\left\|B\right\|_{C^{3k}(\mathcal{H}_{32r_{0}}(z_{0}))},

\displaystyle D_{k}\coloneqq\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{c_{k}}f(t,x,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty})}+\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{c_{k}}{F(t,x,\cdot)}\right\|_{C^{3k}(\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty})}

with
r_{w^{\prime}}\coloneqq 16(r^{2}|w^{\prime}-v_{0}^{\prime}|)^{\frac{1}{3}}
, where the space
\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{c_{k}}F(t,x,\cdot)\|_{C^{3k}}
is defined in (
7.5
). The choice of
r_{w^{\prime}}
will be required in order to have (
•
‣
7.2
) and we note for any
j\leq k
and
\epsilon\in[0,1]
,

(7.24)

\displaystyle\|{\partial_{t}^{j}f}\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq c(k,j,\epsilon)\|f\|_{C^{3k+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0}))}.

Note that once (
7.22
) is established, the proof can be concluded easily (see Step 4). We now divide the proof of (
7.22
) into three steps.

Step 1. Estimates of higher order different quotients of the boundary data.
First, we are going to prove

(7.25)

\displaystyle|h|^{k}\|\widehat{g}_{k}-a_{r}\|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq cr^{\frac{4}{3}}\langle v_{0}\rangle\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{4}\delta_{h,k}f(t,x^{\prime},0,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle+cr^{\frac{2(2-\epsilon)}{3}}\langle v_{0}\rangle\sup_{(t,x^{\prime})\in\mathbf{B}_{\frac{3}{2}}}\underbrace{\int_{\frac{1}{{\langle w\rangle}^{p}}\leq|w_{n}|}\int_{{w_{n}<-64r}}\langle w\rangle^{5}[\delta_{h,k}f]_{C^{2-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{\frac{1}{64{\langle w\rangle}^{p}}}(t,x^{\prime},0,w))}(w_{n})_{-}\,dw}_{\eqqcolon L_{1}}

\displaystyle+cr^{\frac{2(2-\epsilon)}{3}}\langle v_{0}\rangle\sup_{(t,x^{\prime})\in\mathbf{B}_{\frac{3}{2}}}\underbrace{\int_{\frac{1}{{\langle w\rangle}^{p}}\geq|w_{n}|}\int_{{w_{n}<-64r}}\langle w\rangle^{5}[\delta_{h,k}f]_{C^{2-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{\frac{|w_{n}|}{64}}(t,x^{\prime},0,w))}(w_{n})_{-}\,dw}_{\eqqcolon L_{2}},

where
\mathbf{B}_{\frac{3}{2}}\coloneqq I_{(\frac{3}{2})^{2}}\times B^{\prime}_{(\frac{3}{2})^{3}}
and
c=c(n,\epsilon,p)
. Let us fix
(t,x^{\prime},0,v)\in\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})\subset\mathcal{H}_{1,\infty}
to observe

\displaystyle|h|^{k}|\widehat{g}_{k}(t,x^{\prime},0,v)-a_{r}|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int_{w_{n}<-64r-r_{w^{\prime}}}\left(\delta_{h,k}f(t,x^{\prime},0,w^{\prime},w_{n})-\delta_{h,k}f(t_{0},x_{0}^{\prime},0,w^{\prime},w_{n})\right)(w_{n})_{-}\,dw\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\left|\int_{0>w_{n}>-64r-r_{w^{\prime}}}\delta_{h,k}f(t,x^{\prime},0,w^{\prime},w_{n})(w_{n})_{-}\,dw\right|\eqqcolon J_{1}+J_{2}.

To estimate
J_{1}
, we investigate the term
J
defined by

\displaystyle J\coloneqq|\delta_{h,k}f(t,x^{\prime},0,w^{\prime},w_{n})-\delta_{h,k}f(t_{0},x_{0}^{\prime},0,w^{\prime},w_{n})|.

Now we split into two cases, as
\mathcal{H}_{\frac{|w_{n}|}{2}}(t_{0},x_{0}^{\prime},0,w)\not\subset\mathcal{H}_{\frac{3}{2},\infty}
when
|w_{n}|\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\langle w\rangle}}
and we want to get Hölder estimates on the cylinder
\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1})
, where
r\lesssim\langle v_{1}\rangle^{-p}
.

•

Assume
\frac{1}{{\langle w\rangle}^{p}}\geq{|w_{n}|}
and note

\displaystyle J

\displaystyle\leq|\delta_{h,k}f(t,x^{\prime},0,w^{\prime},w_{n})-\delta_{h,k}f(t_{0},x^{\prime}+(t_{0}-t)w^{\prime},(t_{0}-t)w_{n},w^{\prime},w_{n})|

\displaystyle\quad+|\delta_{h,k}f(t_{0},x^{\prime}+(t_{0}-t)w^{\prime},(t_{0}-t)w_{n},w^{\prime},w_{n})-\delta_{h,k}f(t_{0},x_{0}^{\prime},0,w^{\prime},w_{n})|.

By the choice of
r_{w^{\prime}}=16(r^{2}|w^{\prime}-v_{0}^{\prime}|)^{\frac{1}{3}}
and
\frac{1}{{\langle w\rangle}^{p}}\geq{|w_{n}|}
, we have

\displaystyle(t_{0},x^{\prime}+(t_{0}-t)w^{\prime},(t_{0}-t)w_{n},w)\in\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(t,x^{\prime},0,w)\subset\mathcal{H}_{\frac{3}{2},\infty},

(7.26)

\displaystyle(t_{0},x^{\prime}+(t_{0}-t)w^{\prime},(t_{0}-t)w_{n},w)\in\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(t_{0},x_{0}^{\prime},0,w)\subset\mathcal{H}_{\frac{3}{2},\infty},

where
R_{0}\coloneqq\frac{|w_{n}|}{64}
. Therefore, we estimate
J
as

(7.27)

\displaystyle J

\displaystyle\leq c(r^{2-\epsilon}+|x^{\prime}-x_{0}^{\prime}+(t_{0}-t)w^{\prime}|^{\frac{2-\epsilon}{3}}+|(t_{0}-t)w_{n}|^{\frac{2-\epsilon}{3}})\sup_{(t,x^{\prime})\in\mathbf{B}_{\frac{3}{2}}}[\delta_{h,k}f]_{C^{2-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(t,x^{\prime},0,w))}

\displaystyle\leq cr^{\frac{2(2-\epsilon)}{3}}\langle v_{0}\rangle\langle w\rangle\sup_{(t,x^{\prime})\in\mathbf{B}_{\frac{3}{2}}}[\delta_{h,k}f]_{C^{2-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(t,x^{\prime},0,w))},

where we have also used the fact that
(t,x^{\prime},0,v)\in\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})
, which implies

\displaystyle|x^{\prime}-(x_{0}^{\prime}+(t_{0}-t)w^{\prime})|\leq|x^{\prime}-x_{0}^{\prime}+(t_{0}-t)v_{0}^{\prime}|+|(t_{0}-t)(v_{0}^{\prime}-w^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq r^{3}+r^{2}(\langle v_{0}\rangle+\langle w\rangle)

\displaystyle\leq cr^{2}\langle v_{0}\rangle\langle w\rangle,

•

Assume
\frac{1}{{\langle w\rangle}^{p}}\leq{|w_{n}|}
. Then we write

\displaystyle J

\displaystyle\leq|\delta_{h,k}f(t,x^{\prime},0,w^{\prime},w_{n})-\delta_{h,k}f(t_{0},x^{\prime},0,w^{\prime},w_{n})|+|\delta_{h,k}f(t_{0},x^{\prime},0,w^{\prime},w_{n})-\delta_{h,k}f(t_{0},x_{0}^{\prime},0,w^{\prime},w_{n})|

\displaystyle\eqqcolon J_{1,1}+J_{1,2}.

We may assume
t_{0}<t
. Now let us choose
R_{0}\coloneqq\frac{1}{64{\langle w\rangle}^{p}}
and define a sequence
t_{i}\coloneqq t_{0}+(16)^{2}(i-1)(R_{0})^{5}
to see that

\displaystyle(R_{0})^{3}>(16)^{2}(R_{0})^{5}\langle w\rangle\quad\text{and}\quad R_{0}^{2}\langle w\rangle\leq\frac{1}{64}.

Thus, there is a positive integer
m
such that
t\in(t_{m-1},t_{m}]
and

\displaystyle(t_{i-1},x^{\prime},0,w^{\prime},w_{n})\in\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(t_{i},x^{\prime},0,w)\subset\mathcal{H}_{\frac{3}{2},\infty},\quad(16)^{2}(m-1)(R_{0})^{5}<|t-t_{0}|.

Using these, we can estimate
J_{1,1}
as

\displaystyle J_{1,1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{m}|\delta_{h,k}f(t_{i},x^{\prime},0,w)-\delta_{h,k}f(t_{i-1},x^{\prime},0,w)|

\displaystyle\leq c\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(|t_{i}-t_{i-1}|^{\frac{2-\epsilon}{2}}+|(t_{i}-t_{i-1})w|^{\frac{2-\epsilon}{3}}\right)[\delta_{h,k}f]_{C^{2-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(t_{i},x^{\prime},0,w))}

\displaystyle\leq c\sum_{i=1}^{m}|r^{2}\langle w\rangle|^{\frac{2(1-\epsilon)}{3}}[\delta_{h,k}f]_{C^{2-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(t_{i},x^{\prime},0,w))}

\displaystyle\leq cm\sup_{i\in[1,m]}r^{\frac{2(2-\epsilon)}{3}}\langle w\rangle^{\frac{2}{3}}[\delta_{h,k}f]_{C^{2-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(t_{i},x^{\prime},0,w))}

\displaystyle\leq c\sup_{(t,x^{\prime})\in\mathbf{B}_{\frac{3}{2}}}r^{\frac{2(2-\epsilon)}{3}}\langle w\rangle^{1+5p}[\delta_{h,k}f]_{C^{2-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(t,x^{\prime},0,w))}.

Similarly, by choosing
x_{0,i}^{\prime}=x_{0}^{\prime}+(i-1)(R_{0})^{3}
, we estimate
J_{2,2}
as

\displaystyle J_{1,2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{m}|\delta_{h,k}f(t_{0},x_{i}^{\prime},0,w)-\delta_{h,k}f(t_{0},x_{i-1}^{\prime},0,w)|

\displaystyle\leq c\sum_{i=1}^{m}|x^{\prime}-x^{\prime}_{0}|^{\frac{2(1-\epsilon)}{3}}[\delta_{h,k}f]_{C^{2-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(t_{0},x^{\prime}_{i-1},0,w))}

\displaystyle\leq cr^{\frac{2(2-\epsilon)}{3}}\langle v_{0}\rangle^{\frac{2}{3}}\langle w\rangle^{3p}\sup_{(t,x^{\prime})\in\mathbf{B}_{\frac{3}{2}}}[\delta_{h,k}f]_{C^{2-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(t,x^{\prime},0,w))},

where
m
is the positive integer such that
x^{\prime}\in[x_{m-1}^{\prime},x_{m}^{\prime}]
and we have used the fact that
|x^{\prime}-x_{0}^{\prime}|\leq r^{3}+|(t-t_{0})v_{0}^{\prime}|
by
(t,x^{\prime},0,v)\in\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})
. Combining the estimates
J_{1,1}
and
J_{1,2}
gives

(7.28)

\displaystyle J\leq cr^{\frac{2(2-\epsilon)}{3}}\langle v_{0}\rangle\langle w\rangle^{10p}\sup_{(t,x^{\prime})\in\mathbf{B}_{\frac{3}{2}}}[\delta_{h,k}f]_{C^{2-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(t,x^{\prime},0,w))}.

Next, we estimate
J_{1}
as

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int\int^{0}_{-16r-r_{w^{\prime}}}\sup_{w_{n}<0}\left|\delta_{h,k}f(t,x^{\prime},0,w^{\prime},w_{n})\right||w_{n}|\,dw_{n}\,dw^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq cr^{\frac{4}{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\sup_{w_{n}<0}\left|\delta_{h,k}f(t,x^{\prime},0,w^{\prime},w_{n})\right|(1+|w^{\prime}-v_{0}^{\prime}|^{\frac{2}{3}})\,dw^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq cr^{\frac{4}{3}}\langle v_{0}^{\prime}\rangle^{\frac{2}{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\sup_{w_{n}<0}\left|\delta_{h,k}f(t,x^{\prime},0,w^{\prime},w_{n})\right|\langle w^{\prime}\rangle^{4}\langle w^{\prime}\rangle^{-\frac{10}{3}}\,dw^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq cr^{\frac{4}{3}}\langle v_{0}\rangle\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{4}\delta_{h,k}f(t,x^{\prime},0,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

Combining the previous estimate with (
7.27
) and (
7.28
) yields (
7.25
), as desired.

Step 2. Regularity of higher order different quotients of the solution.

By taking the different quotient operator to (
7.2
), we get for any
l\leq k
,

(7.29)

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})\delta_{h,l}f-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A_{lh}\nabla_{v}\delta_{h,l}f)&=B_{lh}\cdot\nabla_{v}\delta_{h,l}f+\delta_{h,l}F+G_{l}&&\quad\mbox{in $\{x_{n}>0\}\cap\mathcal{H}_{\frac{7}{4},\infty}$},\\
\delta_{h,l}f&=\delta_{h,l}\mathcal{N}f&&\quad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where

\displaystyle G_{l}\coloneqq\sum_{i=0}^{l}\mathrm{div}_{v}(\delta_{h,l-i}A_{ih}\nabla_{v}\delta_{h,l}f)+\delta_{h,l-i}B_{ih}\cdot\nabla_{v}\delta_{h,i}f.

Then by
subsection 7.1
, we have for any
\mathcal{H}_{2\rho}(z_{1})\cap\gamma_{0}=\emptyset
with
\rho\leq r_{1}=\frac{\langle v_{1}\rangle^{p}}{16}
,
x_{1,n}=0
, and
v_{1,n}<0
, and
l\in\{0,\dots,k\}
,

\displaystyle\rho^{\epsilon-1}\|\nabla_{v}\delta_{h,l}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1}))}+[\delta_{h,l}f]_{C^{2-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\langle v_{1}\rangle^{2}(\rho^{-2+\epsilon}\|\delta_{h,l}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho}(z_{1}))}+\|\delta_{h,l}F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho}(z_{1}))})

\displaystyle+c\langle v_{1}\rangle^{2}\sum_{i=0}^{l-1}(\|\delta_{h,l-i}A_{ih}\|_{C^{1}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho}(z_{1}))}+\|\delta_{h,l-i}B_{ih}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho}(z_{1}))})\|\nabla_{v}\delta_{h,i}f\|_{C^{1-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho}(z_{1}))},

where
c=c(n,\epsilon,\|A_{lh}\|_{C^{1-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho}(z_{1}))},c_{B})
. Therefore, by iterating this estimate
l-1
times, we deduce

(7.30)

\displaystyle\left[\frac{\delta_{h,l}f}{|h|^{l}}\right]_{C^{2-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\langle v_{1}\rangle^{4(l+1)}\sum_{i=0}^{l}\left(\rho^{-2+\epsilon}\left\|\frac{\delta_{h,i}f}{|h|^{i}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho}(z_{1}))}+\left\|\frac{\delta_{h,i}F}{|h|^{i}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho}(z_{1}))}\right),

where
c=c(n,l,\epsilon,c_{B},C_{l})
and the constant
C_{l}
is determined in (
7.23
). We will use this estimate on the terms
L_{1},L_{2}
in (
7.25
).

Step 3. Induction.
Now, based on an inductive argument, we will prove that for any
j\in\{0,1,\ldots,k\}
and
\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
, there is a constant
N_{j}=N_{j}(n,j,p)
such that for any
z_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{\frac{3}{2},\infty}
and
\rho\leq{r_{2}}=\frac{1}{64{\langle v_{2}\rangle}^{p}}
,

(7.31)

\displaystyle\left[\frac{\delta_{h,j}f}{|h|^{j}}\right]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho}(z_{2}))}+\left\|\frac{\delta_{h,j}f}{|h|^{j}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho}(z_{2}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\langle v_{2}\rangle^{N_{j}}\left(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{4r_{2}}(t,x_{2},v_{2}))}+\left\|{F}\right\|_{C^{3j}(\mathcal{H}_{4r_{2}}(t,x_{2},v_{2}))}+D_{j}\left\|{\mathcal{M}}\right\|_{C^{3_{j}+\frac{7}{6}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{4r_{2}}(t,x_{2},v_{2}))}\right),

where
c=c(n,\delta,j,C_{j},p)
, and
C_{j},D_{j}
are determined in (
7.23
), provided that the constant
M
given in (
7.21
) is sufficiently large, which will be used in (
7.34
) and (
7.35
). Note that we just choose
\frac{7}{6}\in(1,\frac{4}{3})
for convenience. Once (
7.31
) is proved, we can use it to estimate the first term in (
7.25
).

Clearly, for
j=0
, combining (
7.30
) and (
7.25
), we have that when
A\in C^{2(\epsilon-\frac{1}{2})}
for
\epsilon>\frac{1}{2}
, then
g\in C^{{\frac{4\epsilon}{3}}-\delta}
for any
\delta>0
. Therefore, we get (
7.6
) and then apply
Theorem 6.2
to get (
7.31
) with
j=0
.

Now, under the assumption (
7.31
) for any
j=0,\ldots,l
, we will prove (
7.31
) with
j=l+1
.

Step 3-1. Higher regularity for
\frac{\delta_{h,{l+1}f}}{|h|^{l+\alpha}}
for
\alpha<\frac{1}{6}
away from
\gamma_{0}
.
Let us fix
\alpha<\frac{1}{6}
. Then using (
7.31
), (
A.2
), and (
A.3
), we derive for any
z_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{1,\infty}

(7.32)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\delta_{h,l+1}f}{|h|^{l+\alpha}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2r_{2}}(z_{2}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\langle v_{2}\rangle^{c_{l}}\left(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{4r_{2}}(t,x_{2},v_{2}))}+\left\|{F}\right\|_{C^{3l}(\mathcal{H}_{4r_{2}}(t,x_{2},v_{2}))}\right)

\displaystyle+cD_{l}\langle v_{2}\rangle^{c_{l}}\left\|{\mathcal{M}}\right\|_{C^{3l+\frac{7}{6}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{4r_{2}}(t,x_{2},v_{2}))},

where
c=c(n,\alpha,l,C_{l},p)
,
c_{l}=c_{l}(n,l,p)
.

Using this and (
7.30
) with
\frac{\delta_{h,l}f}{|h|^{l}}
replaced by
\frac{\delta_{h,l+1}f}{|h|^{l+\alpha}}
, we get for any
z_{1}\in\mathcal{H}_{1,\infty}
with
\mathcal{H}_{2\rho}(z_{1})\cap\gamma_{0}=\emptyset
and
\rho\leq r_{1}
,
x_{1,n}=0
and
v_{1,n}<0
,

(7.33)

\displaystyle\left[\frac{\delta_{h,l+1}f}{|h|^{l+\alpha}}\right]_{C^{2-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\rho^{-2+\epsilon}\langle v_{1}\rangle^{c_{l}}\left(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{4r_{1}}(t,x_{1},v_{1}))}+\left\|{F}\right\|_{C^{3(l+1)}(\mathcal{H}_{4r_{1}}(t,x_{1},v_{1}))}\right)

\displaystyle+c\rho^{-2+\epsilon}D_{l}\langle v_{1}\rangle^{c_{l}}\left\|{\mathcal{M}}\right\|_{C^{3l+\frac{7}{6}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{4r_{1}}(t,x_{1},v_{1}))},

where
c=c(n,\alpha,l,\epsilon,c_{B},C_{l},p)
and
r_{1}=\frac{\langle v_{1}\rangle^{-p}}{64}
. Indeed, we have also used some covering argument to get the radius
4r_{1}
for the cylinders given in the right-hand side of (
7.33
). Now plugging this with
z_{1}
and
\rho
replaced by
(t,x^{\prime},0,w)
and
\frac{1}{64{\langle w\rangle}^{p}}\eqqcolon\widetilde{r}
into
{L_{1}}
with
k=l+1
given in (
7.25
) leads to

(7.34)

\displaystyle\frac{L_{1}}{|h|^{l+\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{w}}\leq|w_{n}|}\int_{w_{n}<-64r}\langle w\rangle^{c_{l}+12p}\left(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{4\widetilde{r}}(\widetilde{z}))}+\left\|{F}\right\|_{C^{3(l+1)}(\mathcal{H}_{4\widetilde{r}}(\widetilde{z}))}\right)\,dw

\displaystyle+cD_{l}\int_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{w}}\leq|w_{n}|}\int_{w_{n}<-64r}\langle w\rangle^{c_{l}+12p}\left\|{\mathcal{M}}\right\|_{C^{3l+\frac{7}{6}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{4\widetilde{r}}(\widetilde{z}))}\,dw

\displaystyle\leq cD_{l+1},

where
\widetilde{z}=(t,x^{\prime},0,w)
, we choose
M=M(n,l,p)
large enough, recalling the definition of
D_{l}
given in (
7.21
) and using (
7.23
).
Similarly, using (
7.33
) with
\rho
and
z_{1}
replaced by
\frac{|w_{n}|}{64}
and
\widetilde{z}\coloneqq(t,x^{\prime},0,w)
, we estimate
\frac{L_{2}}{|h|^{l+\alpha}}
as

(7.35)

\displaystyle\frac{L_{2}}{|h|^{l+\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{w}}\geq|w_{n}|}\int_{w_{n}<-64r}\langle w\rangle^{10p+c_{l}}\left(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{4\widetilde{r}}(\widetilde{z}))}+\left\|{F}\right\|_{C^{3(l+1)}(\mathcal{H}_{4\widetilde{r}}(\widetilde{z}))}\right)(w_{n})_{-}^{-1+\epsilon}\,dw

\displaystyle+cD_{l}\int_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{w}}\geq|w_{n}|}\int_{w_{n}<-64r}\langle w\rangle^{10p+c_{l}}\left\|{\mathcal{M}}\right\|_{C^{3l+\frac{7}{6}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{4\widetilde{r}}(\widetilde{z}))}(w_{n})_{-}^{-1+\epsilon}\,dw

\displaystyle\leq cD_{l+1}\int_{w_{n}<-64r}\langle w^{\prime}\rangle^{-2}(w_{n})_{-}^{-1-\epsilon}\,dw_{n}\,dw^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq cD_{l+1}r^{-\epsilon},

where
c=c(n,\alpha,l,\epsilon,c_{B},C_{l},p,\epsilon)
. Since (
7.34
) and (
7.35
) are true for any
(t,x^{\prime})\in\mathbf{B}_{\frac{3}{2}}
, combining these estimates, (
7.32
) and (
7.25
) with

and
\epsilon=\frac{1}{10}
leads to

(7.36)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\delta_{h,l+1}g}{|h|^{l+\alpha}}\right\|_{C^{\frac{7}{6}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq cD_{l+1},

where
c=c(n,\alpha,l,\epsilon,c_{B},C_{l},p)
.
Note that inductively, we also get

(7.37)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\delta_{h,j}g}{|h|^{j}}\right\|_{C^{\frac{7}{6}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq D_{j}

for any
j\in\{0,\ldots,l\}
.

Step 3-2. Explosion of gradients of
\frac{\delta_{h,l}f}{|h|^{l}}
near
\gamma_{0}
.
The results in this step are required in order to treat the source terms
G_{l}
in divergence form in (
7.29
). We combine
C^{1/2-\delta}
estimates near
\gamma_{0}
with higher order estimates away from
\gamma_{0}
. First, using
subsection 7.1
with

\mathcal{H}_{2\rho}(z_{1})\cap\gamma_{0}=\emptyset
with
\rho\leq 1
, and following the same iterative argument as in (
7.30
) with
\delta_{h,l}f
replaced by
\delta_{h,l}f-\delta_{h,l}f(z_{1})
, we deduce

\displaystyle\rho^{-\frac{1}{6}}\left\|\frac{\nabla_{v}\delta_{h,l}f}{|h|^{l}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1}))}+\left[\frac{\nabla_{v}\delta_{h,l}f}{|h|^{l}}\right]_{C^{\frac{1}{6}}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\langle v_{1}\rangle^{4(l+1)}\sum_{i=0}^{l}\rho^{-\frac{7}{6}}\left\|\frac{\delta_{h,i}f-\delta_{h,i}f(z_{1})}{|h|^{i}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho}(z_{1}))}

\displaystyle\quad+c\langle v_{1}\rangle^{4(l+1)}\sum_{i=0}^{l}\left(\left\|\frac{\delta_{h,i}F}{|h|^{i}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho}(z_{1}))}+\left[\frac{\delta_{h,i}\mathcal{N}f}{|h|^{i}}\right]_{C^{\frac{7}{6}}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho}(z_{1}))}\right),

where
c=c(n,l,c_{B},C_{l})
. Now plugging in the
C^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}
estimates of
\frac{\delta_{h,i}f_{(l-i)h}}{|h|^{i}}
given in (
7.31
) with
j\in\{0,\ldots,l\}
and
z_{2}
replaced by
z_{1}
, and using (
7.37
), we deduce for any
\delta>0
,

(7.38)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\delta_{h,l}\nabla_{v}f}{|h|^{l}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\langle v_{1}\rangle^{c_{l}}\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}-\delta}\left(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{4r_{1}}(t,x_{1},v_{1}))}+\left\|{F}\right\|_{C^{3l}(\mathcal{H}_{4r_{1}}(t,x_{1},v_{1}))}\right)

\displaystyle+cD_{l}\langle v_{1}\rangle^{c_{l}}\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}-\delta}\left\|\mathcal{M}\right\|_{C^{3l+\frac{7}{6}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{4r_{1}}(t,x_{1},v_{1}))},

where
c_{l}=c_{l}(n,l,p)
and
c=c(n,\alpha,\delta,l,c_{B},C_{l},p)
.

Step 3-3.
C^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}
estimates of
\frac{\delta_{h,l+1}f}{|h|^{l+\alpha}}
up to
\gamma_{0}
.
Now we fix
z_{2}\in\gamma_{0}
and
\widehat{r}_{2}\coloneqq\frac{\langle v_{2}\rangle^{-p}}{64}(1-2^{-\frac{1}{p}})
to see that for any
z_{1}\in\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{r}_{2}}(z_{2})
,
r_{1}=\frac{\langle v_{1}\rangle^{-p}}{64}\leq 2r_{2}=\frac{\langle v_{2}\rangle^{-p}}{32}
, which implies
\mathcal{H}_{4r_{1}}(z_{1})\subset\mathcal{H}_{32r_{2}}(z_{2})
.
First, let us write
\widetilde{G}\coloneqq\sum\limits_{i=0}^{l}\left(\frac{\delta_{h,l-i+1}A}{|h|^{l-i+\alpha}}\frac{\delta_{h,i}\nabla_{v}f_{(l+1-i)h}}{|h|^{i}}\right)
to see that

\displaystyle\sup_{(t,x^{\prime},v^{\prime})\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\widehat{r}_{2}}(z^{\prime}_{2})}\|\widetilde{G}\|_{L^{8-\delta_{0}}(H_{\widehat{r}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{H_{\widehat{r}_{2}}}\sup_{z_{1}\in\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{r}_{2}}(z_{2})}\sum_{i=0}^{l}\left\|\frac{\delta_{h,l}\nabla_{v}f}{|h|^{l}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(z_{1}))}\,dx_{1,n}\,dv_{1,n}

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{H_{r}}\max\{|x_{1,n}|^{\frac{1}{3}},|v_{1,n}|\}^{(-\frac{1}{2}-\sigma)(8-\delta_{0})}

\displaystyle\times\Bigg(\langle v_{2}\rangle^{c_{l}}\left(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{32r_{2}}(z_{2}))}+\left\|{F}\right\|_{C^{3l}(\mathcal{H}_{32r_{2}}(z_{2}))}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+cD_{l}\langle v_{2}\rangle^{c_{l}}\left\|{\mathcal{M}}\right\|_{C^{3l+\frac{7}{6}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{32r_{2}}(z_{2}))}\Bigg)\,dx_{n}dv_{n},

where
c_{l}=c_{l}(n,l,p)
,
c=c(n,\alpha,\sigma,l,c_{B},C_{l},p,\delta_{0})
and we have used (
7.38
) with
\delta=\sigma
and
\rho=\frac{1}{2}\max\{|x_{2,n}|^{\frac{1}{3}},|v_{2,n}|\}
. Then note that for any small
\delta_{0}
, there is a small constant
\sigma=\sigma(\delta_{0})
such that
(\frac{1}{2}+\sigma)(8-\delta_{0})<4
so that we have

\displaystyle\sup_{(t,x^{\prime},v^{\prime})\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\widehat{r}_{2}}(z^{\prime}_{2})}\|\widetilde{G}\|_{L^{8-\delta_{0}}(H_{\widehat{r}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\leq c\langle v_{2}\rangle^{c_{l}}\left(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{32r_{2}}(z_{2}))}+\left\|{F}\right\|_{C^{3l}(\mathcal{H}_{32r_{2}}(z_{2}))}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+D_{l}\langle v_{1}\rangle^{c_{l}}\left\|{\mathcal{M}}\right\|_{C^{3l+\frac{7}{6}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{32r_{2}}(z_{2}))},

where
c_{l}=c_{l}(n,l,p)
,
c=c(n,\alpha,\sigma,l,c_{B},C_{l},p,\delta_{0})
.
Similarly, we get the same estimates for
\widetilde{F}\coloneqq\frac{\delta_{h,l+1}F}{|h|^{l+\alpha}}+\sum\limits_{i=0}^{l}\frac{\delta_{h,l-i+1}B}{|h|^{l-i+\alpha}}\cdot\frac{\delta_{h,i}\nabla_{v}f_{(k-i)h}}{|h|^{i}}
.
Therefore, we can apply
subsection 7.1
with
f=\frac{\delta_{h,l+1}f}{|h|^{l+\alpha}}
,
G=\widetilde{G}
, and
F=\widetilde{F}
together with (
7.38
) and (
7.36
) to derive that for any
z_{2}\in\gamma_{0}
and
\rho\leq r_{2}
, we have

(7.39)

\displaystyle\left[\frac{\delta_{h,l+1}f}{|h|^{l+\alpha}}\right]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho}(z_{2}))}+\left\|\frac{\delta_{h,l+1}f}{|h|^{l+\alpha}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho}(z_{2}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\langle v_{2}\rangle^{c_{l}}\left(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{32r_{2}}(z_{2}))}+\left\|{F}\right\|_{C^{3(l+1)}(\mathcal{H}_{32r_{2}}(z_{2}))}+D_{l+1}\left\|{\mathcal{M}}\right\|_{C^{3(l+1)+\frac{7}{6}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{32r_{2}}(t,x_{2},v_{2}))}\right),

where
c_{l}=c_{l}(n,l,p)
and
c=c(n,\alpha,\sigma,l,\|A\|_{C^{1}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho}(z_{1}))},c_{B},C_{l})
, whenever
M=M(n,l,p)
given in (
7.21
) is sufficiently large (this was needed for (
7.34
) and (
7.35
)). Moreover, when
z_{2}
is away from
\gamma_{0}
, then by (
7.38
) with
\rho=r_{2}
, we observe that
\widetilde{F}
and
\widetilde{G}
are bounded. Now by
subsection 7.1
, we obtain the same estimate as in (
7.39
). Therefore, we have proved (
7.39
) for any
z_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty}
.

Thus, taking
\delta=\frac{1}{2}-3\alpha
and using some covering argument to reduce the radius of the cylinders given in the right-hand side, we have for any
z_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{1,\infty}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\delta_{h,l+1}f}{|h|^{l+2\alpha}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho}(z_{2}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\langle v_{1}\rangle^{c_{l}}\left(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{4r_{2}}(t,x_{2},v_{2}))}+\left\|{F}\right\|_{C^{3(l+1)}(\mathcal{H}_{4r_{2}}(t,x_{2},v_{2}))}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+cD_{l+1}\langle v_{2}\rangle^{c_{l}}\left\|{\mathcal{M}}\right\|_{C^{3(l+1)+\frac{7}{6}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{4r_{2}}(t,x_{2},v_{2}))}.

This estimate is a higher order version of (
7.32
). Since
\alpha\in(0,\frac{1}{6})
was arbitrary, we can iterate repeat the previous procedure, starting from (
7.32
) with
\alpha
replaced by
2\alpha
, six times and finally derive (
7.31
) with
j=l+1
, which implies (
7.31
) for any
j\in\{0,\ldots,k\}
by the induction.

Step 4. Conclusion.

Combining (
7.31
) with
j=k
, (
7.30
) and (
7.25
) immediately yields (
7.22
). Then, by (
7.22
), we have

\displaystyle\|\partial^{k}_{t}g\|_{C^{\frac{7}{6}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq c\langle v_{0}\rangle^{c_{k}}D_{k},

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,k,C_{k})
and
c_{k}=c_{k}(n,k,p)
. On the other hand, by considering
\delta^{x_{i}}_{h}g(z)=g(t,x+he_{i},v)-g(z)
with
i\in\{1,\ldots,k-1\}
instead of
\delta_{h}g(z)=g(t+h,x,v)-g(z)
and observing that (
7.24
) with
\partial_{t}
replaced by
\partial_{x_{i}}
holds, we also deduce (
7.22
) with
\delta_{h}
replaced by
\delta^{x_{i}}_{h}
, as
\delta^{x_{i}}_{h}f
satisfies the equation (
7.29
) with
\delta_{h,k}
replaced by
\delta^{x_{i}}_{h,k}
. Thus, we obtain that for any
i,j
with
i+j\leq{k}
,

(7.40)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{i}\partial^{j}_{t}\mathcal{N}f\|_{C^{\frac{7}{6}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\|\mathcal{M}\|_{C^{3k+\frac{7}{6}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}\langle v_{0}\rangle^{c_{k}}\bigg(\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{c_{k}}f(t,x,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty})}

\displaystyle+\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{c_{k}}F(t,x,\cdot)\|_{C^{3k}(\mathcal{H}_{2,\infty})}\bigg),

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,k,c_{B})
and
c_{k}=c_{k}(n,k,p)
, whenever
\langle v_{0}\rangle^{M}\|\mathcal{M}\|_{C^{3k}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq 1
for large
M=M(n,k,p)
and
\|B\|_{C^{3k}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq c_{B}\langle v_{0}\rangle^{2}
. Therefore, by (
A.1
), we directly check that when
k=2\lambda
,

(7.41)

\displaystyle[\mathcal{N}f]_{C^{\lambda,\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq c\sum_{i+j\leq 2\lambda}\|\partial_{x}^{i}\partial^{j}_{t}\mathcal{N}f\|_{C^{\frac{7}{6}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}.

Thus, combining (
7.40
) and (
7.41
) yields (
7.7
), which completes the proof.
Note that one can slightly improve the value of
k
here, but we decided to work in a suboptimal framework in order make the proof easier to read.
∎

Proof of
Theorem 7.1
.

Since we have proved the regularity of the boundary data
\mathcal{N}f
with in-flow, we apply
Theorem 6.2
and
[
35
, Lemma 4.4]
together with
section 7
to deduce the desired result.
∎

We end this section by proving that the
C^{\frac{1}{2}}
regularity obtained in
Theorem 7.1
is optimal. We provide a counterexample in 1D for the canonical choice of
\mathcal{M}(v)=2e^{-|v|^{2}}
being the appropriately normalized boundary Maxwellian
5
5

5

Note that
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{M}(w)w_{-}\textnormal{\,d}w=1
, but our construction also works for more general choices of
\mathcal{M}
.

Example 7.7
.

Let
n=1
. Take a cutoff function
\psi=\psi(v)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(B_{4})
such that
\psi\equiv 1
in
(-2,2)
.
Then,
f\coloneqq\phi_{0}\psi
is a weak solution to

\displaystyle v\partial_{x}f-\partial_{vv}f=-2\partial_{v}\phi_{0}\partial_{v}\psi-\phi_{0}\partial_{vv}\psi\eqqcolon F\quad\text{in }\{x>0\}

Since
\phi_{0}\in C^{\infty}_{loc}([0,\infty)\times\{|v|\geq 2\})
, we have
F\in C^{\infty}_{loc}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R})
. Next we select a cut off function
\xi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
such that
\xi(v)\equiv 2
on
v\geq-1
and
\xi(v)\equiv 0
on
v\leq-2
, and
\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\xi(w)e^{-|w|^{2}})w_{-}\textnormal{\,d}w\neq 1
. Now, we choose
a\not=0
such that

\displaystyle a=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}f(0,w)w_{-}\,dw\right)^{-1}\left(1-\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\xi(w)e^{-|w|^{2}})w_{-}\textnormal{\,d}w\right),

and define
\widetilde{f}=af+\xi(v)e^{-|v|^{2}}
. Then, setting
\mathcal{M}(v)={2e^{-|v|^{2}}}
, by construction, it holds

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}\widetilde{f}(0,v)=\mathcal{M}(v)\int_{\mathbb{R}}(af(0,w)+\xi(w)e^{-|w|^{2}})w_{-}\,dw=\mathcal{M}(v)=\xi(v)e^{-|v|^{2}}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ for }v\in(0,\infty),

and therefore

\left\{\begin{aligned} v\partial_{x}\widetilde{f}-\partial_{vv}\widetilde{f}&=\widetilde{F}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x>0\}$},\\
\widetilde{f}&=\mathcal{N}\widetilde{f}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{0\}\times\{v>0\}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where
\widetilde{F}=F-\partial_{vv}(\xi(v)e^{-|v|^{2}})\in C^{\infty}_{loc}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R})
, and we used that
\phi_{0}(0,v)=0
for
v>0
. However, (
C.37
) implies that the optimal regularity of the solution is
\widetilde{f}\in C^{\frac{1}{2}}_{loc}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R})
is optimal.

8.
Proof of main results in general domains

In this section, we will generalize our previous results to general domains via a flattening argument (similar to
[
8
,
15
,
36
,
35
]
) and prove
Theorem 1.1
,
Theorem 1.3
,
Theorem 1.7
, and
Theorem 1.6
. Note that we also establish
Theorem 8.1
,
Theorem 8.2
,
Theorem 8.4
, and
Theorem 8.6
, which are generalizations and sharpened versions of
Theorem 1.1
,
Theorem 1.3
,
Theorem 1.6
, and
Theorem 1.7
, respectively, to kinetic Fokker Planck equations with sufficiently smooth coefficients. Since the flattening argument is quite well understood, we will omit most parts and give suitable references instead.

Let us assume that the domain
\Omega
is of class
C^{\beta}
with
\beta\geq 2
. We now recall several useful facts which are already proved in
[
35
, Section 2.4]
and
[
36
, Chapter 3]
.

•

There are constants
R_{0},R_{1}\leq 1
depending only on
\Omega
such that for any
{x}_{1}\in\partial\Omega
, there is a
C^{\beta-2}

diffeomorphism

(8.1)

\Psi^{-1}_{{x}_{1}}(t,y,w)\equiv\Psi^{-1}(t,y,w)=(t,\varphi^{-1}(y),\nabla\varphi^{-1}(y)w),

where
\varphi^{-1}
is a
C^{\beta}
diffeomorphism and

(8.2)

\displaystyle(I\times(B_{{R_{0}}}({x}_{0}^{\prime},0))\times\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap\{x_{n}>0\}

\displaystyle\subset\Psi(I\times(\Omega\cap B_{R_{1}}({x}_{1}))\times\mathbb{R}^{n})

\displaystyle\subset(I\times(B_{2R_{0}}({x}_{0}^{\prime},0))\times\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap\{x_{n}>0\},

\displaystyle(I\times B_{{R_{0}}}({x}_{0}^{\prime},0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap\Gamma_{-}

\displaystyle\subset\Psi((I\times(\Omega\cap B_{R_{1}}({x}_{1}))\times\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap\gamma_{-})

\displaystyle\subset(I\times B_{2R_{0}}({x}_{0}^{\prime},0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap\Gamma_{-}

with
I
being a time interval
(a,b)
and
x_{0}=(x_{0}^{\prime},0)=\Psi(x_{1})
. To avoid confusion of the notation on the incoming part, we write
\Gamma_{-}=\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\}
.

•

If
f
is a weak solution to (
1.2
), then
\widetilde{f}=f\circ\Psi^{-1}(t,y,w)
is a weak solution to

(8.3)

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+w\cdot\nabla_{y})\widetilde{f}-\mathrm{div}_{w}(\widetilde{A}\nabla_{w}\widetilde{f})&=\widetilde{B}\cdot\nabla_{w}\widetilde{f}+\widetilde{F}&&\qquad\mbox{in $I\times(B_{{R_{0}}}({x}_{0}^{\prime},0)\cap\{x_{n}>0\})\times\mathbb{R}^{n}$},\\
\widetilde{f}&=\widetilde{g}&&\qquad\mbox{in $(I\times B_{{R_{0}}}({x}_{0}^{\prime},0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap\Gamma_{-}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where we write
\Gamma_{-}\coloneqq(\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\})
and

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{i,j}(t,y,w)\coloneqq\widehat{A}_{i,l}(y)A_{l,k}(\Psi^{-1}(t,y,w))\widehat{A}_{j,k}(y)\quad\text{with}\quad\widehat{A}\coloneqq D\varphi(\varphi^{-1}(y)),

\displaystyle\widetilde{B}_{i}\coloneqq\widehat{A}_{i,j}(y)B_{j}(\Psi^{-1}(t,y,w))+\widehat{A}^{-1}_{j,s}(y)w_{s}\widehat{A}^{-1}_{l,k}w_{k}\partial_{x_{j},x_{l}}\varphi(x),

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}(t,y,w)\coloneqq F(\Psi^{-1}(t,y,w)),\quad\widetilde{g}(t,y,w)\coloneqq g(\Psi^{-1}(t,y,w)).

We note that when the domain is smooth, then the distance function
d_{\Omega}(x)=:\mathrm{dist}(x,\partial\Omega)\in C^{\infty}(\overline{\Omega})
. This allows us to follow every argument given in
[
35
, Section 2.4]
when a regularized distance is replaced by the distance function. In particular, by using the same notation as in
[
35
, Section 2.4]
, we observe
\psi^{-1}(x)\coloneqq(x^{\prime},d_{\Omega}(x))
and
\varphi^{-1}(y)\coloneqq\psi(y^{\prime},0)+y_{n}(\nabla d_{\Omega})(\psi(y))
, where the function
\varphi
given in (
8.1
). Thus, we have the following.

(8.4)

\displaystyle y_{n}=(\varphi(x))_{n}=\big(\varphi\big(\psi(y^{\prime},0)+d_{\Omega}(x)\nabla d_{\Omega}(\psi(y))\big)_{n}=d_{\Omega}(x),

\displaystyle w_{n}=(\nabla\varphi^{-1}(y))^{-1}v)_{n}=(\nabla\varphi(x)v)_{n}=\nabla d_{\Omega}(x)\cdot v=n_{x}\cdot v,

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{n,n}(t,y,w)=(\nabla\varphi(x))_{n,k}A_{k,l}(z)(\nabla\varphi(x))_{n,l},

where
n_{x}\coloneqq\nabla d_{\Omega}(x)
and we have used the fact that for any
x\in\Omega
, there is a boundary point
\psi(y^{\prime},0)\in\partial\Omega
so that
\psi(y^{\prime},d_{\Omega}(x))=x
and
x=\psi(y^{\prime},0)+d_{\Omega}(x)\nabla d_{\Omega}(\psi(y))
with
y=(y^{\prime},d_{\Omega}(x))
.

For a more detailed discussion, we refer to
[
35
, Section 2.4]
.

As we will deal with general domains, we frequently use the following notation:

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}_{r}(z_{1})\coloneqq\{z\,:\,t\in I_{r^{2}}(t_{1}),\,v\in B_{r}(v_{1}),\,x\in B_{r^{3}}(x_{1}+(t-t_{1})v_{1})\cap\Omega\}.

8.1.
Optimal regularity for in-flow and proof of
Theorem 1.3

Now we give the global Hölder regularity with in-flow condition for general domains.

Theorem 8.1
.

Let
\Omega
be a bounded domain of class
C^{2,\epsilon}
for some
\epsilon>0
. Let
f
be a weak solution to

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}){f}-\mathrm{div}_{v}({A}\nabla_{v}f)&={B}\cdot\nabla_{v}f+{F}&&\qquad\mbox{in $(-1,1)\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{n}$},\\
{f}&={g}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where

\displaystyle\sup_{z_{1}\in(-1,1)\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{n}}\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))}+\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))})\leq 1.

Then we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}([-1/8,1/8]\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq c\left(\|\langle v\rangle^{q}f\|_{L^{1}((-1,1)\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|\langle v\rangle^{q}G\|_{L^{\infty}((-1,1)\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{n})}+[g]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}_{q}(\gamma_{-})}\right)

for some constants
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon)
and
q=q(n)
, where we write

\displaystyle[g]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}_{q}(\gamma_{-})}\coloneqq\sup_{z_{0}\in\gamma_{-}}\langle v_{0}\rangle^{q}[g]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap{\mathcal{Q}_{1}(z_{0})})}.

Proof.

First, let us fix
z_{1}\in\gamma
. By (
8.2
) and (
8.3
), we have
\widetilde{f}=f\circ\Psi_{x_{1}}^{-1}
is a solution to (
8.3
) for some
R_{1},R_{2}\leq 1
. By
[
35
, Lemma 2.17]
and since the domain is of class
C^{2,\epsilon}
, we have

(8.5)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{A}\|_{C^{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\|{A}\|_{C^{{\epsilon}}(\Psi^{-1}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})))},

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{B}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c(\|B\|_{L^{{\infty}}(\Psi^{-1}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})))}+\langle v_{0}\rangle^{2}),

\displaystyle\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\|F\|_{L^{{\infty}}(\Psi^{-1}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})))}

\displaystyle[\widetilde{g}]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c[g]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}+{\epsilon}}(\Psi^{-1}((\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0}))))},

where
\Psi\equiv\Psi_{x_{1}}
,
z_{0}\coloneqq\Psi(z_{1})
and
c=c(n,\epsilon,\Lambda)
, whenever
r\leq\langle v_{0}\rangle^{-N}
for some large constant
N=N(n,\epsilon)
. In particular, we choose
N
large enough so that
N\geq\frac{2}{\epsilon}
. Thus, by combining
Theorem 6.2
,
[
35
, Lemma 2.25]
and
subsection 7.1
to estimate the
L^{\infty}
-norm of
f
by its
L^{1}
-norm, we have

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{f}\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\rho_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\langle v_{0}\rangle\left(\rho_{0}^{-(4n+2)}\|\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}+\rho_{0}^{2}\|\widetilde{F}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}+\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}}[\widetilde{g}]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{2\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\langle v_{0}\rangle^{p}\|\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}+\|\widetilde{F}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}+[\widetilde{g}]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{2\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\right),

where
\rho_{0}=\frac{R_{1}}{2}{\langle v_{0}\rangle^{-N}}
and
c=c(n,\epsilon,\Lambda)
.
Now, by scaling back together with
[
35
, Lemma 2.17]
and (
8.2
), there is a constant
r_{1}=\frac{\langle v_{1}\rangle^{-N}}{c_{1}}
for some constant
c_{1}=c_{1}(\Omega)
such that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbf{H}}_{r_{1}}(z_{1}))}\leq c\langle v_{1}\rangle^{q}\left(\|{f}\|_{L^{1}({\mathbf{H}}_{2r_{1}}(z_{1})}+\|{F}\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbf{H}}_{2r_{1}}(z_{1}))}+[{g}]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}+{\epsilon}}(\gamma_{-}\cap{{Q}}_{2r_{1}}(z_{1}))}\right),

where
c=c(n,\epsilon,\Lambda)
and
q=q(n,\epsilon)
. Thus, by a covering argument as in
[
35
, Theorem 5.4]
, we derive the desired estimate.
∎

Proof of
Theorem 1.3
.

The result follows immediately from
Theorem 8.1
and the fact that the smoothness of the solution away from the grazing set is already proved in
[
35
, Theorem 5.6]
.
∎

8.2.
Optimal regularity for diffuse reflection and proof of
Theorem 1.1

Next we provide the proof of global Hölder regularity with diffuse reflection condition for general domains.

Theorem 8.2
.

Let
\Omega
be a bounded domain of class
C^{2,\epsilon}
for some
\epsilon>0
. Let
f
be a weak solution to

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}){f}-\mathrm{div}_{v}({A}\nabla_{v}f)&={B}\cdot\nabla_{v}f+{F}&&\qquad\mbox{in $(-1,1)\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{n}$},\\
{f}&=\mathcal{N}f&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}f(t,x,v)\coloneqq\mathcal{M}(t,x,v)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f(t,x,w)(w\cdot n_{x})_{-}\,dw.

Assume

(8.6)

\displaystyle\sup_{z_{1}\in(-1,1)\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big(\langle v_{1}\rangle^{M}\|\mathcal{M}\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{1}(z_{1}))}+\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))}+\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))}\big)\leq 1

for some
M>0
. There is
M_{0}=M_{0}(n,\epsilon)
such that if
M>M_{0}
, then we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}([-1/8,1/8]\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq c\left(\|\langle v\rangle^{q}f\|_{L^{1}((-1,1)\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|\langle v\rangle^{q}G\|_{L^{\infty}((-1,1)\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{n})}\right)

for some constants
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon)
and
q=q(n)
.

Proof.

First, let us fix
z_{1}\in\gamma
. Then by (
8.3
), we have that
\widetilde{f}
is a weak solution to

(8.7)

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+w\cdot\nabla_{y})\widetilde{f}-\mathrm{div}_{w}(\widetilde{A}\nabla_{w}\widetilde{f})&=\widetilde{B}\cdot\nabla_{w}\widetilde{f}+\widetilde{F}&&\qquad\mbox{in $I\times(B_{\frac{R_{0}}{2}}({x}_{0}^{\prime},0)\cap\{x_{n}>0\})\times\mathbb{R}^{n}$},\\
\widetilde{f}&=\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}\widetilde{f}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\Gamma_{-}\cap(I\times B_{\frac{R_{0}}{2}}(\overline{x}_{0}^{\prime},0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n})$},\end{aligned}\right.

where
I=(t_{0}-(R_{0}/2)^{2},t_{0}+(R_{0}/2)^{2})
with
t_{0}\in I_{\frac{1}{2}}
,
\Gamma_{-}=(\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\})
and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}\widetilde{f}(t,y,w)\coloneqq\mathcal{M}(t,\varphi^{-1}(y),\nabla\varphi^{-1}(y)w)|\nabla\varphi^{-1}(y)|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\widetilde{f}(t,y^{\prime},0,\xi)(\xi_{n})_{-}\,d\xi,

which follows from the change of variables and (
8.4
). Next, as in (
8.5
), there is a large constant
p=p(n,\epsilon)
for which the first three conditions in (
8.5
) are satisfied whenever
r\leq\langle v_{0}\rangle^{-p}
with
z_{0}\coloneqq\Psi(z_{1})
. Moreover, by taking
M=M(n,\epsilon)\geq 1
large so that we observe from (
8.6
) that

(8.8)

\displaystyle\langle v_{2}\rangle^{M}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{1}(z_{0}))}\leq c(\epsilon,\Omega),

where we write

(8.9)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(t,y,w)\coloneqq\mathcal{M}(t,\varphi^{-1}(y),\nabla\varphi^{-1}(y)w)|\nabla\varphi^{-1}(y)|.

This together with (
8.5
) and (
8.6
) allows us to apply
Theorem 7.1
. Hence, we have

(8.10)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{f}\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq cr_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\langle v_{0}\rangle(\|\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2r_{0}}(z_{0}))}+r_{0}^{2}\|\widetilde{F}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2r_{0}}(z_{0}))})

\displaystyle+c\langle v_{0}\rangle\|\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{0}}(z_{0}))}\left(\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{q}\widetilde{f}(t,x,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\infty})}+\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{q}\widetilde{F}(t,x,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\infty})}\right),

where
c=c(n,\epsilon,\Omega)
,
q=q(n,\epsilon)
and we write
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\infty}\coloneqq I\times(B_{\frac{R_{1}}{2}}({x}_{1}^{\prime},0)\cap\{x_{n}>0\})\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Using the change of variables along with
[
35
, Lemma 2.17]
, we can rewrite (
8.10
) as

(8.11)

\displaystyle\|{f}\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{H}_{\rho_{1}}(z_{1}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\langle v_{1}\rangle(\|{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{H}_{2\rho_{1}}(z_{1}))}+\|{F}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{H}_{2\rho_{1}}(z_{1}))})

\displaystyle+c\langle v_{1}\rangle\|{\mathcal{M}}\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{2\rho_{1}}(z_{1}))}\left(\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{q}{f}(t,x,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(I\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{q}{F}(t,x,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(I\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{n})}\right)

for some constants
c=c(n,\epsilon,\Omega)
and
q=q(n,\epsilon)
, where
\rho_{1}=\frac{\langle v_{1}\rangle^{-p}}{c_{1}}
for some constant
c_{1}=c_{1}(\Omega)\geq 1
. Now using the covering argument as in
[
35
, Theorem 5.4]
together with (
8.8
), we derive the desired global
C^{\frac{1}{2}}
estimate from (
8.11
). This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 8.3
.

Note that by the same proof, we could establish
C^{\lambda,\epsilon}
estimates for diffuse reflection near
\gamma
(but away from
\gamma_{0}
), whenever (
7.4
) and (
7.5
) hold for
A,B,F,\mathcal{M}
, using the second claim in
Theorem 7.1
together with the flattening argument. Below, we give the proof only for smooth coefficients.

We now give the proof of
Theorem 1.1
.

Proof of
Theorem 1.1
.

Since the
C^{\frac{1}{2}}
-regularity follows from
Theorem 8.2
, we only give the proof of smoothness of the solution away from
\gamma_{0}
. For any
z_{1}\in\gamma_{-}
, we get that
\widetilde{f}=f\circ\Psi^{-1}
is a weak solution to (
8.7
). Now using the definition (
8.9
), we see that for any
M>0
,
k\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
,
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, and
\mathcal{H}_{r_{2}}(z_{2})\subset I\times(B_{\frac{R_{1}}{2}}({x}_{1}^{\prime},0)\cap\{x_{n}>0\})\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

(8.12)

\displaystyle\langle v_{2}\rangle^{M}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\|_{C^{k,\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{{r_{2}}}(z_{2}))}\leq c(p,k,\epsilon,\Omega,M),

as
\mathcal{M}
has fast decay as
|v|\to\infty
. Next, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, there is a large constant
p=p(n,k,\epsilon,\Omega)
such that if
r_{2}\leq\frac{\langle v_{2}\rangle^{-p}}{c_{2}}
for some constant
c_{2}=c_{2}(n,k,\epsilon,\Omega)
, then

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{A}\|_{C^{k,\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{2}}(z_{2}))}+\frac{1}{\langle v_{2}\rangle^{2}}\|\widetilde{B}\|_{C^{k,\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{2}}(z_{2}))}\leq c(k,\epsilon,\Omega),

where we have used
[
35
, Lemma 2.17]
. Now using this, (
8.12
), and
Theorem 7.1
, we get
f\in C^{k,\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{r_{2}}(z_{2}))
, whenever
\mathcal{H}_{r_{2}}(z_{2})\subset\widetilde{H}_{\infty}
. This implies that
f
is smooth away from the grazing set, which completes the proof.
∎

8.3.
Higher order estimates near
\gamma_{0}
and proofs of
Theorem 1.6
and
Theorem 1.7

Now we are ready to prove our two remaining main theorems.

Proof of
Theorem 1.6
and
Theorem 1.7
.

Let us fix
z_{1}\in\gamma_{0}
. We have (
8.2
) and that
\widetilde{f}
is a weak solution to (
8.3
) with
A=I_{n}
,
B=0
, and
z_{0}=\Psi(z_{1})
. In addition, from (
8.2
) and (
8.4
), we have

\displaystyle\Psi^{-1}({\mathcal{H}}_{\frac{R_{0}}{2}}(z_{0})\cap\{y_{n}\leq w_{n}^{\frac{3}{\epsilon}}\})\subset\mathbf{H}_{R_{1}}(z_{1})\cap\mathcal{R}^{\epsilon}_{-}

for some constants
R_{0},R_{1}\leq 1
depending on
n,|z_{0}|,\Omega
.
Therefore, for the proof of
Theorem 1.6
, it suffices to show that

(8.13)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{f}\|_{C^{3-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{\frac{R_{0}}{2}}(z_{0})\cap\{y_{n}\leq w_{n}^{\frac{3}{\epsilon}}\})}\leq c\left(\|\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(z_{0}))}+[\widetilde{F}]_{C^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(z_{0}))}+[\widetilde{g}]_{C^{3-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(z_{0}))}\right)

for some constant
c=c(n,\epsilon,|z_{0}|,\Omega)
, as the desired estimate follows by scaling back together with
[
35
, Lemma 2.17]
. By
Theorem 6.6
, for any
\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{2})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\frac{R_{0}}{2}}(z_{0})\cap\{y_{n}\leq w_{n}^{\frac{3}{\epsilon}}\}
,

(8.14)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{f}\|_{C^{3-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{2}))}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\|\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\frac{3}{4}R_{0}}(z_{0}))}+[\widetilde{F}]_{C^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{\frac{3}{4}R_{0}}(z_{0}))}+[\widetilde{g}]_{C^{3-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{\frac{3}{4}R_{0}}(z_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\|\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(z_{0}))}+[\widetilde{F}]_{C^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(z_{0}))}+[\widetilde{g}]_{C^{3-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(z_{0}))}\right),

where
c=c(n,\epsilon,|z_{0}|,\Omega)
, as
\|\widetilde{A}\|_{C^{\frac{5}{2}}}
,
\|\widetilde{B}\|_{C^{\frac{3}{2}}}
and
R_{1}
depend only on
n,|z_{0}|
and
\Omega
. In particular, we have also used
[
35
, Lemma 2.25]
and
subsection 7.1
for the last inequality. Now using the covering argument given in
[
35
, Lemma 2.9]
, we derive (
8.13
), which completes the proof of
Theorem 1.6
.

Now we are going to prove
Theorem 1.7
. Note from (
8.4
) that
\widetilde{A}_{n,n}=(\nabla\varphi(x))_{n,k}(\nabla\varphi(x))_{n,k}=|\nabla\varphi(x)|^{2}\equiv 1
. Therefore, by using
Theorem 6.3
and applying the same argument as in (
8.14
), which allows us to replace the
L^{\infty}
-norm of
\widetilde{f}
by its
L^{1}
-norm, we have

(8.15)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\widetilde{f}}{\phi_{0}}\right\|_{C^{0,1}(\mathcal{H}_{\frac{R_{0}}{2}}(z_{0})\setminus\{(v_{n})^{3}\geq x_{n}\})}\leq c\left(\|\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(z_{0}))}+\|\widetilde{F}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(z_{0}))}\right)

for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda,\Omega,|z_{0}|)
. On the other hand, we observe from (
8.4
) that
\varphi(y_{n},w_{n})=\varphi(d_{\Omega}(x),(n_{x}\cdot v))
. Thus using this and (
8.15
) together with the change of variables yields the desired estimate. This completes the proof.
∎

8.4.
Generalization of
Theorem 1.6
and
Theorem 1.7
to equations with coefficients

We end this section by providing a general version of
Theorem 1.6
and
Theorem 1.7
. We consider the broad class of kinetic Fokker-Planck equations as in (
1.2
) with sufficiently smooth coefficients.

First, we state a comprehensive version of
Theorem 1.6
. It follows directly from
Theorem 6.6
by repeating the same argument as in the proof of
Theorem 1.6
, and thus we skip the details.

Theorem 8.4
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a bounded smooth domain and let
z_{1}\in\gamma_{0}
. Let
F\in C^{0,1}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))
,
A\in C^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))
,
B\in C^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))
and
g\in C^{2,1}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{1}(z_{1}))
. Let
f
be a solution to

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}){f}-\mathrm{div}_{v}({A}\nabla_{v}{f})&={B}\cdot\nabla_{v}{f}+{F}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1})$},\\
f&=g&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{1}(z_{1})$}.\end{aligned}\right.

Then for any
\epsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle[f]_{C^{3-\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1/2}(z_{1})\cap\mathcal{R}^{\epsilon}_{-})}\leq c(\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))}+\|F\|_{C^{0,1}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))}+[g]_{C^{2,1}(\mathcal{Q}_{1}(z_{1})\cap\gamma_{-})})

for some constant
c=c(n,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))},\|B\|_{C^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))},|z_{1}|,\Omega)
, where
\mathcal{R}^{\epsilon}_{-}\coloneqq\{d_{\Omega}(x)^{\epsilon}\leq(n_{x}\cdot v)^{3}\}
.

Next, we give a general version of
Theorem 1.7
. To this end, we write for any
z\in I_{2}\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{n}

(8.16)

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{kin}}(z_{1})\coloneqq\max\left\{r\,:\,\mathbf{H}_{r}(z_{1})\cap\gamma_{0}=\emptyset\right\}\eqsim\min\{\mathrm{dist}(z_{1},z)\,:\,z\in\gamma_{0}\}.

We recall that we have assumed that the projection onto the boundary
\Omega
is unique in
\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{0})
. If not, then we can just make the radius smaller. Thus, we may write
n_{x}
as the outer unit normal vector at the projection
\overline{x}\in\partial\Omega
of
x\in\Omega
.

First, we provide a lemma that describes the behavior of the kinetic distance function near
\gamma_{0}
.

Lemma 8.5
.

Let
\Omega
be a bounded convex domain of class
C^{2}
. We fix
z_{0}\in(I_{1}\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap\gamma_{0}
. Then we have for any
z\in\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{0})
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{c}\max\{d_{\Omega}(x)^{\frac{1}{3}},\langle v_{0}\rangle^{-2}|n_{x}\cdot v|\}\leq\mathrm{dist}_{kin}(z)\leq c\max\{d_{\Omega}(x)^{\frac{1}{3}},|n_{x}\cdot v|\}

for some constant
c=c(n,\Omega)
.

Proof.

First, we note that for any
z_{1}\in\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{0})
,

(8.17)

\displaystyle\overline{z}_{1}\coloneqq(t_{1},x_{1}-(n_{x_{1}}\cdot x_{1})n_{x_{1}},v_{1}-(n_{x_{1}}\cdot v_{1})n_{x_{1}})\in\gamma_{0},\quad\mathrm{dist}(z_{1},\overline{z}_{1})\eqsim\max\{d_{\Omega}(x_{1})^{\frac{1}{3}},|n_{x_{1}}\cdot v_{1}|\},

where we have used the fact that
n_{x_{1}}\cdot x_{1}=d_{\Omega}(x_{1})
. This implies that

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{kin}}(z_{1})\lesssim\max\{d_{\Omega}(x_{1})^{\frac{1}{3}},|n_{x_{1}}\cdot v_{1}|\}.

For the lower bound, first we use the convexity condition of the domain. In particular, we are going to prove that for any
z_{2}\in\gamma_{0}
, when
z_{1}\in\mathbf{H}_{r}(z_{2})
, then
d_{\Omega}(x_{1})\leq r^{3}
. To do this, we observe

\displaystyle r^{3}>|x_{1}-(x_{2}+(t_{1}-t_{2})v_{2})|>d_{\Omega}(x_{1}),

as
x_{2}+(t_{1}-t_{2})v_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\Omega
by the fact that
n_{x_{2}}\cdot v_{2}=0
and since the domain is convex. Therefore, if
r<\frac{d_{\Omega}(x_{1})^{\frac{1}{3}}}{16}
, then
\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1})\cap\gamma_{0}=\emptyset
, which implies
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{kin}}(z_{1})\gtrsim{d_{\Omega}(x_{1})^{\frac{1}{3}}}
.

On the other hand, since the domain
\Omega
is smooth, there is a universal constant
c_{0}=c_{0}(\Omega)\geq 1
such that
|n_{x_{1}}-n_{x_{2}}|\leq c_{0}|x_{1}-x_{2}|
. Using this, we can prove for any
r=\frac{|n_{x_{1}}\cdot v_{1}|}{100c_{0}\langle v_{0}\rangle^{2}}
,

(8.18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1})\cap\gamma_{0}=\emptyset.

To prove it, we assume by contradiction that there is
z_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{1})\cap\gamma_{0}
. Now note that

\displaystyle n_{x_{2}}\cdot v_{2}=(n_{x_{2}}-n_{x_{1}})\cdot v_{2}+n_{x_{1}}\cdot(v_{2}-v_{1})+n_{x_{1}}\cdot v_{1}.

We next estimate

\displaystyle|(n_{x_{2}}-n_{x_{1}})\cdot v_{2}+n_{x_{1}}\cdot(v_{2}-v_{1})|

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}|x_{2}-x_{1}||v_{2}|+r

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}|x_{2}-(x_{1}+(t_{1}-t_{2})v_{1})||v_{2}|+|(t_{1}-t_{2})v_{1}||v_{2}|+r

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}(r^{3}\langle v_{2}\rangle+r^{2}\langle v_{1}\rangle^{2}+r),

where we have used the fact that
\frac{\langle v_{0}\rangle}{2}\leq\langle v_{i}\rangle\leq 2\langle v_{0}\rangle
for each
i=1,2
. Therefore, using this and the fact that
r\leq 1
and
r\langle v_{1}\rangle\leq\frac{|n_{x_{1}}\cdot v_{1}|}{50c_{0}\langle v_{0}\rangle}\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle|n_{x_{2}}\cdot v_{2}|\geq|n_{x_{1}}\cdot v_{1}|-c(r^{3}\langle v_{2}\rangle+r^{2}\langle v_{1}\rangle^{2}+r)\geq|n_{x_{1}}\cdot v_{1}|-\frac{1}{2}\frac{|n_{x_{1}}\cdot v_{1}|}{\langle v_{1}\rangle}>0,

This proves (
8.18
) and hence
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{kin}}(z_{1})\gtrsim\frac{|n_{x_{1}}\cdot v_{1}|}{\langle v_{0}\rangle^{2}}
. Therefore, we conclude

\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{kin}}(z_{1})\gtrsim\max\left\{d_{\Omega}(x_{1})^{\frac{1}{3}},\frac{|n_{x_{1}}\cdot v_{1}|}{\langle v_{0}\rangle^{2}}\right\},

which completes the proof.
∎

Now, we are in a position to state and prove the main result of this section. It says that a variant of
Theorem 1.7
remains true for equations with coefficients. In this case, the function
\Phi
does not have an elegant explicit formula, however, we can prove that it behaves like
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathrm{kin}}^{1/2}
up to higher order if
\Omega
is a convex domain.

Theorem 8.6
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a bounded smooth and convex domain, and let
z_{1}\in\gamma_{0}
. Let
F\in L^{\infty}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))
,
A\in C^{k+\epsilon}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))
, and
B\in C^{\epsilon}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))
for some
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\epsilon>0
. Let
f
be a solution to

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}){f}-\mathrm{div}_{v}({A}\nabla_{v}{f})&={B}\cdot\nabla_{v}{f}+{F}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1})$},\\
f&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{1}(z_{1})$}.\end{aligned}\right.

Then, there exists a function
\Phi\in C^{1/2}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))
such that

(8.19)

\displaystyle\Phi\asymp_{\langle v_{1}\rangle}\mathrm{dist}_{\text{kin}}^{1/2},\qquad D^{\beta}\Phi\lesssim_{|v_{1}|}\mathrm{dist}_{\text{kin}}^{1/2-|\beta|}\quad\text{in }\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1})\setminus\mathcal{R}^{1}_{-}

for any
\beta\in\mathbb{N}
with
|\beta|\leq k

and it holds
