\displaystyle\frac{f}{\Phi}\in C^{0,1}(\mathbf{H}_{1/2}(z_{1})\setminus\mathcal{R}^{1}_{-})),

where
\mathcal{R}^{1}_{-}\coloneqq\{d_{\Omega}(x)\leq(n_{x}\cdot v)^{3}\}
.
Moreover, we have the estimate

\displaystyle\|f/\Phi\|_{C^{0,1}(\mathbf{H}_{1/2}(z_{1})\setminus\mathcal{R}_{-}^{1})}\leq C\big(\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))}+\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))}\big),

where
C
depends only on
n,|z_{1}|,\Omega,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{1,\epsilon}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))},\|B\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))}
.

Proof.

As in the proof of (
8.15
), we have for
z_{0}=\Psi(z_{1})
,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\widetilde{f}}{{{\Phi}}}\right\|_{C^{0,1}(\mathcal{H}_{\frac{R_{0}}{2}}(z_{0})\setminus\{(v_{n})^{3}\geq x_{n}\})}\leq c\left(\|\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(z_{0}))}+\|\widetilde{F}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(z_{0}))}\right),

for some constants
c=c(n,|z_{1}|,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{1+\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(z_{0}))},\|B\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{R_{0}}(z_{0}))})
and
R_{0}=R_{0}(|z_{1}|,\Omega)
, where we write
{{\Phi}}(t,y,w)\coloneqq\phi_{0}\left(y_{n}/\sqrt{\widetilde{A}_{n,n}(t,y,w)},w_{n}/\sqrt{\widetilde{A}_{n,n}(t,y,w)}\right)
. Thus, using the observation
\widetilde{A}_{n,n}(t,y,w)=(\nabla\varphi(x))_{n,k}A_{k,l}(z)(\nabla\varphi(x))_{n,l}
from (
8.4
) and the change of variables, we have

\displaystyle\|f/\Phi\|_{C^{0,1}(\mathbf{H}_{1/2}(z_{1})\setminus\mathcal{R}_{-}^{1})}\leq C\big(\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))}+\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))}\big),

for some constants
c=c(n,|z_{1}|,\|A\|_{C^{1,\epsilon}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))},\|B\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))}),\Omega
. To investigate the behavior
\Phi
, we rewrite
\Phi
as

\displaystyle{{\Phi}}(t,x,v)=\phi_{0}\left(d_{\Omega}(x)G(z),(n_{x}\cdot v)G(z)\right)\quad\text{with}\quad G(z)\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{(\nabla\varphi(x))_{n,k}A_{k,l}(z)(\nabla\varphi(x))_{n,l}}}.

Note that
G(z)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))
with

(8.20)

\displaystyle c(k,\Omega,\Lambda,\|A\|_{C^{k}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{1}))})^{-1}\leq\|G\|_{C^{k}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1}))}\leq c(k,\Omega,\Lambda,\|A\|_{C^{k}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(z_{1}))}),

as
A
is positive symmetric and
|\nabla\varphi|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{c}
for some constant
c=c(\Omega)
.
First, from (
C.37
) with
m=0
and
subsection 8.4
, we derive

\displaystyle{\Phi}(z)\asymp\max\{d_{\Omega}(x)^{\frac{1}{3}},|n_{x}\cdot v|\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\asymp_{\langle v_{1}\rangle}\mathrm{dist}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{kin}}(z)\quad\text{for any }z\in\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1})\setminus\mathcal{R}^{1}_{-}.

For the second relation given in (
8.19
), we observe that since
\phi_{0}
is a weak solution to
(\partial_{t}+v_{n}\partial_{x_{n}})\phi_{0}-\Delta_{v_{n}}\phi_{0}=0
, by
subsection 2.2
, we have for any
|v_{1,n}|\leq 2(x_{1,n})^{\frac{1}{3}}
,

\displaystyle|D^{\beta}\phi_{0}(x_{1,n},v_{1,n})|\leq c\rho^{-\beta}\|\phi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(0,x_{1,n},v_{1,n}))},

where
c=c(\beta)
and
\rho\coloneqq\frac{(x_{1,n})^{\frac{1}{3}}}{64}
. By (
C.36
), we have

(8.21)

\displaystyle|D^{\beta}\phi_{0}(x_{1,n},v_{1,n})|\leq c(x_{1,n})^{-\frac{\beta}{3}+\frac{1}{6}}.

Next for any
(-v_{1,n})\geq 2(x_{1,n})^{\frac{1}{3}}
, by
subsection 3.3
with
v_{0}
and
R
replaced by
v_{1,n}
and
R=\frac{v_{1,n}}{2}
, respectively, we obtain

(8.22)

\displaystyle|D^{\beta}\phi_{0}(x_{1,n},v_{1,n})|\leq\|D^{\beta}\phi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{R}(0,v_{1,n}))}\leq c|v_{1,n}|^{-\beta+\frac{1}{2}},

where we have used (
C.36
). By (
8.21
) and (
8.22
), we get

\displaystyle|D^{\beta}\phi_{0}(x_{1,n},v_{1,n})|\leq c\max\{(x_{1,n})^{\frac{1}{3}},|v_{1,n}|\}^{-\beta+\frac{1}{2}}

for some constant
c=c(\beta)
. Now using this and (
8.20
) together with the fact that
d_{\Omega}(x)
and
n_{x}\cdot v
are smooth, we have

\displaystyle|D^{\beta}\Phi(z)|\leq c\max\{d_{\Omega}(x)^{\frac{1}{3}},|(n_{x}\cdot v)|\}^{-\beta+\frac{1}{2}},

where
c=c(n,\beta,A,\Omega)
. Combining this and
subsection 8.4
leads to the desired estimate

\displaystyle D^{\beta}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}\leq c\mathrm{dist}_{kin}^{\frac{1}{2}-\beta}\quad\text{in }\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{1})\setminus\mathcal{R}_{-}^{1}

for some constant
c=c(\beta,n,\Omega,A,\langle v_{1}\rangle)
,
which completes the proof.
∎

Appendix A
Functions depending only on time and spatial variables

Here, we provide some basic facts about functions
g=g(t,x^{\prime})
, i.e. which depend only on
t,x^{\prime}
(whenever
\Omega=\{x_{n}>0\}
), with respect to kinetic Hölder spaces. These results are required for the proof of
section 7
.

•

For any
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,

(A.1)

\displaystyle[g]_{C^{\lambda,\epsilon}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq c\left([\partial_{x}^{l}g]_{C_{x}^{\frac{\lambda+\epsilon-3l}{3}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0}))}+\sum_{j=0}^{l-1}[\partial_{x}^{j}\mathcal{D}_{t}^{k_{j}}g]_{C^{\lambda+\epsilon-(2k_{j}+3j)}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0}))}\right),

where
\mathcal{D}\coloneqq\partial_{t}+v^{\prime}\cdot\nabla_{x^{\prime}}
,
c=c(n,\lambda,\epsilon)
,
\lambda-3<3l\leq\lambda
and
\lambda-2<2k_{j}+3j\leq\lambda
.

•

For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and
r\leq 1
,

(A.2)

\displaystyle[g]_{C_{t}^{\frac{\epsilon}{3}}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0}))}+[g]_{C_{x}^{\frac{\epsilon}{3}}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq c\langle v_{0}\rangle^{\frac{\epsilon}{3}}[g]_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0}))},

where
c=c(n,\epsilon)
and

\displaystyle[g]_{C_{t}^{\frac{\epsilon}{3}}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0}))}=\sup_{(t,x,v),(\widehat{t},x,v)\in\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})}\frac{|g(t,x^{\prime},v)-g(\widehat{t},x^{\prime},v)|}{|t-\widehat{t}|^{\frac{\epsilon}{3}}},

\displaystyle[g]_{C_{x}^{\frac{\epsilon}{3}}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0}))}=\sup_{(t,x^{\prime},v),(t,\widehat{x},v)\in\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0})}\frac{|g(t,x^{\prime},v)-g(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},v)|}{|x-\widehat{x}|^{\frac{\epsilon}{3}}}.

•

For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, we have

(A.3)

\displaystyle\sup_{0<|h|<\frac{r}{2}}\left\|\frac{g(t+h,x^{\prime},v)-g(t,x^{\prime},v)}{|h|^{\frac{\epsilon}{3}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq c[g]_{C_{t}^{\frac{\epsilon}{3}}(\mathcal{H}_{2r}(z_{0}))}.

Proof.

To prove (
A.1
), for
v_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
,
X^{\prime}=(t,x^{\prime},v_{1}^{\prime})
and
X_{1}^{\prime}=(t_{1},x_{1}^{\prime},v_{1}^{\prime})
, we observe

(A.4)

\displaystyle|(g-p_{X_{1}^{\prime}})(X^{\prime})|\leq|(g-p_{X_{1}^{\prime}})(X^{\prime})-(g-p_{\overline{X}_{1}^{\prime}})(X^{\prime})|+|(g-p_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{1}})(X^{\prime})|\eqqcolon J_{1}+J_{2},

where
\overline{X}_{1}^{\prime}=(t,\overline{x}_{1}^{\prime},v_{1}^{\prime})
with
\overline{x}^{\prime}_{1}\coloneqq x_{1}^{\prime}+(t-t_{1})v_{1}^{\prime}
, and
p_{X_{1}^{\prime}}
and
p_{\overline{X}_{1}^{\prime}}
are
k
-order Taylor polynomial of
g
at
X_{1}^{\prime}
and
\overline{X}_{1}^{\prime}
, respectively. Note from
[
27
, (2.3) in Definition 2.3]
that

\displaystyle p_{(t,x^{\prime},v^{\prime})}((t,x^{\prime},v^{\prime})\circ(t_{2},x_{2}^{\prime},0))=\sum_{2i+3j\leq k}\mathcal{D}^{i}\nabla_{x^{\prime}}^{j}g(t,x^{\prime},v^{\prime})(t_{2})^{i}\cdot(x_{2}^{\prime})^{j},

where
p_{(t,x^{\prime},v^{\prime})}
is
k
-order Taylor polynomial of
g
at
(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},v^{\prime})
. Using this and the following observations

X^{\prime}=X_{1}^{\prime}\circ(t-t_{1},x^{\prime}-\overline{x}_{1}^{\prime},0)
and
X^{\prime}=\overline{X}_{1}^{\prime}\circ(0,x^{\prime}-\overline{x}_{1}^{\prime},0)
, we have

(A.5)

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{l}|x^{\prime}-\overline{x}_{1}^{\prime}|^{j}|\partial_{x}^{j}g(t_{1},x_{1}^{\prime})-\partial_{x}^{j}g(t,\overline{x}_{1}^{\prime})-(\sum_{i=1}^{k_{j}}\partial_{x}^{j}\mathcal{D}^{i}g(t_{1},x_{1}^{\prime})(t-t_{1})^{i}|,

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle\leq|g(t,x^{\prime})-g(t,\overline{x}_{1}^{\prime})-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\partial_{x}^{j}g(t,\overline{x}_{1}^{\prime})(x-\overline{x}_{1}^{\prime})^{j}|,

where
k-2<2k_{j}+3j\leq k
and
k-3<3m\leq k
. By the fundamental theorem of calculus, we have

\displaystyle J_{1}+J_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{l-1}|t-t_{1}|^{k_{j}}|x^{\prime}-x_{1}^{\prime}-(t-t_{1}^{\prime})v_{1}^{\prime}|^{j+\frac{(k+\epsilon)-(2k_{j}+3l)}{2}}[\mathcal{D}^{k_{j}}\partial_{x}^{j}g]_{C^{(k+\epsilon)-(2k_{j}+3j)}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0}))},

\displaystyle\quad+|x^{\prime}-x_{1}^{\prime}-(t-t_{1}^{\prime})v_{1}^{\prime}|^{\frac{k+\epsilon}{3}}[\partial_{x}^{l}g]_{C_{x}^{\frac{k+\epsilon-3m}{3}}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{r}(z_{0}))},

which implies (
A.1
).

On the other hand, (
A.2
) follows from

\displaystyle\frac{\left(|t-t^{\prime}|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|x-x^{\prime}-(t-t^{\prime})v|^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)^{\epsilon}}{|t-t^{\prime}|^{\frac{\epsilon}{3}}+|x-x^{\prime}|^{\frac{\epsilon}{3}}}\leq c\langle v\rangle^{\frac{\epsilon}{3}}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix B
Perturbation argument with unbounded right-hand side

Here, we give the proof of
subsection 2.2
via the perturbation argument based on
[
30
]
.

Proof of
subsection 2.2
.

We assume that
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
is a one-sided cylinder, as the estimates on two-sided cylinders directly follow as in
[
35
, Lemma 2.23]
. Let us fix
r\leq\frac{1}{4}
. Let
f_{1}
be a weak solution to

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f_{1}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}f_{1})&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{Q}_{{r}}$},\\
f_{1}&=f&&\qquad\mbox{in $\partial_{\mathrm{kin}}\mathcal{Q}_{{r}}$},\end{aligned}\right.

and let
f_{2}
be a weak solution to

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})f_{2}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A(0)\nabla_{v}f_{2})&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r}{2}}$},\\
f_{2}&=f_{1}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\partial_{\mathrm{kin}}\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r}{2}}$}.\end{aligned}\right.

Note that
f-f_{1}
solves

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})(f-f_{1})-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}(f-f_{1}))=B\nabla_{v}f+F-\mathrm{div}_{v}(G)\quad\text{in }\mathcal{Q}_{r},

and satisfies
f-f_{1}=0
on
\partial_{\mathrm{kin}}\mathcal{Q}_{r}
. Therefore we get by testing the equation for
f-f_{1}
with
f-f_{1}
itself,

(B.1)

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}|\nabla_{v}(f-f_{1})|^{2}\,dz\leq c\left(r^{2}\|B\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r})}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}|\nabla_{v}f|^{2}\,dz+r^{4}\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r})}+r^{2+2\epsilon}[G]_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{Q}_{r})}\right),

where we have used
-\mathrm{div}_{v}(G)=-\mathrm{div}_{v}(G-G(0))
, as well as Poincaré’s inequality, which yields

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}|f-f_{1}|^{2}\,dz\leq cr^{2}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}|\nabla_{v}(f-f_{1})|^{2}\,dz

and allows to absorb the term on the right-hand side to the left-hand side in (
B.1
).
Next by
[
30
, Lemma 4.6]
and (
B.1
), we have

(B.2)

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r}{2}}}|\nabla_{v}(f_{1}-f_{2})|^{2}\,dz\leq cr^{2\epsilon}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}|\nabla_{v}f_{1}|^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq c(r^{2\epsilon}+r^{2}\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r})}){\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}|\nabla_{v}f|^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle+c\left(r^{4}\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r})}+r^{2+2\epsilon}[G]_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{Q}_{r})}\right),

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{Q}_{1})})
.
Now using (
B.1
) and (
B.2
) and following the same lines as in the proof of
[
30
, (5.33) in Lemma 5.7]
with
w=f_{1}
,
g=f_{2}
, and
\alpha=\beta=\epsilon
, we derive

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}}|\nabla_{v}f-(\nabla_{v}f)_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho}}|^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq c(\rho/r)^{4n+2+2\epsilon}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}|\nabla_{v}f-(\nabla_{v}f)_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}|^{2}\,dz+c(r^{2\epsilon}+r^{2}\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r})})\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}|\nabla_{v}f|^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle+c\left(r^{4n+2+2\epsilon}[G]_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{Q}_{r})}+r^{4n+2+2}\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r})}\right)

for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{Q}_{1})})
. Now by applying a technical iteration lemma as in
[
30
, Lemma 5.7]
, we get

(B.3)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{v}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{r})}\leq c(\|\nabla_{v}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{1}{2}})}+\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{1}{2}})}+[G]_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{1}{2}})}).

On the other hand, by
subsection 2.2
and
[
12
]
, we have for any
\delta\in(0,1)
and any affine function
l=a+b\cdot v
,

\displaystyle[f_{2}]_{C_{\ell}^{1+\delta}(\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r}{4}})}\leq cr^{-(1+\delta)}\|f_{2}-l\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{3r}{8}})}\leq cr^{-(1+\delta)}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r}{2}}}|(f_{2}-l)|^{2}\,dz,

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\delta)
, as
f_{2}-l
is also a weak solution. Therefore, by taking
l=f_{2}(0)+\nabla_{v}f_{2}(0)\cdot v
and using the definition of the kinetic Hölder space
C^{1+\delta}
, we derive for any
\rho\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
,

(B.4)

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho r}}|f_{2}-l|^{2}\,dz\leq c\rho^{2(1+\delta)}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r}{2}}}|f_{2}-l|^{2}\,dz.

Now we want to estimate the following terms.

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho r}}|f-l|^{2}\,dz\leq c{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho r}}|f-f_{1}|^{2}\,dz+c{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho r}}|f_{1}-f_{2}|^{2}\,dz+c{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho r}}|f_{2}-l|^{2}\,dz\eqqcolon\sum_{i=1}^{3}J_{i}.

Using Poincaré’s inequality and (
B.1
), we estimate
J_{1}
as

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\rho^{-(4n+2)}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}|f-f_{1}|^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq\rho^{-(4n+2)}r^{2}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}|\nabla_{v}(f-f_{1})|^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq c\rho^{-(4n+2)}r^{2}\left(r^{2}\|B\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r})}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}|\nabla_{v}f|^{2}\,dz+r^{4}\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r})}+r^{2+2\epsilon}[G]_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{Q}_{r})}\right).

The estimate of
J_{2}
follows directly from (
B.2
). For the term
J_{3}
, we use (
B.4
) to see that

\displaystyle J_{3}\leq c\rho^{2(1+\delta)}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}|f_{2}-l|^{2}\,dz\leq c\rho^{2(1+\delta)}\left(J_{1}+J_{2}+{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}|f-l|^{2}\,dz\right).

Altogether, we deduce

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{\rho r}}|f-l|^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq c\rho^{2(1+\delta)}{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}|f-l|^{2}\,dz+c\rho^{-(4n+2)}(r^{4}\|B\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r})}+r^{2+2\epsilon})\|\nabla_{v}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r})}

\displaystyle\quad+c\rho^{-(4n+2)}r^{4}\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r})}+r^{2+2\epsilon}[G]_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{Q}_{r})}.

Now we choose
\delta=\frac{1+\epsilon}{2}
and
\rho=\rho(n,\Lambda,\epsilon)
such that

(B.5)

\displaystyle\rho^{2+2\delta-(2+2\epsilon)}=\rho^{\frac{1-\epsilon}{2}}\leq\frac{1}{4c}.

Then, as in the proof of
[
30
, Lemma 5.6]
together with (
B.3
), we get for any
r\leq\frac{1}{4}

(B.6)

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}\frac{|f-l|^{2}}{r^{2(1+\epsilon)}}\,dz\leq c(\|\nabla_{v}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{1}{2}})}+\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{1}{2}})}+[G]_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{1}{2}})}),

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{Q}_{1})},\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{1})})
. Lastly, note that
\widetilde{f}\coloneqq f-l
solves

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{v})\widetilde{f}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}\widetilde{f})=B\cdot\nabla_{v}\widetilde{f}+\widetilde{F}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(\widetilde{G}),

where
\widetilde{F}\coloneqq F+B\cdot\nabla_{v}f(0)
and
\widetilde{G}\coloneqq G-A\nabla_{v}f(0)
. Thus, using
[
12
]
, we derive

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{f}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r}{2}})}

\displaystyle\leq c({\mathchoice{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-9.7pt}{\kern 4.30554pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.4pt,width=4.3pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-7.0pt}{}{}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{r}}|\widetilde{f}|^{2}\,dz+r^{4}\|\widetilde{F}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{r})}+r^{2+2\epsilon}[\widetilde{G}]_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{Q}_{r})}).

Now combining this, (
B.3
), and (
B.6
) together with energy estimates implies that for any
r\leq\frac{1}{4}
,

\displaystyle\|{f}-l\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r}{2}})}\leq cr^{1+\epsilon}(\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{1}{2}})}+\|F\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{1}{2}})}+[G]_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{1}{2}})}).

This yields the desired estimate.
∎

Appendix C
Properties of 1D solutions with in-flow condition

The goal of this section is to obtain an infinite countable family of homogeneous solutions to Kolmogorov’s equation in 1D in the half-space with absorbing and in-flow boundary condition and to establish some fine properties of these solutions. For our main theorem, we only rely on these results for
\phi_{0},\phi_{\pm 1},\psi_{0}
. Although some of the results might have already been established in the literature, we decided to give proofs that are as self-contained as possible since we believe that such a presentation might be of independent interest.

Our analysis is based on the representation of solutions in terms of Tricomi confluent hypergeometric functions
U(a,b,z)
. First, let us recall several basic properties of
U(a,b,z)
, which can be found in
[
7
]
and
[
1
]
:

(C.1)

\displaystyle U^{\prime}(a,b,z)=-aU(a+1,b+1,z),

(C.2)

z(a+1)U(a+2,b+2,z)+(z-b)U(a+1,b+1,z)-U(a,b,z)=0,

(C.3)

\displaystyle zU^{\prime}(a,b,z)=a\big[(1+a-b)U(a+1,b,z)-U(a,b,z)\big],

(C.4)

a(a-b+1)U(a+1,b,z)+(b-2a-z)U(a,b,z)+U(a-1,b,z)=0,

(C.5)

U(a,b,z)=\frac{\Gamma(1-b)}{\Gamma(a-b+1)}+O(z^{1-b})\quad\text{ as }z\to 0,\quad\text{if }0<b<1,\quad

(C.6)

U(a,b,z)=\frac{\Gamma(b-1)}{\Gamma(a)}z^{1-b}+\frac{\Gamma(1-b)}{\Gamma(a-b+1)}+O(z^{2-b}),\quad\text{ as }z\to 0,\quad\text{if }1<b<2,

(C.7)

U(a,b,z)=\frac{\Gamma(b-1)}{\Gamma(a)}z^{1-b}+O(z^{2-b}),\quad\text{ as }z\to 0,\quad\text{if }2<b,

(C.8)

U(a,b,z)\leq c(a,b)z^{-a}\quad\text{when }z\gg 1,

(C.9)

U(a,b,z)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(a)}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-z\xi}\xi^{a-1}(1+\xi)^{b-a-1}\,d\xi\quad\text{for any }a>0,\quad z>0,

(C.10)

U(a,b,z)=z^{1-b}U(a-b+1,2-b,z),

Moreover, note that
U(a,b,z)
is analytic on the complex plane cut along
(-\infty,0]
.

C.1.
1D solutions with absorbing boundary condition

Using these formulas, we find a family of solutions to Kolmogorov’s equation in 1D with absorbing boundary condition. Note that the function
\phi_{0}
has already appeared in the literature; see, for instance,
[
13
]
.

Lemma C.1
.

For any integer
m\geq-1
, let us define

(C.11)

\displaystyle\phi_{m}(x,v)\coloneqq\begin{cases}c_{m}x^{\lambda_{m}}U\left(-\frac{1}{6}-m,\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)&\quad\text{if }v<0,\\
c_{m}M_{m}x^{\lambda_{m}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}U\left(\frac{5}{6}+m,\frac{2}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)&\quad\text{if }v\geq 0,\end{cases}

where we write

(C.12)

\displaystyle c_{m}\coloneqq\begin{cases}1&\quad\text{if }m=-1,\\
(-1)^{m}&\quad\text{if }m\geq 0,\end{cases}

and

\lambda_{m}\coloneqq 1/6+m\quad\text{and}\quad M_{m}=\frac{\Gamma(7/6+m)}{\Gamma(1/6-m)}.

Then
\phi_{m}
is a classical solution to

(C.13)

\left\{\begin{aligned} v\partial_{x}\phi_{m}-\partial_{vv}\phi_{m}&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x>0\}\times\mathbb{R}$},\\
\phi_{m}&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x=0\}\times\{v>0\}$}.\end{aligned}\right.

In addition, for any
m\geq 0
,
\phi_{m}
are weak solutions to (
C.13
)
and
\phi_{-1}
is a weak solution to

(C.14)

\displaystyle v\partial_{x}\phi_{-1}-\partial_{vv}\phi_{-1}=0\quad\text{in }((1/R^{3},\infty)\times\mathbb{R})\cup((0,\infty)\times(-\infty,-1/R))

for any
R>0
.

Remark C.2
.

Note that because of the constant
c_{m}
, it holds
\phi_{m}\geq 0
when
v\geq 0
.

Remark C.3
.

The reader might wonder, whether it is possible to find solutions to (
C.13
) with another homogeneity than
\lambda_{m}=\frac{1}{6}+m
. We show in
subsection 3.3
that this is impossible. However, note that by replacing the values
\lambda_{m}
by
a
, as well as
-\frac{1}{6}-m
and
\frac{5}{6}+m
in (
C.11
) by
-a<0
and
\frac{2}{3}+a
, respectively, the corresponding function
\phi
will still solve (
C.13
) except for
\{v=0\}
. However, it is impossible to find
M
such that
\phi
is
C^{1}
across
\{v=0\}
. More precisely, if we consider a function

(C.15)

\displaystyle\phi(x,v)\coloneqq\begin{cases}x^{a}U\left(-a,\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)&\quad\text{if }v\leq 0,\\
Mx^{a}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}U\left(\frac{2}{3}+a,\frac{2}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)&\quad\text{if }v>0\end{cases}

for some constant
M>0
, then
\phi
satisfies (
C.13
) except for
\{v=0\}
. However, to ensure
\phi\in C^{1}
at
\{v=0\}
, the constant
M
has to satisfy

\displaystyle\lim_{v\nearrow 0}\phi(x,v)=\lim_{v\nearrow 0}x^{a}\frac{\Gamma(1/3)}{\Gamma(-a+1/3)}=\lim_{v\searrow 0}Mx^{a}\frac{\Gamma(1/3)}{\Gamma(a+1)}=\lim_{v\searrow 0}\phi(x,v),

and

\displaystyle\lim_{v\nearrow 0}\partial_{v}\phi(x,v)

\displaystyle=\lim_{v\nearrow 0}ax^{a}\left(-\frac{v^{2}}{3x}\right)\left(\frac{(-v)^{3}}{9x}\right)^{-\frac{2}{3}}\frac{\Gamma(2/3)}{\Gamma(-a+1)}

\displaystyle=\lim_{v\searrow 0}-Mx^{a}\left(\frac{2}{3}+a\right)\left(\frac{v^{2}}{3x}\right)\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{-\frac{2}{3}}\frac{\Gamma(2/3)}{\Gamma(a+5/3)}=\lim_{v\searrow 0}\partial_{v}\phi(x,v),

where we have used (
C.5
) and (
C.6
).
This is equivalent to

(C.16)

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(a+1)}{\Gamma(-a+1/3)}=\frac{a}{\frac{2}{3}+a}\frac{\Gamma(a+5/3)}{\Gamma(-a+1)}=-\frac{\Gamma(a+2/3)}{\Gamma(-a)}.

We now consider the following three cases.

•

a,a+\frac{2}{3}\notin\mathbb{Z}
. Then by the fact that

(C.17)

\Gamma(\xi)\Gamma(1-\xi)=\frac{\pi}{\sin(\pi\xi)}
when
\xi\notin\mathbb{Z}
,

we have

\displaystyle-\Gamma(1-(-a))\Gamma(-a)=-\frac{\pi}{\sin(\pi(-a))}\quad\text{and}\quad\Gamma(1-(a+2/3))\Gamma(a+2/3)=\frac{\pi}{\sin(\pi(a+2/3))}.

To get (
C.16
), it should be
(a+a+2/3)=1+2k
for any
k\geq 0
, which gives
a=k+1/6
.

•

a\in\mathbb{Z}
and
a+\frac{2}{3}\notin\mathbb{Z}
. Then we get

\displaystyle\Gamma(1-(-a))\Gamma(-a)\in\mathbb{Z}\quad\text{and}\quad\Gamma(1-(a+2/3))\Gamma(a+2/3)=\frac{\pi}{\sin(\pi(a+2/3))}=\frac{\pi}{\pm\sqrt{3}/2}\notin\mathbb{Z},

which implies that (
C.16
) never happens.

•

a\notin\mathbb{Z}
and
a+\frac{2}{3}\in\mathbb{Z}
. Similarly, we get that (
C.16
) is impossible.

Hence, we find that for all solutions to (
C.13
) of the form (
C.15
), it must be
a=\frac{1}{6}+m
.

Special care is required in the proof of
subsection C.1
due to the branch cut of
U
in
(-\infty,0]
.

Proof of
subsection C.1
.

First, we claim that for any
m\geq-1
, the functions

\displaystyle U\left(-\frac{1}{6}-m,\frac{2}{3},z\right)\quad\text{and}\quad e^{z}U\left(\frac{5}{6}+m,\frac{2}{3},-z\right)

solve Kummer’s equation

(C.18)

\left(\frac{1}{6}+m\right)\Psi_{m}(z)+\left(\frac{2}{3}-z\right)\Psi_{m}^{\prime}(z)+z\Psi_{m}^{\prime\prime}(z)=0.

in
z>0
and
z<0
, respectively.
This seems to be a standard fact for the first function (see
[
7
, 13.2.6]
) but is less standard for the second one. Hence, we compute it by hand. First, we observe that by (
C.1
)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dz}\left(e^{z}U\left(a,b,-z\right)\right)

\displaystyle=e^{z}U\left(a,b,-z\right)-e^{z}U^{\prime}\left(a,b,-z\right)

\displaystyle=e^{z}\big(U\left(a,b,-z\right)+aU\left(a+1,b+1,-z\right)\big),

and

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{dz^{2}}\big(e^{z}U\left(a,b,-z\right)\big)

\displaystyle=e^{z}\big(U\left(a,b,-z\right)+2aU\left(a+1,b+1,-z\right)-aU^{\prime}(a+1,b+1,-z)\big)

\displaystyle=e^{z}\big(U\left(a,b,-z\right)+2aU\left(a+1,b+1,-z\right)+a(a+1)U(a+2,b+2,-z)\big).

We now set
a_{m}=\frac{5}{6}+m
and
b=\frac{2}{3}
and compute the left-hand side in (
C.18
) with
\Psi_{m}(z)=e^{z}U\left(a_{m},b,-z\right)
. This yields,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{6}+m\right)e^{z}U\left(a_{m},b,-z\right)+\left(\frac{2}{3}-z\right)\left(e^{z}U\left(a_{m},b,-z\right)\right)^{\prime}+z\left(e^{z}U\left(a_{m},b,-z\right)\right)^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=e^{z}\left[\left(\frac{1}{6}+m\right)U\left(a_{m},b,-z\right)+\left(\frac{2}{3}-z\right)\big(U\left(a_{m},b,-z\right)+a_{m}U\left(a_{m+1},b+1,-z\right)\big)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+ze^{z}\big[U\left(a_{m},b,-z\right)+2a_{m}U\left(a_{m+1},b+1,-z\right)+a_{m}a_{m+1}U\left(a_{m+2},b+2,-z\right)\big]

\displaystyle=e^{z}\left(a_{m}U\left(a_{m},b,-z\right)+a_{m}\left(\frac{2}{3}+z\right)U\left(a_{m+1},b+1,-z\right)+za_{m}a_{m+1}U\left(a_{m+2},b+2,-z\right)\right)

\displaystyle=-a_{m}e^{-z}\left(-U\left(a_{m},b,-z\right)+\left(-z-\frac{2}{3}\right)U\left(a_{m}+1,b+1,-z\right)+(-z)(a_{m}+1)U\left(a_{m}+2,b+2,-z\right)\right)

\displaystyle=0,

where we have applied (
C.2
) in the last line.
This implies that
e^{z}U\left(a_{m},b,-z\right)
is a solution to (
C.18
) for
z\in(-\infty,0)
. Similarly, one can show that also
U(-\frac{1}{6}-m,\frac{2}{3},z)
is a solution to (
C.18
) for
z\in(0,\infty)
.

It follows from an easy computation (see e.g.
[
35
]
) that
x^{\frac{1}{6}+m}\Psi_{m}(-v^{3}/(9x))
is a solution to
v\partial_{x}g-\partial_{vv}g=0
, whenever
\Psi_{m}
solves (
C.18
). Hence, we get that

(C.19)

g_{m,1}(x,v)\coloneqq x^{\frac{1}{6}+m}U\left(-\frac{1}{6}-m,\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\quad\text{for }(x,v)\in D_{1}:=\{x>0\}\times\{v<0\},

and

(C.20)

g_{m,2}(x,v)\coloneqq M_{m}x^{\frac{1}{6}+m}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}U\left(\frac{5}{6}+m,\frac{2}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\quad\text{for }(x,v)\in D_{2}:=\{x>0\}\times\{v>0\}

are solutions to

v\partial_{x}g_{m,i}-\partial_{vv}g_{m,i}=0\quad\text{in }D_{i}

for each
i\in\{1,2\}
.

In order to conclude that
\phi_{m}
defined in (
C.11
) are classical solutions, it remains to check that

(C.21)

\displaystyle g_{m,1}(x,0)=g_{m,2}(x,0),\quad\partial_{x}g_{m,1}(x,0)=\partial_{x}g_{m,2}(x,0),\quad\partial_{v}g_{m,1}(x,0)=\partial_{v}g_{m,2}(x,0),

and

(C.22)

\displaystyle\partial_{vv}g_{m,1}(x,0)=\partial_{vv}g_{m,2}(x,0)=0.

•

For the first claim in (
C.21
) we recall
\lambda_{m}=1/6+m
and
M_{m}=\Gamma(7/6+m)/\Gamma(1/6-m)
. By (
C.5
) and
\Gamma(z+1)=z\Gamma(z)
,

\displaystyle g_{m,1}(x,0)=x^{\lambda_{m}}\frac{\Gamma(1/3)}{\Gamma(1/6-m)}=x^{\lambda_{m}}\frac{\Gamma(1/3)}{\Gamma(7/6+m)}M_{m}=g_{m,2}(x,0).

•

For the second claim in (
C.21
) we recall
a_{m}=5/6+m
and
b=2/3
.
Next, using (
C.5
), (
C.6
), and (
C.1
), we derive

\displaystyle\partial_{x}g_{m,1}(x,v)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{m}x^{\lambda_{m}-1}U\left(-\lambda_{m},b,-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)+x^{\lambda_{m}}\frac{v^{3}}{9x^{2}}U^{\prime}\left(-\lambda_{m},b,-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{m}x^{\lambda_{m}-1}\frac{\Gamma(1/3)}{\Gamma(-\lambda_{m}-b+1)}+O(v).

Similarly, we get

\displaystyle\partial_{x}g_{m,2}(x,v)=M_{m}\lambda_{m}x^{\lambda_{m}-1}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}U\left(a_{m},b,-z\right)+O(v),

which implies

\displaystyle\partial_{x}g_{m,1}(x,0)=\frac{\Gamma(1/3)}{\Gamma(1/6-m)}\lambda_{m}x^{\lambda_{m}-1}=\partial_{x}g_{m,2}(x,0).

•

For the third claim in (
C.21
) we observe that by (
C.5
), (
C.6
), and (
C.1
),

(C.23)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}g_{m,1}(x,v)=-x^{\lambda_{m}}\frac{v^{2}}{3x}U^{\prime}\left(-\lambda_{m},b,-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)

\displaystyle={\underbrace{-\lambda_{m}x^{\lambda_{m}}\frac{v^{2}}{3x}U\left(-\lambda_{m}+1,b+1,-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)}_{J_{1}}}

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{m}\frac{\Gamma(b)}{\Gamma(-\lambda_{m}+1)}{x^{\lambda_{m}}}\frac{v^{2}}{3x}\left(-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{-\frac{2}{3}}+O(v),

and

(C.24)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}g_{m,2}(x,v)

\displaystyle={\underbrace{M_{m}x^{\lambda_{m}}\left[\left(-\frac{v^{2}}{3x}\right)e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}U\left(a_{m},b,\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)+e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\frac{v^{2}}{3x}U^{\prime}\left(a_{m},b,\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\right]}_{J_{2}}}

\displaystyle=(-a_{m})M_{m}x^{\lambda_{m}}\frac{v^{2}}{3x}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}U\left(a_{m}+1,b+1,\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)+O(v)

\displaystyle=-M_{m}a_{m}\frac{\Gamma(b)}{\Gamma(a_{m}+1)}x^{\lambda_{m}}\frac{v^{2}}{3x}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{-\frac{2}{3}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}+O(v).

Before we compare
\partial_{v}g_{m,1}(x,0)
and
\partial_{v}g_{m,2}(x,0)
, we note

\displaystyle-a_{l}=(-5/6-l)=(1/6-(l+1))=\lambda_{-(l+1)}\quad\text{for any integer }l.

In addition, we observe

\displaystyle\Gamma(a_{m}+1)=a_{m}\Gamma(a_{m})=\cdots=\prod_{i=-m}^{m}a_{i}\Gamma(a_{-m})=\prod_{i=-m}^{m}a_{i}\Gamma(-\lambda_{m-1})=\prod_{i=-m}^{m}a_{i}\Gamma(-\lambda_{m}+1),

and

\displaystyle\Gamma(7/6+m)=\Gamma(\lambda_{m+1})=\prod_{i=-m}^{m}\lambda_{i}\Gamma(\lambda_{-m})=\prod_{i=-m}^{m}\lambda_{i}\Gamma(1/6-m),

which implies

\displaystyle\lambda_{m}\frac{\Gamma(b)}{\Gamma(-\lambda_{m}+1)}=\left(\prod_{i=-m}^{m}\lambda_{i}\right)a_{m}\frac{\Gamma(b)}{\Gamma(-\lambda_{m}+1)}\left(\prod_{i=-m}^{m}a_{i}\right)^{-1}=M_{m}a_{m}\frac{\Gamma(b)}{\Gamma(a_{m}+1)}.

Thus, we deduce

\displaystyle\partial_{v}g_{m,1}(x,0)=-\lambda_{m}x^{\lambda_{m}-\frac{1}{3}}3^{\frac{1}{3}}\frac{\Gamma(b)}{\Gamma({-\lambda_{m}+1})}=-M_{m}a_{m}x^{\lambda_{m}-\frac{1}{3}}3^{\frac{1}{3}}\frac{\Gamma(b)}{\Gamma({a_{m}+1})}=\partial_{v}g_{m,2}(x,0).

•

To prove (
C.22
) we use (
C.5
), (
C.6
), and (
C.1
). For
v<0
, we recall
J_{2}
and observe that

\displaystyle\partial_{vv}g_{m,1}(x,v)

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{m}x^{\lambda_{m}}\frac{2v}{3x}U\left(-\lambda_{m}+1,b+1,-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)+\lambda_{m}x^{\lambda_{m}}\frac{v^{2}}{3x}\frac{v^{2}}{3x}U^{\prime}\left(-\lambda_{m}+1,b+1,-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{m}x^{\lambda_{m}}\frac{2v}{3x}\frac{\Gamma(b)}{\Gamma(-\lambda_{m}+1)}\left(-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{-\frac{2}{3}}+O(v)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda_{m}(\lambda_{m}-1)x^{\lambda_{m}}\frac{v^{4}}{(3x)^{2}}U\left(-\lambda_{m}+2,b+2,-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{m}x^{\lambda_{m}}\frac{2v}{3x}\frac{\Gamma(b)}{\Gamma(-\lambda_{m}+1)}\left(-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{-\frac{2}{3}}+O(v)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda_{m}(\lambda_{m}-1)x^{\lambda_{m}}\frac{v^{4}}{(3x)^{2}}\frac{\Gamma(b+1)}{\Gamma(-\lambda_{m}+2)}\left(-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{-\frac{5}{3}}

\displaystyle=O(v).

For
v>0
, by using
J_{1}
and similar arguments,

\displaystyle\partial_{vv}g_{m,2}(x,v)

\displaystyle=O(v)+M_{m}x^{\lambda_{m}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\left[\frac{2v}{3x}U^{\prime}\left(a_{m},b,\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)+\frac{v^{2}}{3x}\frac{v^{2}}{3x}U^{\prime\prime}\left(a_{m},b,\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=O(v)-M_{m}x^{\lambda_{m}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}a_{m}\frac{2v}{3x}U\left(a_{m}+1,b+1,\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)+

\displaystyle\quad+M_{m}x^{\lambda_{m}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}a_{m}(a_{m}+1)\frac{v^{4}}{9x^{2}}U\left(a_{m}+2,b+2,\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)

\displaystyle=O(v)+M_{m}x^{\lambda_{m}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}a_{m}\left[-\frac{2v}{3x}\frac{\Gamma(b)}{\Gamma(a_{m}+1)}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{-\frac{2}{3}}+a_{m+1}\frac{v^{4}}{9x^{2}}\frac{\Gamma(b+1)}{\Gamma(a_{m}+2)}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{-1-\frac{2}{3}}\right]

\displaystyle=O(v).

Therefore, we have verified (
C.22
).

Hence, we conclude that the function
\phi_{m}
is a classical solution to

(C.25)

v\partial_{x}\phi_{m}-\partial_{vv}\phi_{m}=0\quad\text{in }\{x>0\}\times\mathbb{R}.

Moreover, we are able to show that the
\phi_{m}
satisfy the absorbing boundary condition, i.e. we prove

(C.26)

\displaystyle\phi_{m}(0,v)=0\quad\text{if }v>0.

To do this, first, we note from (
C.9
) that for
m\geq 0

\displaystyle\phi_{m}(x,v)=(-1)^{m}\frac{M_{m}}{\Gamma(a_{m})}x^{\lambda_{m}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\xi}\xi^{a_{m}-1}(1+\xi)^{b-a_{m}-1}\,d\xi\quad\text{if }v\geq 0.

After a change of variables, we deduce

(C.27)

\displaystyle\phi_{m}(x,v)=(-1)^{m}\frac{M_{m}}{\Gamma(a_{m})}x^{\lambda_{m}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\xi}\xi^{a_{m}-1}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}+\xi\right)^{b-a_{m}-1}\,d\xi.

Therefore, when
v^{3}\geq x
, we get since
b-a_{m}-1<0
for any
m\geq 0
,

(C.28)

\displaystyle|\phi_{m}(x,v)|\leq cx^{\lambda_{m}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}+b-a_{m}-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\xi}\xi^{a_{m}-1}\,d\xi\leq c{x^{\lambda_{m}}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{-a_{m}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}.

Similarly, for
m=-1
, we deduce from (
C.11
), (
C.9
), and (
C.10
)

\displaystyle\phi_{-1}(x,v)

\displaystyle=M_{-1}x^{-\frac{5}{6}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}U\left(\frac{1}{6},\frac{4}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{-1}}{\Gamma(1/6)}x^{-\frac{5}{6}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{5}{6}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}+\xi\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}\,d\xi\quad\text{if }v>0.

For
v^{3}\geq x
, we get

(C.29)

\displaystyle\phi_{-1}(x,v)\leq cx^{-\frac{5}{6}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\left[\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}\int_{0}^{\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{5}{6}}\,d\xi+\int_{\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}^{\infty}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{4}{6}}\right]\leq cx^{-\frac{5}{6}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}.

Thus, we have
\phi(x,v)\to 0
as
x\to 0
for any
v>0
. Hence, by (
C.25
) and (
C.26
), the functions
\phi_{m}
are classical solutions to (
C.13
) for any
m\geq-1
.

Lastly, we want to prove that
\phi_{m}
solves (
C.13
) for any
m\geq 0
and
\phi_{-1}
solves (
C.14
) in the weak sense, respectively.

•

Assume
m\geq 0
. First, we prove

(C.30)

\displaystyle\partial^{k}_{v}\phi_{m}\in L^{2}(H_{R})

for any
R>0
and
k=1,2
. To this end, by applying the operator
\partial^{2}_{v}
to the formulas (
C.19
) and (
C.20
), and using (
C.1
), we deduce

(C.31)

\displaystyle|\partial_{v}^{2}g_{m,1}|

\displaystyle\leq cx^{\lambda_{m}}\left(\frac{|v|}{x}\Bigg|U^{\prime}\left(-\lambda_{m},\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\Bigg|+\frac{|v|^{4}}{x^{2}}\Bigg|U^{\prime\prime}\left(-\lambda_{m},\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\Bigg|\right)

\displaystyle\leq cx^{\lambda_{m}}\left(\frac{|v|}{x}U\left(-\lambda_{m-1},\frac{5}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)+\frac{|v|^{4}}{x^{2}}U\left(-\lambda_{m-2},\frac{8}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\right),

and

\displaystyle|\partial_{v}^{2}g_{m,2}|

\displaystyle\leq cx^{\lambda_{m}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{18x}}\frac{v}{x}\left(\Bigg|U\left(a_{m},\frac{2}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\Bigg|+\Bigg|U^{\prime}\left(a_{m},\frac{2}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\Bigg|+\Bigg|U^{\prime\prime}\left(a_{m},\frac{2}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\Bigg|\right)

\displaystyle\leq cx^{\lambda_{m}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{18x}}\frac{v}{x}\left(U\left(a_{m},\frac{2}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)+U\left(a_{m+1},\frac{5}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)+U\left(a_{m+2},\frac{8}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\right),

where we have used the fact that
(v^{2}/x)^{2}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\leq c\frac{v}{x}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{18x}}
.
Note

(C.32)

\displaystyle U(a,b,\pm v^{3}/9x)\leq\begin{cases}c&\quad\text{if }\pm v^{3}\leq x,\\
c(|v|^{3}/9x)^{-a}&\quad\text{if }\pm v^{3}\geq x\end{cases}

for some constant
c=c(a,b)
, where we have used the fact that
U(a,b,z)
is analytic with respect to the
z
-variable and (
C.8
). Using these properties, we get

(C.33)

\displaystyle\int_{H_{R}\cap\{x\leq-v^{3}\}}|\partial_{v}^{2}\phi_{m}|^{2}+\int_{H_{R}\cap\{x\geq|v|^{3}\}}|\partial_{v}^{2}\phi_{m}|^{2}+\int_{H_{R}\cap\{x\leq v^{3}\}}|\partial_{v}^{2}\phi_{m}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\int_{-R}^{0}\int_{x\leq|v|^{3}}|v|^{-4+6\lambda_{m}}\,dx\,dv+\int_{0}^{R^{3}}\int_{|v|^{3}\leq x}x^{2(\lambda_{m}-1)}v^{2}\,dv\,dx+\int_{0}^{R}\int_{x\leq v^{3}}|v|^{-4+6\lambda_{m}}\,dx\,dv\right)

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\int_{-R}^{0}|v|^{-1+6\lambda_{m}}\,dv+\int_{0}^{R^{3}}x^{2\lambda_{m}-1}\,dx+\int_{0}^{R}|v|^{-1+6\lambda_{m}}\,dx\,dv\right)\leq c,

where we have also used the fact that
(v^{3}/x)^{k}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{18x}}\leq c(k)
. Similarly, we can prove
\partial_{v}\phi_{m}\in L^{2+}(H_{R})
. Then, by the equation, we get
v\partial_{x}\phi_{m}=\partial_{vv}\phi_{m}
, which gives
v\partial_{x}\phi_{m}\in L^{2}(H_{R})
.
Therefore, by Remark
2.1
,
\phi_{m}
is a weak solution to (
C.13
).

•

Assume
m=-1
. Similarly, we prove

\displaystyle\partial^{k}_{v}\phi_{-1}\in L^{2}(D_{R})

for any
R>1
and
k=1,2
, where we write
D_{R}\coloneq(0,R^{3})\times(-R,-1/R)
. To prove it for
k=2
, we employ (
C.31
) with
\lambda_{m}=\lambda_{-1}
to get

\displaystyle\int_{D_{R}\cap\mathcal{R}^{+}}|\partial^{2}_{v}\phi_{-1}|^{2}+\int_{(1/R)^{3}}^{R^{3}}\int_{-R}^{R}|\partial^{2}_{v}\phi_{-1}|^{2}\leq c\left(\int_{-R}^{-1/R}\int_{x<|v|^{3}}|v|^{-4+6\lambda_{-1}}\,dx\,dv+1\right)\leq c,

where we have also used the fact that
\phi_{-1}
is smooth away from the boundary. Similarly, we can prove
\partial_{v}\phi_{m}\in L^{2}(D_{R})
. In addition,
\phi_{-1}
is a classical solution to (
C.13
),
v\partial_{x}\phi_{-1}=\partial_{vv}\phi_{-1}\in L^{2}(D_{R})
, which gives that it is a weak solution to (
C.14
).

This completes the proof.
∎

Remark C.4
.

Here, we remark that as in (
C.30
), we can get more general results, which are used in
subsection 5.1
. For any
\epsilon>0
and
k=0,1,2
, we have

(C.34)

\displaystyle v^{k}\partial^{k+1}_{v}\phi_{0}\in L^{8-\epsilon}(H_{R})\quad\text{and}\quad v^{k}\partial^{k+2}_{v}\phi_{0}\in L^{\frac{8}{3}-\epsilon}(H_{R}).

For
k=0
, using (
C.23
), (
C.24
) and (
C.32
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{H_{R}\cap\{x\leq-v^{3}\}}|\partial_{v}\phi_{0}|^{8-\epsilon}+\int_{H_{R}\cap\{x\geq|v|^{3}\}}|\partial_{v}\phi_{0}|^{8-\epsilon}+\int_{H_{R}\cap\{x\leq v^{3}\}}|\partial_{v}\phi_{0}|^{8-\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\int_{-R}^{0}\int_{x\leq|v|^{3}}|v|^{\frac{-8+\epsilon}{2}}\,dx\,dv+\int_{0}^{R^{3}}\int_{|v|^{3}\leq x}(x^{-\frac{5}{6}}v^{2})^{8-\epsilon}\,dv\,dx+\int_{0}^{R}\int_{x\leq v^{3}}|v|^{\frac{-8+\epsilon}{2}}\,dx\,dv\right)

\displaystyle\leq c(R).

Similarly, we can prove
v^{2}\phi_{-1},v^{3}\partial_{v}\phi_{-1}\in L^{8-\epsilon}(H_{R})
. Therefore, we prove the first claim in (
C.34
), as
v^{k}\partial^{k+1}_{v}\phi_{0}
is a sum of
\partial_{v}\phi_{0},\,v^{2}\phi_{-1},\,v^{3}\partial_{v}\phi_{-1}
by (
C.40
).

On the other hand, by considering
|\partial_{v}^{2}\phi_{m}|^{\frac{8}{3}-\epsilon}
instead of
|\partial_{v}^{2}\phi_{m}|^{2}
in (
C.33
), we directly derive
\partial^{2}_{v}\phi_{0}\in L^{\frac{8}{3}-\epsilon}(H_{R})
.
Next, we get

\displaystyle\int_{H_{R}\cap\{x\leq-v^{3}\}}|v^{3}\partial^{2}_{v}\phi_{-1}|^{\frac{8}{3}-\epsilon}+\int_{H_{R}\cap\{x\geq|v|^{3}\}}|v^{3}\partial^{2}_{v}\phi_{-1}|^{\frac{8}{3}-\epsilon}+\int_{H_{R}\cap\{x\leq v^{3}\}}|v^{3}\partial^{2}_{v}\phi_{-1}|^{\frac{8}{3}-\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\int_{-R}^{0}\int_{x\leq|v|^{3}}|v|^{-\frac{3}{2}(\frac{8}{3}-\epsilon)}\,dx\,dv+\int_{0}^{R^{3}}\int_{|v|^{3}\leq x}(x^{-\frac{11}{6}}v^{4})^{\frac{8}{3}-\epsilon}\,dv\,dx+\int_{0}^{R}\int_{x\leq v^{3}}|v|^{-\frac{3}{2}(\frac{8}{3}-\epsilon)}\,dx\,dv\right)

\displaystyle\leq c(R).

Similarly, we also derive that
v^{2}\partial_{v}\phi_{-1}\in L^{\frac{8}{3}-\epsilon}(H_{R})
and
v^{k}\partial_{v}^{k+2}
can be represented as a sum of such functions. Thus, we can deduce the second claim in (
C.34
).

Now we discuss several properties of the functions
\phi_{m}
such as the precise asymptotic behavior of
\phi_{m}
close to the grazing point
(0,0)
and at infinity, the regularity of
\phi_{m}
and the relation between
\phi_{m}
and
\phi_{m-1}
. To do so, it is convenient to split

\displaystyle(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}=\mathcal{R}^{-}\cup\mathcal{R}^{0}\cup\mathcal{R}^{+}

into the following three characteristic regions:

(C.35)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{-}\coloneqq\{(x,v)\,:\,0<x\leq v^{3}\quad\text{with }v\geq 0\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{0}\coloneqq\{(x,v)\,:\,x\geq v^{3}\geq-x\quad\text{with }x>0\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{+}\coloneqq\{(x,v)\,:\,0<x\leq-v^{3}\quad\text{with }v\leq 0\}.

We say that
f_{1}(x,v)\eqsim f_{2}(x,v)
in a set
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
, whenever there exists
C>0
such that for all
x,v\in\mathcal{A}
it holds
C^{-1}f_{2}(x,v)\leq f_{1}(x,v)\leq Cf_{2}(x,v)
.

Lemma C.5
.

Let the functions
\phi_{m}
be defined as in (
C.11
) for
m\geq-1
. Then, the following hold:

•

\phi_{0}
satisfies

(C.36)

\displaystyle\phi_{0}(x,v)\eqsim\begin{cases}x^{\frac{1}{6}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{-\frac{5}{6}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\quad&\text{if }(x,v)\in\mathcal{R}^{-},\\
x^{\frac{1}{6}}\quad&\text{if }(x,v)\in\mathcal{R}^{0},\\
|v|^{\frac{1}{2}}\quad&\text{if }(x,v)\in\mathcal{R}^{+}.\end{cases}

•

For any
m\geq 1
,
\phi_{m}
satisfies

(C.37)

\displaystyle\phi_{m}(x,v)\eqsim\begin{cases}x^{\frac{1}{6}+m}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{-\frac{5}{6}-m}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\quad&\text{if }(x,v)\in\mathcal{R}^{-},\\
x^{\frac{1}{6}+m}\quad&\text{if }(x,v)\in\mathcal{R}^{0}\cap(\{x>0\}\times\{v\geq 0\}).\end{cases}

•

\phi_{-1}
satisfies

(C.38)

\phi_{-1}(x,v)\eqsim\begin{cases}x^{-\frac{5}{6}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\quad&\text{if }(x,v)\in\mathcal{R}^{-},\\
x^{-\frac{5}{6}}\quad&\text{if }(x,v)\in\mathcal{R}^{0},\\
|v|^{-\frac{5}{2}}\quad&\text{if }(x,v)\in\mathcal{R}^{+}.\end{cases}

•

The functions
\phi_{m}
are smooth in
H_{1}\cap\mathcal{R}^{-}
in the sense that for
(x,v)\in H_{1}\cap\mathcal{R}^{-}
with
x\leq v^{3+\epsilon}
for some
\epsilon>0
,

(C.39)

\displaystyle|D^{k}\phi_{m}(x,v)|\leq c(k,m,\epsilon).

•

For any
m\geq 0
,

(C.40)

\displaystyle c_{m}\partial_{x}\phi_{m}(x,v)=c_{m-1}\left(\frac{1}{36}-m^{2}\right)\phi_{m-1}(x,v),

where the constants
c_{m}
are given in (
C.12
).

We first establish the asymptotic behavior of the functions
\phi_{m}
. To do so, we crucially use the integral representation formula of
U
from (
C.9
). Note that the formula (
C.37
) cannot cover all points
(x,v)\in(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}
, since the functions
\phi_{m}
change sign for
m\geq 1
. This follows from (
C.36
) and (
C.40
).

Proof of (
C.36
)-(
C.38
).

Let us recall
b=\frac{2}{3}
,
a_{m}=\frac{5}{6}+m
, and
\lambda_{m}=\frac{1}{6}+m
. We consider the two cases
m\geq 0
and
m=-1
separately.

First, we assume
m\geq 0
. We split the proof into four parts.

•

Suppose
(x,v)\in\mathcal{R}^{-}
. Using the explicit formula of
\phi_{m}
given by (
C.27
) when
v>0
, we derive since
b-a_{m}-1<0

\displaystyle\phi_{m}(x,v)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{c}x^{\lambda_{m}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}+b-a_{m}-1}\int_{0}^{\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}e^{-\xi}\xi^{a_{m}-1}\,d\xi

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{c}x^{\lambda_{m}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{-a_{m}}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{9}}e^{-\xi}\xi^{a_{m}-1}\,d\xi\geq\frac{1}{c}x^{\lambda_{m}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{-a_{m}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}

for some constant
c=c(m)
, where we have used the fact that
v^{3}/x\geq 1
. Using this and (
C.28
),

(C.41)

\displaystyle\phi_{m}(x,v)\eqsim x^{\lambda_{m}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{-a_{m}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{x}}\quad\text{in }\mathcal{R}^{0}\cap(\{x>0\}\times\{v\geq 0\}).

•

Suppose
(x,v)\in\mathcal{R}^{0}\cap(\{x>0\}\times\{v\geq 0\})
. Proceeding as before and using the fact that
v^{3}\leq x
and
a_{m}>0
for
m\geq 0
, we observe

\displaystyle\phi_{m}(x,v)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{c}x^{\lambda_{m}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{-a_{m}}\int_{0}^{\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}e^{-\xi}\xi^{a_{m}-1}\,d\xi\geq\frac{x^{\lambda_{m}}}{c}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{-a_{m}}\int_{0}^{\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\xi^{a_{m}-1}\,d\xi\geq cx^{\lambda_{m}}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\phi_{m}(x,v)

\displaystyle\leq cx^{\lambda_{m}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\xi}\xi^{a_{m}-1}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}+\xi\right)^{b-a_{m}-1}\,d\xi

\displaystyle\leq cx^{\lambda_{m}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\left[\int_{0}^{\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\xi^{a_{m}-1}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{b-a_{m}-1}\,d\xi+\int_{\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}^{\infty}\xi^{b-2}\,d\xi\right]\leq cx^{\lambda_{m}},

where we have also used the fact that
b-2=-5/3<-1
and
a_{m}>0
. Thus, we get

(C.42)

\displaystyle\phi_{m}(x,v)\eqsim x^{\lambda_{m}}\quad\text{in }\mathcal{R}^{0}\cap(\{x>0\}\times\{v\geq 0\}).

For the remaining regions, i.e. when
v<0
, we only consider the case
m=0
. Using (
C.11
), (
C.10
), (
C.9
), and a change of variables, we deduce the following formula for
v<0
,

\displaystyle\phi_{0}(x,v)

\displaystyle=x^{\frac{1}{6}}\left(-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}U\left(\frac{1}{6},\frac{4}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Gamma(1/6)}x^{\frac{1}{6}}\left(-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\xi}\xi^{-\frac{5}{6}}(1+\xi)^{\frac{1}{6}}\,d\xi=\frac{1}{\Gamma(1/6)}x^{\frac{1}{6}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{5}{6}}\left(-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}+\xi\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}\,d\xi.

We stress that this formula is not valid for
m\geq 1
since it requires
-\frac{1}{6}-m-b+1=\frac{1}{6}-m>0
.

•

Suppose
(x,v)\in\mathcal{R}^{0}\cap(\{x>0\}\times\{v<0\})
. Then we deduce

\displaystyle\phi_{0}(x,v)\geq\frac{1}{c}x^{\frac{1}{6}}\int_{0}^{1}\xi^{-\frac{5}{6}}\,d\xi\geq\frac{1}{c}x^{\frac{1}{6}}

and

\displaystyle\phi_{0}(x,v)\leq cx^{\frac{1}{6}}\left[\int_{0}^{1}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{5}{6}}\,d\xi+\int_{1}^{\infty}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{4}{6}}\,d\xi\right]\leq cx^{\frac{1}{6}},

where we have used the fact that
|v|^{3}\leq x
. Altogether, we have shown

(C.43)

\displaystyle\phi_{0}(x,v)\eqsim x^{\frac{1}{6}}\quad\text{in }\mathcal{R}^{0}\cap(\{x>0\}\times\{v<0\}).

•

Suppose
(x,v)\in\mathcal{R}^{+}
. Observe

\displaystyle\phi_{0}(x,v)\geq\frac{1}{c}x^{\frac{1}{6}}\left(-\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}\int_{0}^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{5}{6}}\,d\xi\geq\frac{1}{c}|v|^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{9}}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{5}{6}}\,d\xi\geq\frac{1}{c}|v|^{\frac{1}{2}}

and

\displaystyle\phi_{0}(x,v)\leq cx^{\frac{1}{6}}\left[\left(-\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}\int_{0}^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{5}{6}}\,d\xi+\int_{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}^{\infty}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{4}{6}}\,d\xi\right]\leq cx^{\frac{1}{6}}\left(\left(-\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}+1\right)\leq c|v|^{\frac{1}{2}},

where we have used the fact that
x\leq|v|^{3}
. This gives

(C.44)

\displaystyle\phi_{0}(x,v)\eqsim|v|^{\frac{1}{2}}\quad\text{in }\mathcal{R}^{-}.

By (
C.41
) (
C.42
), (
C.43
), and (
C.44
), we obtain (
C.36
) and (
C.37
). This completes the proof for
m\geq 0
.

Finally, we consider the case
m=-1
. By (
C.11
), (
C.9
), and (
C.10
), we get

\displaystyle\phi_{-1}(x,v)=\begin{cases}x^{-\frac{5}{6}}U\left(\frac{5}{6},\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(5/6)}x^{-\frac{5}{6}}\left(-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{1}{6}}\left(-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}+\xi\right)^{-\frac{7}{6}}\,d\xi&\quad\text{if }v<0,\\
M_{-1}x^{-\frac{5}{6}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}U\left(\frac{1}{6},\frac{4}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)=\frac{M_{-1}}{\Gamma(1/6)}x^{-\frac{5}{6}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{5}{6}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}+\xi\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}\,d\xi&\quad\text{if }v\geq 0.\end{cases}

We follow similar arguments as in the proof for
m\geq 0
.

•

Suppose
(x,v)\in\mathcal{R}^{-}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\phi_{-1}(x,v)\geq\frac{x^{-\frac{5}{6}}}{c}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}\int_{0}^{\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{5}{6}}\,d\xi\geq\frac{x^{-\frac{5}{6}}}{c}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{9}}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{5}{6}}\,d\xi\geq\frac{x^{-\frac{5}{6}}}{c}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}},

and the corresponding upper bound was already deduced in (
C.29
).

•

Suppose
(x,v)\in\mathcal{R}^{0}\cap(\{x>0\}\times\{v\geq 0\})
. Then we get

\displaystyle\phi_{-1}(x,v)\geq\frac{x^{-\frac{5}{6}}}{c}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}\int_{0}^{\frac{9x}{v^{3}}}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{5}{6}}\,d\xi\geq\frac{x^{-\frac{5}{6}}}{c}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}\int_{0}^{\frac{9x}{v^{3}}}\xi^{-\frac{5}{6}}\,d\xi\geq\frac{x^{-\frac{5}{6}}}{c},

and

\displaystyle\phi_{-1}(x,v)\leq cx^{-\frac{5}{6}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\left[\left(\frac{v^{3}}{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}\int_{0}^{\frac{9x}{v^{3}}}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{5}{6}}\,d\xi+\int_{\frac{9x}{v^{3}}}^{\infty}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{4}{6}}\,d\xi\right]\leq cx^{-\frac{5}{6}}.

•

Suppose
(x,v)\in\mathcal{R}^{0}\cap(\{x>0\}\times\{v<0\})
. Then we have

\displaystyle\phi_{-1}(x,v)\geq\frac{x^{-\frac{5}{6}}}{c}\left(-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}-\frac{7}{6}}\int_{0}^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{1}{6}}\,d\xi\geq\frac{x^{-\frac{5}{6}}}{c}\left(-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{-\frac{5}{6}}\int_{0}^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\xi^{-\frac{1}{6}}\,d\xi\geq\frac{x^{-\frac{5}{6}}}{c},

and

\displaystyle\phi_{-1}(x,v)\leq c{x^{-\frac{5}{6}}}\left(-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\left[\left(-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{-\frac{7}{6}}\int_{0}^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{1}{6}}\,d\xi+\int_{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}^{\infty}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{4}{3}}\,d\xi\right]\leq cx^{-\frac{5}{6}}.

•

Suppose
(x,v)\in\mathcal{R}^{+}
. Then we obtain

\displaystyle\phi_{-1}(x,v)\geq\frac{x^{-\frac{5}{6}}}{c}\left(-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}-\frac{7}{6}}\int_{0}^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{1}{6}}\,d\xi\geq\frac{x^{-\frac{5}{6}}}{c}\left(-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}-\frac{7}{6}}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{9}}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{1}{6}}\,d\xi\geq\frac{|v|^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{c},

and

\displaystyle\phi_{-1}(x,v)\leq c{x^{-\frac{5}{6}}}\left(-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\left[\left(-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{-\frac{7}{6}}\int_{0}^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{1}{6}}\,d\xi+\int_{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}^{\infty}e^{-\xi}\xi^{-\frac{4}{3}}\,d\xi\right]\leq c|v|^{-\frac{5}{2}}.

∎

Proof of (
C.39
).

Fix two points
(x,v)\in H_{1}\cap\mathcal{R}^{-}
such that
v^{3+\epsilon}\geq x
for some
\epsilon>0
. Using (
C.11
) with
v\geq 0
, we have for some constant
c\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\phi_{m}(x,v)=cx^{\lambda_{m}}\widetilde{U}\left(a_{m},\frac{2}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right),

where we write
\lambda_{m}\coloneqq\frac{1}{6}+m
,
a_{m}\coloneqq\frac{5}{6}+m
, and
\widetilde{U}(a,b,z)\coloneqq e^{-z}U(a,b,z)
. Since by
[
7
, 13.3.27]
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{U}^{(j)}(a,b,z)=(-1)^{j}\widetilde{U}(a,b+j,z),

we observe

\displaystyle D^{k}\phi_{m}(x,v)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in J,\\
3|A_{j}|+|B_{j}|\leq 3(\lambda_{m}+2k)\end{subarray}}C_{j}x^{A_{j}}v^{B_{j}}\widetilde{U}^{(j)}\left(a_{m},\frac{2}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in J,\\
3|A_{j}|+|B_{j}|\leq 3(\lambda_{m}+2k)\end{subarray}}C_{j}^{\prime}x^{A_{j}}v^{B_{j}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}U\left(a_{m},\frac{2}{3}+j,\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)

for some constants
C_{j},C_{j}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
, where
J=\{0,1,\ldots,k\}
.

Using this together with the fact that
|z|^{K}e^{-|z|}\leq c(K)e^{-|z|/2}
for any
K>0
,
v^{3}/x\geq v^{-\epsilon}
, and (
C.8
), we derive

(C.45)

\displaystyle|D^{k}\phi_{m}(x,v)|\leq cx^{-(\lambda_{m}+2k)}v^{-3(\lambda_{m}+2k-a_{m})}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}

\displaystyle\leq c(k,m)v^{-6(\lambda_{m}+2k)-a_{m}}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{18x}}

\displaystyle\leq c(k,m)v^{-6(\lambda_{m}+2k)-a_{m}}e^{-\frac{1}{18v^{\epsilon}}}

\displaystyle\leq c(k,m,\epsilon).

This completes the proof of (
C.39
).
∎

Proof of (
C.40
).

First, given
\lambda>0
and
c\in\mathbb{R}
, we deduce from (
C.3
) and the chain rule,

\displaystyle\partial_{x}U(a,b,c/x)=-x^{-1}(c/x)U^{\prime}(a,b,c/x)=-x^{-1}a\big[(1+a-b)U(a+1,b,c/x)-U(a,b,c/x)\big].

Hence, we obtain the following formula:

(C.46)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{x}[x^{\lambda}U(a,b,c/x)]&=\lambda x^{\lambda-1}U(a,b,c/x)+x^{\lambda}\partial_{x}U(a,b,c/x)\\
&=x^{\lambda-1}\big[(\lambda+a)U(a,b,c/x)-a(1+a-b)U(a+1,b,c/x)\big].\end{split}

Similarly, we derive

(C.47)

\displaystyle\partial_{x}[x^{\lambda}e^{c/x}U(a,b,-c/x)]

\displaystyle=\lambda x^{\lambda-1}e^{c/x}U(a,b,-c/x)+x^{\lambda-1}(-{c}/{x})e^{c/x}U(a,b,-c/x)

\displaystyle+x^{\lambda}e^{c/x}\partial_{x}U(a,b,-c/x)

\displaystyle=\lambda x^{\lambda-1}e^{c/x}U(a,b,-c/x)+x^{\lambda-1}(-{c}/{x})e^{c/x}U(a,b,-c/x)

\displaystyle-x^{\lambda-1}e^{c/x}a\big[(1+a-b)U(a+1,b,-c/x)-U(a,b,-c/x)\big]

\displaystyle=x^{\lambda-1}e^{c/x}\big[-a(a-b+1)U(a+1,b,-c/x)+(a+\lambda-c/x)U(a,b,-c/x)\big].

Recall from (
C.11
) that

\displaystyle\phi_{m}(x,v)=\begin{cases}c_{m}x^{\frac{1}{6}+m}U\left(-\frac{1}{6}-m,\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)&\quad\text{if }v<0,\\
c_{m}M_{m}x^{\frac{1}{6}+m}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}U\left(\frac{5}{6}+m,\frac{2}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)&\quad\text{if }v\geq 0.\end{cases}

Let us take
m\geq 0
and apply the formula (
C.46
) with

\displaystyle a=-\frac{1}{6}-m,\penalty\ \penalty\ \lambda=\frac{1}{6}+m,\penalty\ \penalty\ b=\frac{2}{3},\penalty\ \penalty\ c=-\frac{v^{3}}{9}.

Then, we deduce for
v<0
,

\displaystyle c_{m}\partial_{x}\phi_{m}(x,v)

\displaystyle=x^{\frac{1}{6}+(m-1)}\left(\frac{1}{6}+m\right)\left(\frac{1}{6}-m\right)U\left(-\frac{1}{6}-(m-1),\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)

\displaystyle=c_{m-1}\left(\frac{1}{36}-m^{2}\right)\phi_{m-1}(x,v).

On the other hand, when
v\geq 0
, using the formula (
C.47
) with

\displaystyle a=\frac{5}{6}+m,\penalty\ \penalty\ \lambda=\frac{1}{6}+m,\penalty\ \penalty\ b=\frac{2}{3},\penalty\ \penalty\ c=-\frac{v^{3}}{9}

and (
C.4
), we get

\displaystyle c_{m}\partial_{x}\phi_{m}(x,v)

\displaystyle=M_{m}x^{\frac{1}{6}+m-1}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}\Bigg[\left(-\frac{5}{6}-m\right)\left(\frac{7}{6}+m\right)U\left(\frac{11}{6}+m,\frac{2}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+(1+2m+v^{3}/(9x))U\left(\frac{5}{6}+m,\frac{2}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=M_{m}x^{\frac{1}{6}+m-1}e^{-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}}U\left(\frac{5}{6}+m-1,\frac{2}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)=c_{m-1}\left(\frac{1}{36}-m^{2}\right)\phi_{m-1}(x,v),

where we have also used the fact that

\displaystyle M_{m}=\left(\frac{1}{36}-m^{2}\right)M_{m-1}.

∎

C.2.
1D solutions with in-flow boundary condition

We now classify all
\lambda
-homogeneous solutions to kinetic Fokker-Planck equations with source term and data being homogeneous polynomials. Obviously, it suffices to consider
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
.

Lemma C.6
.

Let
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
and
h
be a
\lambda
-homogeneous solution to

(C.48)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}v\partial_{x}h-\partial_{vv}h&=p\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ in }(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R},\\
h(x,v)&=av^{\lambda}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ on }\{0\}\times\{v>0\},\end{cases}

where
p\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2}
is a homogeneous polynomial of degree
\lambda-2
and
a\in\mathbb{R}
. In addition, we assume that either
p\neq 0
or
a\neq 0
, and that
h
does not grow exponentially as
|(x,v)|\to\infty
. Then, there are a polynomial
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}
and a unique
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
such that

(C.49)

\displaystyle h=P+\mu\psi_{[\lambda/3]},

where
\psi_{l}
is defined as

(C.50)

\displaystyle\psi_{l}(x,v)\coloneqq x^{\frac{3l+2}{3}}\left(M\left(-\frac{3l+2}{3},\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)+\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)}{\Gamma\left(-\frac{3l+2}{3}\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(-\frac{3l+1}{3}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{4}{3}\right)}\frac{v}{(9x)^{1/3}}M\left(-\frac{3l+1}{3},\frac{4}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\right),

where
M
denotes a hypergeometric function of the first kind.
Moreover, when
\lambda=3l
or
3l+1
for some nonnegative integer
l
, then
\mu=0
.

Remark C.7
.

From (
C.62
), we observe that
\psi_{0}
solves the equation (
C.48
) with
p=0
and
\lambda=2
. However in (
1.13
), we denote by the function
\psi_{0}(x,v)
as
\psi_{0}(x,v)-a{v^{2}}
to ensure that it solves the equation with the constant right-hand side and zero-boundary data.

Remark C.8
.

Note that by
[
7
, 13.2.42]
we have

(C.51)

\displaystyle\psi_{l}(x,v)=c(l)x^{\frac{3l+2}{3}}U\left(-\frac{3l+2}{3},\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right),\qquad\text{ where }\qquad c(l)=3\frac{\Gamma(\frac{4}{3})}{\Gamma(-\frac{3l+1}{3})}

for
v<0
. For
v>0
it extends analytically via the same expression, taking the principal branch of the function
U
for
z\in(-\infty,0)
.

Proof of
subsection C.2
.

If
a\not=0
, we consider
\tilde{h}=h-av^{\lambda}
, which solves

\displaystyle\begin{cases}v\partial_{x}\tilde{h}-\partial_{vv}\tilde{h}&=p+a\lambda(\lambda-1)v^{\lambda-2}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ in }(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R},\\
\tilde{h}(x,v)&=0\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ on }\{0\}\times\{v>0\}.\end{cases}

Hence, it suffices to consider the case
a=0
.

By Step 2 in the proof of
[
35
, Lemma 3.7]
, we know that for any
p\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda-2}
, there exists
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}
such that

\displaystyle v\partial_{x}P-\partial_{vv}P=p\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ in }(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}.

Moreover, there must be
b\in\mathbb{R}
such that

(C.52)

\displaystyle P(0,v)=bv^{\lambda}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ on }\{0\}\times\{v>0\}.

Hence, it suffices to find all
\lambda
-homogeneous solutions
g
such that

(C.53)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}v\partial_{x}g-\partial_{vv}g&=0\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ in }(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R},\\
g(x,v)&=bv^{\lambda}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ on }\{0\}\times\{v>0\},\end{cases}

since then, all solutions to our problem are of the form
h=g+P
.

From
[
35
]
, we know that all functions
g:[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
that are homogeneous of degree
\lambda\geq 0
and solve the homogeneous equation

(C.54)

\displaystyle v\partial_{x}g-\partial_{vv}g=0\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ in }(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}

are of the form

\displaystyle g(x,v)=x^{\frac{\lambda}{3}}\Psi(\tau),\qquad\tau:=\frac{-v^{3}}{9x},

where
\Psi
solves the so-called Kummer’s equation

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{3}\Psi(\tau)+\left(\frac{2}{3}-\tau\right)\Psi^{\prime}(\tau)+\tau\Psi^{\prime\prime}(\tau)=0.

By solving this equation explicitly, we obtain that any solution
g
of (
C.54
) is of the form

(C.55)

\displaystyle g(x,v)=C_{1}x^{\frac{\lambda}{3}}M\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3},\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)-C_{2}9^{-\frac{1}{3}}vx^{\frac{\lambda-1}{3}}M\left(-\frac{\lambda-1}{3},\frac{4}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)

for some
C_{1},C_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
. Recall that if
-a\in\mathbb{N}
, then
M(a,b,z)
is a polynomial in
z
of degree
-a
. Otherwise,

(C.56)

\displaystyle M(a;b;z)\asymp\begin{cases}\frac{\Gamma(b)}{\Gamma(a)}e^{z}z^{a-b}\qquad&\text{ as }z\to+\infty,\\
\frac{\Gamma(b)}{\Gamma(b-a)}(-z)^{-a}\qquad&\text{ as }z\to-\infty.\end{cases}

Now, we consider the case
\lambda\in 3(\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\})
. Then, we must have
C_{2}=0
, since otherwise
g(1,v)
grows exponentially as
v\to-\infty
by (
C.56
), since then
\tau=-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}=-\frac{v^{3}}{9}\to+\infty
. Hence, in that case,
M\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3},\frac{2}{3},\tau\right)
is a polynomial in
\tau=-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}
of degree
\frac{\lambda}{3}
. By an analogous argument, we get that
C_{1}=0
and
M\left(-\frac{\lambda-1}{3},\frac{2}{3},\tau\right)
is polynomial when
\lambda-1\in 3(\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\})
.
Therefore
h\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}
, and we are done.

Hence, we can exclude the cases
\lambda\in 3(\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\})
and
\lambda-1\in 3(\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\})
. Therefore, by application of (
C.56
) to
h
, and setting
\tau=-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}
we obtain

(C.57)

\displaystyle g(x,v)

\displaystyle\asymp C_{1}x^{\frac{\lambda}{3}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{2}{3})}{\Gamma(-\frac{\lambda}{3})}e^{\tau}\tau^{-\frac{\lambda+2}{3}}+C_{2}x^{\frac{\lambda}{3}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{4}{3})}{\Gamma(\frac{1-\lambda}{3})}e^{\tau}\tau^{-\frac{\lambda+2}{3}}\qquad\text{ as }\tau\to+\infty,

(C.58)

\displaystyle g(x,v)

\displaystyle\asymp C_{1}x^{\frac{\lambda}{3}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{2}{3})}{\Gamma(\frac{\lambda+2}{3})}(-\tau)^{\frac{\lambda}{3}}-C_{2}x^{\frac{\lambda}{3}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{4}{3})}{\Gamma(\frac{\lambda+3}{3})}(-\tau)^{\frac{\lambda}{3}}\qquad\text{ as }\tau\to-\infty.

Thus, the parameters
C_{1},C_{2}
must satisfy

(C.59)

\displaystyle C_{1}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{2}{3})}{\Gamma(-\frac{\lambda}{3})}+C_{2}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{4}{3})}{\Gamma(\frac{1-\lambda}{3})}=0,

since otherwise (
C.57
) would imply again that
h(1,v)
grows exponentially as
v\to-\infty
. An equivalent way to guarantee (
C.59
), is by taking
m\in\mathbb{R}
and choosing

\displaystyle C_{1}=m,\qquad C_{2}=-\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)}{\Gamma\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3}\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{1-\lambda}{3}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{4}{3}\right)}m.

Moreover, for any
v>0
we use (
C.58
) and that by sending
x\to 0
it holds
\tau:=-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\to-\infty
, to see that when
\lambda=3l+2
,

(C.60)

\displaystyle g(0,v)=m9^{-\frac{\lambda}{3}}\Gamma\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{\lambda+2}{3})}+\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1-\lambda}{3})}{\Gamma(-\frac{\lambda}{3})\Gamma(\frac{\lambda+3}{3})}\right)v^{\lambda}=m9^{-\frac{\lambda}{3}}\Gamma\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1-\lambda}{3}\right)(-1)^{l+1}\frac{4\pi}{\sqrt{3}}v^{\lambda},

where we used elementary properties of the Gamma function as in (
C.17
) to prove the second equality.

Clearly, we can now find a unique
m=m(\lambda,b)\in\mathbb{R}
such that
g(0,v)=bv^{\lambda}
for any
v>0
, as
\lambda=3l+2
for some
l\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
. In addition, such a choice of the constant
m
makes the corresponding function
g
a solution to (
C.53
).

Hence, altogether, we have proved that all homogeneous solutions
h
to (
C.54
) are of the form

\displaystyle g(x,v)=\mu_{1}P+\mu_{2}x^{\frac{\lambda}{3}}\left(M\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3},\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)+\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)}{\Gamma\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3}\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{1-\lambda}{3}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{4}{3}\right)}\frac{v}{(9x)^{1/3}}M\left(-\frac{\lambda-1}{3},\frac{4}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\right)

for some
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}
and
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, where
\mu_{1}=0
when
\lambda-2\in 3(\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\})
and
\mu_{2}=0
when
\lambda-2\notin 3(\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\})
. Therefore, using this together with (
C.50
), we get the desired result.

∎

We now prove several properties of the functions
\psi_{\lambda}
. Note that (
C.61
) parallelizes the corresponding relation for the
\phi_{m}
(
C.40
). Remarkably, the functions
\psi_{\lambda}
are not smooth in
\mathcal{R}^{-}
, which stands in stark contrast to the behavior of the functions
\phi_{m}
(see (
C.39
)).

Lemma C.9
.

Let
\psi_{l}
be defined as in (
C.50
), with a nonnegative integer
l
. Then, the following hold.

•

For any
l\geq 1
,

(C.61)

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\psi_{l}=c_{l}\psi_{l-1}.

•

\psi_{l}
is a weak solution to

(C.62)

\left\{\begin{aligned} v\partial_{x}\psi_{l}-\partial_{vv}\psi_{l}&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x>0\}\times\mathbb{R}$},\\
\psi_{l}&=c_{\lambda}v^{3l+2}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x=0\}\times\{v>0\}$}.\end{aligned}\right.

for some constant
c_{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
.

•

For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

(C.63)

\displaystyle|\partial_{v}^{k}\psi_{l}(x,v)|\leq c(k,j)\quad\text{for any }(x,v)\in H_{1}\cap\mathcal{R}^{-}\text{ with }x\leq v^{k+3-(3l+2)}.

•

For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,

(C.64)

\displaystyle\frac{|\psi_{l}(x_{1},v)-\psi_{l}(x_{2},v)|}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{1-\epsilon}}\leq c(\epsilon,j)\quad\text{for any }(x_{1},v),(x_{2},v)\in H_{1}\cap\mathcal{R}^{-}\text{ with }x_{1},x_{2}\leq v^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}}.

In particular, we have
\psi_{0}\in C^{3-3\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1}\cap\{x\leq v^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\})
, but
\psi_{0}\notin C^{3-3\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1}\cap\{x\leq v^{\frac{1}{\epsilon^{\prime}}}\})
if
\epsilon^{\prime}>\epsilon
, where
\mathcal{H}_{1}
is the 1-dimensional two-sided kinetic cylinder determined in Section
2
.

Proof.

First, we aim to prove (
C.61
). The proof is very similar to (
C.40
). By
[
1
, 13.4.10]
, we observe

\displaystyle zM^{\prime}(a,b,z)=aM(a+1,b,z)-aM(a,b,z).

Using this, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{x}(M\left(a,b,c/x\right))=-x^{-1}(c/x)M^{\prime}(a,b,c/x)=-x^{-1}\left(aM(a+1,b,c/x)-aM(a,b,c/x)\right).

With this, we derive

(C.65)

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\left(x^{-a}M(a,b,c/x)\right)

\displaystyle=-ax^{-a-1}M(a,b,-c/x)-x^{-a-1}\left(aM(a+1,b,c/x)-aM(a,b,c/x)\right)

\displaystyle=-ax^{-a-1}M(a+1,b,c/x).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\psi_{l}

\displaystyle=\partial_{x}\left(x^{-a}M(a,b,c/x)+Cv9^{-\frac{1}{3}}x^{-a-\frac{1}{3}}M\left(a+\frac{1}{3},b+\frac{2}{3},c/x\right)\right)

\displaystyle=-ax^{-a-1}M(a+1,b,c/x)-Cv9^{-\frac{1}{3}}\left(a+\frac{1}{3}\right)x^{-a-\frac{1}{3}-1}M\left(a+\frac{1}{3}+1,b+\frac{2}{3},c/x\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{3l+2}{3}x^{\frac{3l-1}{3}}\left(M\left(\frac{1-3l}{3},\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)+C\frac{\frac{3l+1}{3}}{\frac{3l+2}{3}}\frac{v}{(9x)^{\frac{1}{3}}}M\left(\frac{2-3l}{3},\frac{4}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{3l+2}{3}x^{\frac{3l-1}{3}}\left(M\left(\frac{1-3l}{3},\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)+\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1-3l}{3}\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2-3l}{3}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{4}{3}\right)}\frac{v}{(9x)^{\frac{1}{3}}}M\left(\frac{2-3l}{3},\frac{4}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{3l+2}{3}\psi_{j-1}

where
a=-\frac{3l+2}{3}
,
b=\frac{2}{3}
,
c=-v^{3}
and
C=\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)}{\Gamma\left(-\frac{3l+2}{3}\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(-\frac{3l+1}{3}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{4}{3}\right)}
. In particular, we have used

\displaystyle\Gamma\left(-\frac{3l+2}{3}\right)\frac{-(3l+2)}{3}=\Gamma\left(\frac{1-3l}{3}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\Gamma\left(-\frac{3l+1}{3}\right)\frac{-(3l+1)}{3}=\Gamma\left(\frac{2-3l}{3}\right).

Therefore, we have proved (
C.61
).

To prove that
\psi_{l}
is a weak solution, first we show

(C.66)

\displaystyle\partial_{vv}\psi_{l}\in L^{\infty}(H_{R})\quad\text{for any }R>0.

To this end, let us write

(C.67)

\displaystyle\widetilde{{M}}(a,b,z)\coloneqq z^{b-1}M\left(a,b,-z\right),\quad\lambda=3l+2\quad\text{and}\quad C\coloneqq\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{4}{3}\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{1-\lambda}{3}\right)}{\Gamma\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3}\right)}

to see that

(C.68)

\displaystyle\psi_{l}(x,v)=x^{\frac{\lambda}{3}}\left(M\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3},\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)+C\widetilde{M}\left(-\frac{\lambda-1}{3},\frac{4}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\right),

by recalling (
C.50
). Before we differentiate the function
\psi_{l}
, based on
[
1
, 13.4.13]
with
z
replaced by
-z
, we claim that for any
z>0
,

(C.69)

\displaystyle\frac{d^{j}}{dz^{j}}\widetilde{M}(a,b,z)=\frac{d^{j}}{dz^{j}}\left(z^{b-1}M(a,b,-z)\right)=(b-j)_{j}z^{b-j-1}M(a,b-j,-z),

where we write

(a)_{0}=1\quad\text{and}\quad(a)_{j}\coloneqq\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}(a+k)\text{ for any }j\geq 1.

When
j=1
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dz}\left(z^{b-1}M(a,b,-z)\right)

\displaystyle=(b-1)z^{b-2}M(a,b,-z)-z^{b-1}M^{\prime}(a,b,-z)

\displaystyle=z^{b-2}((b-1)M(a,b,-z)+(-z)M^{\prime}(a,b,-z))

\displaystyle=z^{b-2}(b-1)M(a,b-1,-z),

where we have used
[
1
, 13.4.13]
with
z
replaced by
-z
for the last equality. Suppose (
C.69
) is true for
j=m-1
. Similarly, we deduce

\displaystyle\frac{d^{m}}{dz^{m}}\left(z^{b-1}M(a,b,-z)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dz}\left((b-m+1)_{m-1}z^{b-m}M(a,b-m+1,-z)\right)

\displaystyle=(b-m+1)_{m-1}(b-m)z^{b-m-1}M(a,b-m+1,-z)

\displaystyle\quad-(b-m+1)_{m-1}z^{b-m}M^{\prime}(a,b-m+1,-z)

\displaystyle=(b-m)_{m}z^{b-m-1}M(a,b-m,-z).

This implies (
C.69
), by induction.

•

When
v<0
, we recall the formula (
C.51
). Then by differentiating the function
\psi_{l}
with respect to the
v
-variable and using (
C.1
), we get

\displaystyle|\partial^{2}_{v}\psi_{l}(x,v)|

\displaystyle\leq c\left(x^{\frac{\lambda}{3}-1}|v|\Bigg|U^{\prime}\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3},\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\Bigg|+x^{\frac{\lambda}{3}-2}|v|^{4}\Bigg|U^{\prime\prime}\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3},\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\Bigg|\right)

\displaystyle\leq c\left(x^{\frac{\lambda}{3}-1}vU\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3}+1,\frac{5}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)+x^{\frac{\lambda}{3}-2}v^{4}U\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3}+2,\frac{8}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\right).

Thus we now use (
C.8
) and the fact that
U(a,b,z)
is analytic, in order to further estimate

\displaystyle|\partial^{2}_{v}\psi_{l}(x,v)|\leq\begin{cases}cv^{{\lambda}-2}\leq cv^{3j}\leq c&\quad\text{if }-v^{3}\geq x,\\
cx^{\frac{\lambda}{3}-\frac{2}{3}}\leq cx^{j}\leq c&\quad\text{if }-v^{3}\leq x.\end{cases}

•

When
v\geq 0
, we differentiate the equation given in (
C.68
) to see that

(C.70)

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{v}\psi_{l}(x,v)

\displaystyle=x^{\frac{\lambda}{3}}\frac{2v}{3x}\left[(-1)M^{{}^{\prime}}\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3},\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)+C\widetilde{M}^{{}^{\prime}}\left(-\frac{\lambda-1}{3},\frac{4}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+x^{\frac{\lambda}{3}}\frac{v^{4}}{9x^{2}}\left[(-1)^{2}M^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3},\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)+C\widetilde{M}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\left(\frac{\lambda-1}{3},\frac{4}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=-cx^{\frac{\lambda}{3}}\frac{v}{x}M\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3}+1,\frac{5}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)+cx^{\frac{\lambda}{3}}\frac{v^{4}}{x^{2}}M\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3}+2,\frac{8}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)

\displaystyle+Cx^{\frac{\lambda}{3}}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\frac{2v}{3x}M^{\prime}\left(-\frac{\lambda-1}{3},\frac{1}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right),

where we have also used
[
7
, 13.3.16]
, (
C.69
), and
[
1
, 13.4.13]
. Using
[
7
, 13.3.16]
again, we derive

\displaystyle|\partial^{2}_{v}\psi_{l}(x,v)|

\displaystyle\leq c\left(x^{\frac{\lambda-3}{3}}|v|M\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3}+1,\frac{5}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)+x^{\frac{\lambda-6}{3}}|v|^{4}M\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3}+2,\frac{8}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad+cx^{\frac{\lambda-4}{3}}|v|^{2}M\left(-\frac{\lambda-1}{3}+1,\frac{4}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right).

If
v^{3}\gg x
, then by using (
C.56
), we deduce

(C.71)

\displaystyle|\partial^{2}_{v}\psi_{l}(x,v)|

\displaystyle\leq c\left(x^{\frac{\lambda-3}{3}}|v|(v^{3}/9x)^{\frac{\lambda-3}{3}}+x^{\frac{\lambda-6}{3}}|v|^{4}(v^{3}/9x)^{\frac{\lambda-6}{3}}+x^{\frac{\lambda-4}{3}}|v|^{2}(v^{3}/9x)^{\frac{\lambda-4}{3}}\right)\leq cv^{\lambda-2}\leq c.

On the other hand, for the case that
v^{3}\leq cx
for some large constant
c\geq 1
, then we have

\displaystyle|\partial^{2}_{v}\psi_{\lambda}(x,v)|\leq c(x^{\frac{\lambda-3}{3}}x^{\frac{1}{3}}+x^{\frac{\lambda-6}{3}}x^{\frac{4}{3}}+x^{\frac{\lambda-4}{3}}x^{\frac{2}{3}})\leq cx^{\frac{\lambda-2}{3}}\leq c.

Indeed, using (
C.60
) and (
C.68
), we get that
\psi_{l}
is continuous up to the boundary
\{x=0\}\times\{v>0\}
with
\psi_{l}=cv^{3l+2}
for some constant
c\in\mathbb{R}
. Altogether, we have proved (
C.66
), namely that
\partial_{vv}\psi_{l}\in L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}((0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R})
. This gives
v\partial_{x}\psi_{l}\in L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}((0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R})
, as
\psi_{l}
solves (
C.62
) in the classical sense. Therefore, by
subsection 2.1
,
\psi_{l}
is a weak solution to (
C.62
).

Next, we are going to prove (
C.63
).
To do so, first, we show that if
v^{3}\gg x
with
v,x\geq 0
and
k>\lambda
,

(C.72)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}^{k}\psi_{l}(x,v)=cv^{3l+2-k}O\left(\frac{x}{v^{3}}\right).

First, let us write

\displaystyle F(z)\coloneqq M\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3},\frac{2}{3},-z\right)+C\widetilde{M}\left(-\frac{\lambda-1}{3},\frac{4}{3},z\right)\quad\text{and}\quad G(v)\coloneqq\frac{v^{3}}{9x}

and recall (
C.67
) to see that

(C.73)

\displaystyle x^{\frac{\lambda}{3}}(F\circ G)(v)=\psi_{l}(x,v).

Then by this and Faá di Bruno’s formula together with the fact that
G^{(j)}(v)=0
when
j\geq 4
, we have

(C.74)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}^{k}(F\circ G)(v)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i+2j+3m=k}\frac{k!}{i!l!2^{j}m!6^{m}}F^{(i+j+m)}(G(v))\left(\frac{v^{2}}{3x}\right)^{i}\left(\frac{2v}{3x}\right)^{j}\left(\frac{2}{3x}\right)^{m}.

Before proceeding with further estimates, we prove that if
\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\gg 1
, then

(C.75)

\displaystyle F^{(j)}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{j}{M}^{(j)}\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3},\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)+C\widetilde{M}^{(j)}\left(-\frac{\lambda-1}{3},\frac{4}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{j}A_{\lambda}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{-j+\frac{\lambda}{3}}\left(\left(-\lambda/3\right)_{j}+O\left(\frac{9x}{v^{3}}\right)\right),

where

\displaystyle A_{\lambda}\coloneq\Gamma\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1-\lambda}{3}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\Gamma\left(\frac{2+\lambda}{3}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1-\lambda}{3}\right)}+\frac{1}{\Gamma\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{3+\lambda}{3}\right)}\right).

To prove this, we use
[
1
, 13.5.1]
together with the fact that
e^{i\pi a}z^{-a}=(-z)^{-a}
(note that we can always take the principle branch of
\log z=\log|z|+i\pi
by the analyticity of
M
) for
z\in\mathbb{R}
to see that when
v^{3}\gg x
, then for any
j
,

(C.76)

\displaystyle M\left(j-\frac{\lambda}{3},j+\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)=\frac{\Gamma(l+2/3)}{\Gamma(2/3+\lambda/3)}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{\frac{\lambda}{3}-j}\left(1+O\left(\frac{9x}{v^{3}}\right)\right),

and

(C.77)

\displaystyle M\left(-\frac{\lambda-1}{3},-j+\frac{4}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)=\frac{\Gamma(-j+4/3)}{\Gamma(-j+\lambda/3+1)}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{\frac{\lambda-1}{3}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{9x}{v^{3}}\right)\right).

Recall from
[
7
, 13.3.16]
and (
C.69
) that

(C.78)

\displaystyle{M}^{(l)}\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3},\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)=\frac{\left(-{\lambda}/{3}\right)_{j}}{(2/3)_{j}}M\left(j-\frac{\lambda}{3},j+\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right),

and

(C.79)

\displaystyle C\widetilde{M}^{(j)}\left(-\frac{\lambda-1}{3},\frac{4}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)=C(4/3-j)_{j}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{\frac{4}{3}-j-1}M\left(-\frac{\lambda-1}{3},-j+\frac{4}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right).

By plugging (
C.76
) and (
C.77
) into (
C.78
) and (
C.79
), respectively, we derive

(C.80)

\displaystyle{M}^{(j)}\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3},\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)=(-\lambda/3)_{j}\frac{\Gamma(2/3)}{\Gamma(2/3+\lambda/3)}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{-j+\frac{\lambda}{3}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{9x}{v^{3}}\right)\right),

and

(C.81)

\displaystyle C\widetilde{M}^{(j)}\left(-\frac{\lambda-1}{3},\frac{4}{3},\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)=C\frac{\Gamma(4/3)}{\Gamma(-j+\lambda/3+1)}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{-j+\frac{\lambda}{3}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{9x}{v^{3}}\right)\right),

where we have also used the fact that
\Gamma(a+j)=(a)_{j}\Gamma(a)
. Note from (
C.67
) that

\displaystyle C\frac{\Gamma(4/3)}{\Gamma(-j+\lambda/3+1)}=C\frac{\Gamma(4/3)(-\lambda/3)_{j}}{(-1)^{j}\Gamma(\lambda/3+1)}=(-1)^{j}\frac{\Gamma((1-\lambda)/3)\Gamma(2/3)(-\lambda/3)_{j}}{\Gamma(-\lambda/3)\Gamma(\lambda/3+1)}.

Therefore, using this, (
C.80
), and (
C.81
), we get (
C.75
).

Now plugging (
C.75
) into (
C.74
) yields

(C.82)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{v}^{k}(F\circ G)(v)}{A_{\lambda}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i+2j+3m=k}\frac{k!}{i!j!2^{j}m!6^{m}}(-1)^{i+j+m}\left(\frac{v^{3}}{9x}\right)^{-(i+j+m)+\frac{\lambda}{3}}\left(\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3}\right)_{i+j+m}+O\left(\frac{9x}{v^{3}}\right)\right)\left(\frac{v^{2}}{3x}\right)^{i}\left(\frac{2v}{3x}\right)^{j}\left(\frac{2}{3x}\right)^{m}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i+2j+3m=k}\frac{k!}{i!j!m!}(-1)^{i+j+m}v^{\lambda-k}x^{-\frac{\lambda}{3}}\left(\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3}\right)_{i+j+m}+O\left(\frac{9x}{v^{3}}\right)\right)3^{i+j-\frac{2\lambda}{3}}.

Now we want to prove for any
k>\lambda
,

\displaystyle a_{k}\coloneqq\sum_{i+2j+3m=k}\frac{1}{i!j!m!}(-1)^{i+j+m}3^{i+j}\left(-\frac{\lambda}{3}\right)_{i+j+m}=0.

This follows from the following observation. For a very small
y
such as
|y|\ll 1
, we get

\displaystyle(1+3y+3y^{2}+y^{3})^{\frac{\lambda}{3}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}{\binom{\lambda/3}{k}}(3y+3y^{2}+y^{3})^{k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\lambda/3(\lambda/3-1)\cdots(\lambda/3-(k-1))}{k!}(3y+3y^{2}+y^{3})^{k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\frac{(-\lambda/3)_{k}}{k!}(3y+3y^{2}+y^{3})^{k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\frac{(-\lambda/3)_{k}}{k!}\sum_{i+j+m=k}\frac{(3y)^{i}(3y^{2})^{l}(y^{3})^{m}}{i!j!m!}k!

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=0\\
i,l,m\geq 0\end{subarray}}^{\infty}(-1)^{i+j+m}\frac{(-\lambda/3)_{i+j+m}}{i!j!m!}3^{i+j}y^{i+2j+3m},

where we have used the generalized binomial theorem and the multinomial theorem. In addition, since
|y|\ll 1
and
(3y+3y^{2}+y^{3})\ll 1
, the above sums all converge. Moreover, we can rewrite
