\displaystyle(1+y)^{\lambda}=(1+3y+3y^{2}+y^{3})^{\frac{\lambda}{3}}=\sum_{{k=0}}^{\infty}\sum_{i+2j+3m=k}(-1)^{i+j+m}\frac{(-\lambda/3)_{i+j+m}}{i!j!m!}3^{i+j}y^{i+2j+3m}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}a_{k}y^{k}.

Therefore, we get
a_{k}=\binom{\lambda}{k}
when
k\leq\lambda=3l+2
and
a_{k}=0
when
k>\lambda=3l+2
.

By combining this result with (
C.82
) and (
C.73
), we arrive at (
C.72
). Therefore, when
k>\lambda=3l+2
, we have for any
x\leq v^{k+3-\lambda}
,

(C.83)

\displaystyle|\partial_{v}^{k}\psi(x,v)|\leq cv^{\lambda-k}\frac{v^{k+3-\lambda}}{v^{3}}\leq c.

Indeed, when
v\leq v_{0}
for some small constant
v_{0}
, then
v^{3}\gg v^{k+3-\lambda}\geq x
and (
C.72
) implies the desired result. If
v\geq v_{0}
, then by applying (
C.56
) with
x\ll 1
in the first equality given in (
C.75
) together with (
C.78
), and (
C.79
), we get (
C.83
).

In addition, when
k\leq\lambda
, by (
C.82
) and (
C.73
), we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{v}^{k}\psi(x,v)|\leq cv^{{\lambda}-k}\leq c.

Altogether, we have proved (
C.63
).

Now, we aim to prove (
C.64
).

•

Assume
|x_{1}-x_{2}|\geq v^{\frac{1+k}{\epsilon}}
. We observe from (
C.70
), (
C.68
), (
C.76
) and (
C.77
) that

(C.84)

\displaystyle\partial^{k}_{v}\psi_{0}(x,v)=cv^{2-k}+v^{2-k}O(9x/v^{3})=\partial^{k}_{v}\psi_{0}(0,v)+v^{2-k}O(9x/v^{3})

when
k\in\{0,1,2\}

v^{3}\gg x
. Since the case
k=1
similarly follows by a few elementary computations together with (
C.68
), (
C.76
) and (
C.77
), we omit the proof. Therefore, we get whenever
v\leq v_{0}
for some small constant
v_{0}
,

(C.85)

\displaystyle\frac{|\partial_{v}^{k}\psi_{0}(x_{1},v)-\partial_{v}^{k}\psi_{0}(x_{2},v)|}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{1-\epsilon}}\leq c\sum_{j=1}^{2}\frac{|\partial_{v}^{k}\psi_{0}(x_{j},v)-\partial_{v}^{k}\psi_{0}(0,v)|}{v^{\frac{(1+k)(1-\epsilon)}{\epsilon}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{v^{\frac{(1+k)(1-\epsilon)}{\epsilon}}}\sum_{j=1}^{2}v^{2-k}O({9x_{j}}/{v^{3}})\leq c,

where we have used the fact that
x_{j}\leq v^{\frac{1+k}{\epsilon}}\ll v^{3}
since
v\leq v_{0}
. When
v\geq v_{0}
, then we use the boundary regularity result from
section 6
to deduce (
C.85
), which is applicable since
(x,v)
is away from
\gamma_{0}
when
v\geq v_{0}
.

•

Assume
|x_{1}-x_{2}|<v^{\frac{1+k}{\epsilon}}
. Then

(C.86)

\displaystyle\frac{|\partial^{k}_{v}\psi_{0}(x_{1},v)-\partial^{k}_{v}\psi_{0}(x_{2},v)|}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{1-\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\leq|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\epsilon}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\partial^{k}_{v}\partial_{x}\psi_{0}(tx_{1}+(1-t)x_{2},v)\right|\,dt

\displaystyle\leq{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\epsilon}}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\partial^{k}_{v}(v^{-1}\partial_{vv})\psi_{0}(tx_{1}+(1-t)x_{2},v)\right|\,dt

\displaystyle\leq c\frac{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\epsilon}}{v^{1+k}}\leq c,

where we have used the fact that
v\partial_{x}\psi_{0}=\partial_{vv}\psi_{0}
and (
C.63
).

Combining (
C.85
) and (
C.86
), we have

(C.87)

\displaystyle\frac{|\psi_{0}(x_{1},v)-\psi_{0}(x_{2},v)|}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{1-\epsilon}}\leq c

\displaystyle\quad\text{for any }x_{1},x_{2}\leq v^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}},

\displaystyle\frac{|\partial^{2}_{v}\psi_{0}(x_{1},v)-\partial_{v}^{2}\psi_{0}(x_{2},v)|}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{1-3\epsilon}}\leq c

\displaystyle\quad\text{for any }x_{1},x_{2}\leq v^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}},

Now we use (
C.63
)and (
C.87
), in order to derive
\psi_{0}\in C^{3-3\epsilon}(\{x\leq v^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\})
. Consider two points
z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{1}\cap\{x\leq v^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\}
and write for any
z\in\mathcal{H}_{1}\cap\{x\leq v^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\}
,
p_{z}
as the second order Taylor polynomial of
\psi_{0}
at
z
. Then we have

\displaystyle|(\psi_{0}-p_{z_{1}})(z_{2})|\leq|(p_{(t_{1},x_{1},v_{2})}-p_{z_{1}})(z_{2})|+|(\psi_{0}-p_{(t_{1},x_{1},v_{2})})(z_{2})|\eqqcolon J_{1}+J_{2},

where
p_{(t_{1},x_{1},v_{2})}
is the second order Taylor polynomial of
\psi_{0}
at
(t_{1},x_{1},v_{2})
.
By the fundamental theorem of the calculus, (
C.63
) and the fact that
v\partial_{x}\psi_{0}=\partial^{2}_{v}\psi_{0}
, we estimate
J_{1}
as

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle\leq|v_{2}\partial_{x}\psi_{0}(x_{1},v_{2})-v_{1}\partial_{x}\psi_{0}(x_{1},v_{1})||t_{2}-t_{1}|

\displaystyle+|\psi_{0}(x_{1},v_{2})-(\psi_{0}(X_{1})+\partial_{v}\psi_{0}(X_{1})(v_{2}-v_{1})+\partial^{2}_{v}\psi_{0}(X_{1})(v_{2}-v_{1})^{2}|

\displaystyle\leq|t_{2}-t_{1}||\partial^{2}_{v}\psi_{0}(x_{1},v_{2})-\partial^{2}_{v}\psi_{0}(x_{1},v_{1})|+c\|\partial_{v}^{3}\psi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\{x_{n}\leq v_{n}^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\})}(v_{2}-v_{1})^{3}

\displaystyle\leq c|t_{2}-t_{1}||v_{1}-v_{2}|,

where
X_{1}\coloneqq(x_{1},v_{1})
.
For the estimate
J_{2}
, we follow the same lines as in (
A.4
) and (
A.5
) to get that

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle\leq|p_{(t_{2},x_{1}+(t_{2}-t_{1})v_{2},v_{2})}(z_{2})-p_{(t_{1},x_{1},v_{2})}(z_{2})|+|\psi_{0}(z_{2})-p_{(t_{2},x_{1}+(t_{2}-t_{1})v_{2},v_{2})}(z_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int_{0}^{1}(\mathcal{D}\psi_{0}(t_{1}+\xi(t_{2}-t_{1}),x_{1}+\xi(t_{2}-t_{1})v_{2},v_{2})-\mathcal{D}\psi_{0}(t_{1},x_{1},v_{2}))\,d\xi(t_{2}-t_{1})\right|

\displaystyle\quad+|\psi_{0}(x_{2},v_{2})-\psi_{0}{(x_{1}+(t_{2}-t_{1})v_{2},v_{2})}|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int_{0}^{1}(v_{2}\partial_{x}\psi_{0}(x_{1}+\xi(t_{2}-t_{1})v_{2},v_{2})-v_{2}\partial_{x}\psi_{0}(x_{1},v_{2}))\,d\xi(t_{2}-t_{1})\right|

\displaystyle\quad+c|x_{2}-(x_{1}+(t_{2}-t_{1})v_{2})|^{1-\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq c(|(t_{2}-t_{1})v_{2}|^{1-3\epsilon}|t_{2}-t_{1}|+|x_{2}-(x_{1}+(t_{2}-t_{1})v_{2})|^{1-\epsilon}),

where
\mathcal{D}=\partial_{t}+v\partial_{x}
, and we have used the fact that
\psi_{0}=\psi_{0}(x,v)
,
v\partial_{x}\psi_{0}=\partial_{v}^{2}\psi_{0}
, and (
C.87
). Combining estimates
J_{1}
and
J_{2}
leads to

(C.88)

\displaystyle|(\psi_{0}-p_{z_{1}})(z_{2})|\leq c(|t_{2}-t_{1}||v_{1}-v_{2}|+|t_{2}-t_{1}|^{2-3\epsilon}+|x_{2}-(x_{1}+(t_{2}-t_{1})v_{2}|^{1-\epsilon}),

which implies that
\psi_{0}\in C^{3-3\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1}\cap\{x\leq v^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\})
when
\epsilon\leq\frac{1}{3}
. Note that when
\epsilon>\frac{1}{3}
, then
\psi_{0}\in C^{3-3\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1}\cap\{x\leq v^{3}\})
follows from the first inequality given in (
C.87
) and (
C.66
).
In addition, for very small
\delta
and
v
, we write
x_{1}=\frac{v^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}-\delta}}{2},\,x_{2}=v^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}-\delta}
. Then using (
C.84
), we have

(C.89)

\displaystyle\frac{|\psi_{0}(x_{1},v)-\psi_{0}(x_{2},v)|}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{1-\epsilon}}\eqsim\frac{v^{2}O(v^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}-\delta-3})}{v^{(\frac{1}{\epsilon}-\delta)(1-\epsilon)}}\eqsim v^{-\delta\epsilon},

which implies that
\psi_{0}\notin C^{3-3\epsilon}(\mathcal{H}_{1}\cap\{x\leq v^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}-\delta}\})
.

For
l\geq 1
, by following the same lines, we deduce

\displaystyle\frac{|\psi_{l}(x_{1},v)-\psi_{l}(x_{2},v)|}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|}\leq c(j)\quad\text{for any }(x_{1},v),(x_{2},v)\in H_{1}\cap\mathcal{R}^{-}.

∎

Appendix D
A stability result for weak solutions

In this section, we prove that the limit of a sequence of weak solutions is also a weak solution. We give a result adapted to the setting of the proof of
section 5
.

Lemma D.1
.

Let
(f_{m})
be a sequence of weak solutions to

(D.1)

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}){f}_{m}-\mathrm{div}_{v}({A}_{m}\nabla_{v}{f}_{m})&=B_{m}\cdot\nabla_{v}f_{m}+F_{m}+\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}F_{m,k}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k}&&\quad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{\frac{1}{r_{m}}}$},\\
{f}_{m}&=g_{m}&&\quad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{1}{r_{m}}}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where
A_{m}\in C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{1/r_{m}})
,
F\in C^{\lambda}(\mathcal{H}_{1/r_{m}})

F_{m,k}\in C^{\lambda,k}(\mathcal{H}_{1/r_{m}})
,
g_{m}\in C^{\alpha}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{1/r_{m}})
,
r_{m}\to 0
and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k}
is one of the elements given in (
5.8
). We assume the following:

•

For any
R\leq\frac{1}{r_{m}}
,

(D.2)

\displaystyle[F_{m}]_{C^{\lambda}(\mathcal{H}_{R})},[F_{m,k}]_{C^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathcal{H}_{R})},\|g_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{-}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{R})}\leq\frac{c(R)}{\theta(r_{m})},\quad\|A_{m}\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}+\|r_{m}^{-1}B_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}\leq\Lambda,

where
\Lambda>0
,
\theta(r_{m})\to 0
as
m\to\infty
.

•

For any
\mathcal{Q}_{1}(z_{0})\Subset\{x_{n}>0\}
,

(D.3)

\displaystyle f_{m}\to f_{\infty}\quad\text{in }C^{1,\alpha}(\mathcal{Q}_{1}(z_{0})).

•

For any
R>0
,

(D.4)

\displaystyle\|f_{m}\|_{C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}+\|\nabla_{v}f_{m}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}\leq c(R).

Then there is a weak solution
f_{\infty}=\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}f_{m}
to

\left\{\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}){f}_{\infty}-\mathrm{div}_{v}({A}_{\infty}\nabla_{v}{f}_{\infty})&=F_{\infty}+F_{\infty,k}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k}&&\quad\mbox{in $\mathcal{H}_{\frac{1}{r_{m}}}$},\\
{f}_{\infty}&=0&&\quad\mbox{in $\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where
A_{\infty}
is a constant matrix satisfying
\Lambda^{-1}I\leq A\leq\Lambda I
,
F_{\infty}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda]}
and
F_{\infty,k}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda+k]}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.1
together with (
D.2
) and (
D.4
) , we have for any
\psi\in L^{2}(\mathcal{H}_{R})
,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathcal{H}_{R}}F_{m}\psi\,dz\right|\leq\|F_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}\leq c(\theta(r_{m})^{-1}+\|f_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{R})})\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}\leq c(R)\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}.

Thus,
F_{m}\to F_{\infty}
for some
F_{\infty}\in L^{2}(\mathcal{H}_{R})
. In particular,
[F_{m}]_{C^{\lambda}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}\to 0
. Thus
\|F_{\infty}\|_{C^{\lambda}(\mathcal{H}_{R})}\leq c(R)
by the above
L^{\infty}
-bound of
F
together with the interpolation argument, which implies
F_{\infty}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\lambda]}
. Similarly, we prove

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{H}_{R}}F_{m,k}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k}\psi\to\int_{\mathcal{H}_{R}}F_{\infty,k}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k}\psi,\qquad\int_{\mathcal{H}_{R}}B_{m}\cdot\nabla_{v}f_{m}\psi\to 0.

Moreover, by (
D.3
) and the above observations,
f_{\infty}
is a weak solution to

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}){f}_{\infty}-\mathrm{div}_{v}({A}_{\infty}\nabla_{v}{f}_{\infty})=F_{\infty}+F_{\infty,k}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{k}\quad\text{in }\mathcal{H}_{R},

where
A_{\infty}
is a constant matrix satisfying the uniformly ellipticity condition. This completes the proof.
∎
